The mask is half size and reads the first sample of multisampled depth; the clouds in it are marched `cloudSamples` (12) times per texel with the clouds' own field, no haze, and from above the layer the clouds occlude nothing. Each pixel takes `samples` (48) filtered reads, so cost follows pixels (about 12 ms per frame at 256 x 192 with Bloom on a Radeon 680M, diagnostic). On a display target the light is blended after the display transform, an approximation. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M, by behaviour (angle, hole, linearity, clouds). | Use it with Bloom, tonemapping or another HDR scene capture, where the blend is exact; lower `samples` (48, 8 to 128) or `cloudSamples` (12, 2 to 64) to cut cost. |
| Rain splashes, wet lens and cover (`@margoengine/sky/weather-fx`, 2026-11-07) | `resolution` 256 a side, 0.2 m texels at the default 25 m half-width. | By method: the cover is one top-down depth map over the weather's box (`resolution` 256 a side, 0.2 m texels at the default 25 m half-width), so cover finer than a texel is lost and only the topmost surface counts (no rain under an overhang that a higher roof does not cover); occluders are drawn in their rest shapes (no skinning, morphs, instancing or displacement) and alpha-masked leaves cover whole. The map is drawn every frame, so its cost follows the occluders' triangles. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M, by behaviour (cover, map heights, splashes, lens). | Splashes are camera-facing sprites on the topmost surface, lens drops shade the frame rather than refract it, snow makes neither, and the lens wets by the map at the camera's own place. | Raise `resolution` (32 to 2048, default 256) or shrink the weather's `radius` (default 25 m) for finer cover texels. |
| Sleet (`precipitation: 'sleet'` in `@margoengine/sky/weather`, 2026-11-07) | By method: rain and snow are two draws of the weather's particles, each with its `snowShare` of the count and its own fall, sway and look, so a particle is wholly rain or wholly snow (no wet, half-melted flakes) and one share holds over the whole box (no rain below a melting height). Under `WeatherFX` both are kept off under cover; snow still makes no splashes or lens drops. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M, against each kind drawn alone. | Rain and snow are two draws of the weather's particles, each with its own share of the count. | A particle is wholly rain or wholly snow, so there are no wet, half-melted flakes and no rain below a melting height. | `snowShare` is live (`weather.options.snowShare`), so drive it from your own temperature to move between rain and snow over time. |
| Puddles and wet ground (`@margoengine/weather/puddles`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `coverage` 0 to 0.8, `size` 0.25 to 100 m, `rippleSize` 0.05 to 5 m. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M (LIM-383). | By method: water stands in the low spots of a procedural relief, not of the scene's geometry, on every core surface within about 15 degrees of flat (roofs and table tops included), with no cover from roofs and no flow; rings tilt the normal and add no height, refraction or reflection of their own. | Core meshes only: glTF-pro models and other packages' own shaders are not wetted. | Use `puddleWGSL` (the same shader maths) in your own materials, or `puddleMask`, `puddleSurface` and `puddleRelief` in JavaScript; `coverage`, `size` and `rippleSize` tune the look. |
| Snow with depth (`@margoengine/weather/snow`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `depth` 0 to 2 m, `drift` 0.25 to 100 m. By method: the lift moves each vertex up by the snow's thickness where its own normal faces up, so a mesh whose faces share no vertices (a `box()`) opens a slit as deep as the snow along its top edges, while smooth meshes (terrain, shared normals) stay closed; the lift follows vertices, so a coarse mesh drifts coarsely. Snow lies on every up-facing core surface with no cover from roofs and no wind drift; shadows and other passes draw the unlifted shape. A vertex stage another package replaced keeps its shape (colour and grain only). Core meshes only, as puddles. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M (LIM-386). | The lift moves each vertex by the snow depth where its own normal faces up, so it follows vertices. | A mesh whose faces share no vertices, such as a `box()`, opens a slit along its top edges, and a coarse mesh drifts coarsely. | Use smooth meshes with shared normals, which stay closed, or lower `depth` (0 to 2 m), since the slit is as deep as the snow; `snowWGSL` is the shader for your own materials. |
| Spatial weather regions (`@margoengine/weather/regions`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: front speed 0 to 200 m/s and width 1 to 100,000 m; cell radius 1 to 1,000,000 m. | Authoring shapes, not a forecast model (LIM-388). | By method: fronts are straight lines at a constant speed and cells are circles at a constant velocity, blended in a fixed order (base, fronts, cells), so there is no front that bends, stalls or occludes and no cell that grows from the air; a place's ground wetness and snow come from the machine it feeds, not from the map's history there. | Stack several `fronts` and `cells` (`addFront`, `addCell`; they apply in order over the base), and give a cell `life: [from, to]` to grow it in and fade it out. |
| Hail lighting and splashes (`lit` and `splashes` on `@margoengine/weather/hail`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `splashes` 0 to 8 chips a stone. | By method: a stone is shaded as a ball from its place across its streak by the engine's one sun and ambient light, Lambert diffuse and a Blinn-Phong glint: it casts no shadow and the scene's shadows do not reach it, so a stone under a roof is lit as in the open; chips burst from the ground height `groundY`, not from the surface the stone meets, and fly on closed-form paths without colliding. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M (LIM-390). | A stone casts no shadow and takes none from the scene, and chips burst from `groundY` rather than from the surface the stone meets. | Set `groundY` to the height where stones should land, and `lit: false` for the flat `color` if the sun shading looks wrong under cover; `splashes` is 0 to 8. |
| Weather drive (`@margoengine/weather/drive`, 2026-11-07) | By method: it writes numbers into targets each frame before they draw; it sets a sky's cloud sheet and fog only when the sky has them, and cloud cover is the state's `cloud` for every layer (no cloud types or heights from the weather); the layer's density (0.02 to 0.08 per metre) and its wind aloft (three times the ground wind) are design choices, not measured profiles. Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M (LIM-391). | It writes numbers into targets each frame before they draw, and the layer's density and wind aloft are design choices. | A target attached before the drive lags a frame, and cloud cover is the state's `cloud` for every layer. | Attach it before the targets for no frame of lag. |
| Weather audio cues (`@margoengine/weather/audio`, 2026-11-07) | By method: the rain and wind are one looping noise each (2 s, filtered: a low-pass for rain, a band-pass for wind), not recordings, and play everywhere at once (no rain on a roof louder than in a field, no direction for the wind); thunder is one generated rumble per strike from the strike's loudness and length, not a recorded clap. | The mix curves are design choices, set at each update, smoothed by `@margoengine/audio` (LIM-394). | Rain and wind sound the same everywhere in the scene, and thunder is a generated rumble, not a recorded clap. | Pass your own `buffers` for recorded sound. |
| Real weather from METAR reports (`@margoengine/weather/data`, 2026-11-07) | By method: one report at a time, the observation only: the trend (NOSIG, BECMG, TEMPO) and remarks are kept as text, not decoded, and TAF forecasts are not read; runway visual range, runway state, wind shear and sea groups are skipped; cloud becomes the greatest layer's cover (no layer heights or types in the state), precipitation one of three intensities, and the state's fog comes from the prevailing visibility, so mist and haze read as fog by their visibility alone. Proved on 798 real reports of 50 aerodromes (LIM-397). | One METAR report is read at a time, and only the observation in it. | Mist and haze read as fog by their visibility alone. | `metarState` returns a plain `WeatherState`: set `fog` or any other field on it before `transitionTo`, for example to clear fog on a mist report. |
| Astronomical sky (`@margoengine/sky/astro`, 2026-11-07) | No counted threshold; the planet elements are valid 1800 to 2050. | Each part uses a published method with a stated accuracy. | By method: the sun by Meeus' low-accuracy method (about 0.01 degree), the moon by his chapter 47 series (about 10 arcseconds) with its parallax, the planets by Standish's Keplerian elements (valid 1800 to 2050; tens of arcseconds, several arcminutes for Saturn), nutation by the short series (0.5 arcsecond); aberration (up to 20.5 arcseconds) is not applied, Saturn's magnitude leaves out its rings, and the planets are points, not discs. Delta T is Espenak and Meeus' polynomials (to their fits, 0.6 s apart where they meet). Stars are points of a 1 px Gaussian with no twinkle; on a float target the sky behind a star is dimmed by 1/64 of the star's alpha (an 8-bit target replaces it by the alpha), and stars are drawn over the moon's disc. The catalogue is the user's to serve (`fetchBrightStarCatalog`; the test copy is not shipped). Proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M. | Set `planets: false` for dates outside 1800 to 2050, and give your own `deltaT` (seconds) where the polynomial fit is poor. |
| Custom casters in `VirtualShadows` (`drawShadow`, 2026-11-06) | Four uniforms per `drawShadow` source, so its pages go to the GPU four per submit. | By method: a `drawShadow` source keeps four uniforms, so its pages go to the GPU four per submit, each batch with `prepareShadow` first. Measured on real elevation data against Blender Cycles with pages about one screen pixel wide, so at a sun 18 degrees up `bias` and `normalBias` both had to be 0.2 world units; 1 unit of `normalBias` lost half the overlap ([check.mjs](../packages/shadows/test/outside/check.mjs)). Slopes turned from the sun are left out of that measure: light over no light means nothing. | Scatter culls by the camera one frame late, as it does in `Shadows`. | A moving source redraws every resident page each frame, as nothing says where it reaches: mark a source `static: true` wherever its `shadowVersion` tells the truth. |
| Projected decals | Enforced ([decals.ts](../packages/post/src/decals.ts)): 4,096 decals per `setDecals`, culled each frame on the CPU by view (bounding sphere against the six clip planes) and screen size (`minPixels`, default 1 px, a conservative span bound), so only the visible ones are uploaded and drawn (LIM-442); colour only (`MaterialDecals` changes normal, roughness and metalness). Cost measured (diagnostic: other jobs running; [decals-gpu.json](../examples/package-benchmarks/results/decals-gpu.json)): 1280 × 720 4x, 1.64 ms without, 1.64 / 1.97 / 6.36 ms with 1 / 16 / 256 one-metre decals. | Screen-space decals need no change to any material shader: one pass reads the scene depth a post capture already provides. | `Decals need scene depth: attach a post capture (Tonemap, Bloom, PostStack, SSAO, TAA) first`; at 4x MSAA decal edges along silhouettes are per pixel; gltf-pro BLEND primitives are tinted with the surface behind them; `multiply` cannot lighten; decals under `minPixels` across are dropped. | `@margoengine/post/decals` (5,243 B gzip) is itself the opt-in; `mode: 'over'` for light-on-dark marks; `minPixels: 0` keeps every decal in view; `@margoengine/post/material-decals` for normal and roughness. |
| VFX repeatable particle order (accepted difference in work, Scott 2026-10-30) | Measured (RTX A6000, Edge 156, 2026-10-30, 8 alternating rounds of `examples/package-benchmarks/vfx-gpu.mjs` against the atomic build): simulate compute 0.094 to 0.184 ms at 1,000,000 particles and 0.010 to 0.031 ms at 100,000; draw unchanged. On the WebGL2 layer's CPU tier, simulate wall time 290 to 58 ms at 20,000 and 370 to 250 ms at 100,000 (the package-route row under Platform was measured before this change). | Births take the lowest dead slots and the draw list is in slot order, instead of atomic counters whose order changed from run to run (two runs of one still frame differed on up to 5,965 of 65,536 pixels). The extra work is four small compute passes (workgroup prefix sums) around the simulation; the output is the same particles in a repeatable order. | The same frames draw the same pixels on every run (`packages/vfx/test/browser-check.mjs`, order case). Size: `@margoengine/vfx/base` 7,339 to 7,978 B gzip, the root 12,376 to 12,999 B; the growth of their limits in `scripts/quality/baselines/sizes.json` was approved by Scott on 2026-10-30. | None needed below about 1,000,000 particles; fewer particles (`capacity`) cost proportionally less. |
| Scatter cull in candidate order (accepted difference in work, 2026-11-05) | Measured (8 alternating rounds of [scatter-cull-gpu.mjs](../examples/package-benchmarks/scatter-cull-gpu.mjs) against the atomic build, 1432 x 768 at 4x MSAA, [A6000 result](../examples/package-benchmarks/results/scatter-cull-gpu-a6000.json), [M6 result](../examples/package-benchmarks/results/scatter-cull-gpu-m6.json)): the distribute pass at 10,000 / 110,000 / 1,000,000 candidates takes 0.012 / 0.044 / 0.049 ms atomic and 0.046 / 0.091 / 0.222 ms ordered on the RTX A6000 (Edge 156, D3D12), 0.011 / 0.111 / 0.485 ms and 0.043 / 0.231 / 1.050 ms on an Apple M6 Mac mini (Chrome for Testing 156, Metal); the LOD draw is unchanged (ratio 0.94 to 1.03). | The atomic cull put each frame's instances in the rings in whichever order the GPU's threads ran, and an Apple M6 at 4x MSAA draws an instance's edge pixels by that order: a still biome view changed about 420 of 76,800 pixels every frame. The extra work: each candidate stores its ring flags (and, when kept, its instance) at its own index, a prefix count over the three rings (`packages/shared/prefix.js`, the WebGL2 route's) gives each its slot, and each ring is compacted (`packages/scatter/src/order.ts`). The output is the same instances in every ring (the benchmark compares them), in candidate order every frame. | Still frames draw the same bits at 4x MSAA (`packages/scatter/test/repeat.browser.mjs`: exact on the A6000 and the M6). GPU memory: about 58 B more per candidate (`ScatterTiles`: per record slot; `gpuBytes` counts it), 32 B of it the culled instance: the zero-config biome world's stored objects went from 5.9 to 12.0 MB; `ScatterTiles` holds at most `maxStorageBufferBindingSize / 32` record slots (4,194,304 at the WebGPU default of 128 MiB, 16,777,216 before). Shadow lists keep their atomic append: a depth-only pass gives the same depth in any order. | Fewer candidates (`count`) cost proportionally less; a candidate past every ring costs its flags and its share of the prefix count only. |
| Picking scattered instances (`@margoengine/scatter/pick`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: transforms are 16 floats an instance; the grid is at most 1,024 cells a side, so cells grow past 1/1,024 of the spread. By method: a uniform XZ grid of world boxes walked front to back; a box test, then the triangles when a mesh is given. | Instances are spread over the ground, so a flat grid holds about one per cell and a ray visits only the cells it crosses; the triangle test per instance is linear in the mesh's triangles. | A click picks the nearest instance; the picker holds 64 bytes of transform and about 125 bytes of box, inverse and flags an instance, plus 4 bytes a grid entry. Any `remove`, `restore` or `setTransform` rebuilds the whole grid on the next pick, in time linear in the instances. The GPU `Scatter` and `ScatterTiles` make their instances on the GPU, so they are not picked. | Pick the transforms you placed (`instanceTransforms`, `groundTransforms`, a `ScatterBatch`); pass `cell` to size the grid by hand; for a tree, pass its `mesh` so the gaps between branches miss. |
| Grass trampling (`@margoengine/scatter/interact`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: 4,096 interactors a frame; 8 to 1,024 cells a side; a bend of at most pi / 2. By method: a flat push field around the camera that moves in whole cells; every instance turns about its root. | One push vector a cell is enough to bend a meadow; a rotation about the root keeps each vertex's distance from it, so the cull radii still hold. | 8 bytes a cell twice (128 KB each at the default 128 cells) and one compute thread a cell that loops over every interactor, so many interactors cost cells times interactors. The field reads no height: an interactor in the air presses the grass under it. Grass more than half the field's size from the camera is not pushed, and a push is lost once its cell leaves the field. | Pass only the interactors on the ground; raise `size` for a wider field and `resolution` for finer footprints. |
| TAA `velocityOnly` (`@margoengine/taa`) | Enforced by check: construction refuses it without `depth`, and a 4x MSAA frame with no PostStack throws `state`. | With no capture of its own it reads the depth and scene target another plugin made. | No anti-aliasing and no `resolved` frame; `gpuState` throws, as there is no history. | Plain `TAA` for anti-aliasing with its vectors. |
| TAA reactive mask (`reactive` in `@margoengine/taa`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the caller draws the mask; particles, splats and blended glTF do not write it themselves. A reactive pixel shows that frame's own jittered sample, so it aliases until history builds again; the mask is read by nearest texel; history alpha holds the mask, so `taa.velocity.resolved` alpha is the mask, not 1; GPU cost not measured (LIM-515). | The effect packages each draw differently, and a mask is the one contract all of them can meet. | Edges of reactive effects shimmer a little more than opaque ones. | Keep mask values below 1 (0.5 to 0.8) where a soft trail is acceptable and less aliasing is wanted. |
| TAA history rejection (`rejection: 'variance'`, `locks` in `@margoengine/taa`, 2026-11-08) | By method: variance clipping uses the same nine samples as the min/max box and only narrows it, so a neighbourhood split evenly between two colours keeps as wide a box (and as much ghost) as the clamp; a lock trusts history fully for eight frames, so a transparent effect without a reactive mask that crosses a locked thin feature ghosts there, and a thin feature that really disappears lingers up to eight frames at 0.9 decay; thin detection looks only along x and y, so a diagonal one-pixel line is not locked; GPU cost not measured (LIM-523). | The resolve keeps one pass and four extra loads; locks need one r32float pair. | Fewer ghosts next to bright specks; thin lines that stay visible. | Draw transparent effects into `reactive`, which drops locks. |
| TAA sharpen (`Sharpen` in `@margoengine/taa/sharpen`, 2026-11-08) | By method: an unsharp mask on the four neighbours whose weight is at most -0.2 at `amount` 1 and falls with the pixel's headroom to black and white, clamped to the 5-tap range, so it never adds a halo or clips but cannot raise a feature above its brightest neighbour; it sharpens only the frame shown, never the history; GPU cost not measured (LIM-525). | The history must stay unsharpened, or each frame would sharpen the last one's sharpening. | Mild by design: at full strength the spread of soft 8 px stripes grows about 1%; dark and bright regions are left almost alone. | `TAAUpscale` keeps its own `sharpen` option, the same function. |
| DOF on the WebGL2 layer (lens and autofocus, `@margoengine/dof` with `@margoengine/webgl2-compat`, 2026-11-08) | Measured (M6 Mac mini, Metal, Chromium): eight lens cases within the layer's parity rule, 0.02% to 0.09% of pixels more than 2 levels from native WebGPU, mean at most 0.018, the same bokeh extent within 1 px; a one-sample capture and the scene before the blur are byte for byte native. By method: the layer reads a multisampled depth as one sample, so the WebGL2 route's CoC takes the nearest depth of each pixel and its four neighbours, which puts silhouettes back on their object but cannot rebuild the four samples, so edge pixels of a blur differ; temporal DOF is WebGPU only (LIM-541). | WebGL2 cannot read one sample of a multisampled texture. | Up to about 0.1% of a blurred frame's pixels, along silhouettes, differ from WebGPU. | WebGPU, or a one-sample capture (`exactSize`), which matches it exactly. |
| Cat's eye and anamorphic bokeh (`catEye`, `anamorphic` of `DOFLens` in `@margoengine/dof/lens`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `catEye` 0 to 1.5 bokeh radii, `anamorphic` 1 to 4. By method: the barrel circle has the bokeh's own radius and moves linearly with the pixel's place in the frame (a first-order model of mechanical vignetting, no exit-pupil geometry of a real lens); a bokeh takes the shape at the pixel it lands on, not at its source; the oval is vertical only; not with an `aperture` image; GPU cost not measured (LIM-538). | Real vignetting depends on each lens's barrel and pupil positions, which a scene does not know. | Corner bokeh lean and narrow like a real fast lens's, without matching a given lens. | Use an `aperture` image for one fixed shape. |
| Temporal DOF (`temporal` of `@margoengine/dof`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the gather runs on a 2x2-averaged scene with the nearest of each block's four depths, so a silhouette inside a blur moves by up to a full-resolution pixel, and a half-resolution near disk is joined back bilinearly; on a real GPU (M6 Mac mini, Metal) the frame stays within 0.27 sRGB levels of the full gather on average, with or without TAA, and in-focus pixels are the scene byte for byte. The smoothing TAA brings is not measured beyond that, nor is the GPU time; an aperture image's far taps do not turn; WebGPU only (LIM-537). | Half the resolution is where the saving comes from; the turn only helps when something averages frames. | Without TAA a faint shimmer in large far disks. | Leave `temporal` off for still renders or without TAA. |
| DOF focal length from the camera (`filmGauge` of `@margoengine/dof`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `filmGauge` above 0 mm; `focalDistance` beyond the focal length the camera gives, checked each frame. By method: the focal length is in metres, for a metre-based scene; with an orthographic camera it still comes from `camera.fov` (LIM-536). | A sensor size in millimetres needs a unit for the scene, and glTF's is the metre. | In a scene in other units the blur is that of a lens scaled by the unit. | Set `focalLength` yourself in the scene's units. |
| Custom bokeh shapes (`aperture` of `DOFLens` in `@margoengine/dof/lens`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the image is baked into the kernels when the effect attaches, as a 32 x 32 transmission mask over its inscribed circle (luminance times alpha, softened by about one cell at its edges) and 16, 32 and 64 far-gather taps spread over the shape; finer detail than a cell, about 1/16 of the bokeh's diameter, is lost; near and far bokeh show the image the same way up, where a real lens flips one of them; not with `blades`; GPU cost not measured (LIM-535). | The gather reads a fixed number of taps per pixel, so the shape is as fine as its taps and mask. | A heart or star bokeh from any image; a large disk shows the tap pattern a little more than the round one, as the taps cover only the open part. | Draw the shape larger (a lower f-number) for more detail per tap. |
| TAA in stereo and XR (`@margoengine/taa` with `@margoengine/xr`, 2026-11-08) | By method: each eye is an engine view with its own TAA history, jitter cycle and captures at the eye's size; no multiview (one pass into a texture array), as the engine has none; the proof uses stereo views drawn as `EyeViews` draws them, not a headset; GPU cost not measured (LIM-531). | The engine draws XR eyes as separate views, and history must not cross eyes. | Each eye pays a full TAA resolve and holds its own history (two rgba16float and two r32float textures per eye). | None: per-eye cost is the price of per-eye history. |
| TAA velocity of shapes a shader moves (NodeMaterial `positionNode`, raw `velocityEntry`, 2026-11-08) | By method: a `positionNode` move that reads the graph's uniform block runs again on last frame's block (kept per TAA view and draw) at last frame's model matrix; a move read from a storage buffer or texture that changes (skinning bones, morph weights, a height map), or from a `Fn` whose body reads a uniform, gets no motion of its own; a raw mesh moves only by the `velocityEntry` it writes, and keeps last frame's value of its own uniforms itself (`margoBefore` holds time, world matrix and view-projection); a raw mesh without the entry, or a transparent one, still gets camera motion only; GPU cost not measured (LIM-533). | A graph's storage and textures have no kept copy, and a raw shader is opaque code: only its author knows where a vertex was. | One more uniform block and a second run of the move per moved vertex in TAA's velocity pass; for raw, one more draw per opted-in mesh. | Register custom deformation as velocity primitives (`taa.add`) when it is in a storage buffer. |
| Raw mesh shared buffers (`@margoengine/shader/raw`) | Enforced by method: meshes share a buffer only when every packed byte is equal, compared on upload. | Comparing needs the bytes, so each shared buffer keeps a CPU copy of them until its meshes have left it. | One more CPU copy per distinct stream, and a first write to a shared buffer makes that mesh a new GPU buffer. | Build meshes from different data, or write to them, and they hold their own buffers. |

Uniform arrays hold 16-byte elements and struct fields are plain types, as WGSL's uniform layout rules require of a generated layout. | SSR, GTAO, SSGI and scene passes see only the depth of a raw mesh, TAA velocity too unless the material has `velocityEntry`, and a pick covers a whole mesh. | Culling trusts the sphere of `position` or `bounds`, so a vertex shader that moves points outside it needs `bounds` or `frustumCulled: false`. |

| Hashed alpha (`@margoengine/materials/alpha-hash`, 2026-11-06) | By method: coverage matches opacity within 0.02 at seven opacities on one surface, and `TAA` with `stochastic` takes a 50% quad's per-pixel spread from 0.50 to 0.16 of white after 48 frames (RTX A6000). | Shadow maps, picking, contact shadows and TAA velocity cut at `cutoff`, not by noise; instanced copies share one pattern. | `stochastic` adds a pixel of ghosting along moving edges; a blended material ignores the feature. | `cutoff` (default 0.5) sets where shadow maps, picking, contact shadows and TAA velocity cut, and `scale` sets the grain size; leave `TAA` `stochastic` off to avoid the ghosting, at the price of grain. |

| Moment transparency (`@margoengine/oit/moments`, 2026-11-06; trigonometric moments 2026-11-07) | Still an approximation: a layer dims itself by a quarter of its own optical depth. Time not measured. | Accuracy follows the depth range, by default a box measured on the first frame after `add` or `fit`: a model moved far from it loses depth order until `fit()`. Transmission and custom blending stay on the sorted path. | Call `fit()` after moving a model far from the measured depth box, or set `depthRange` by hand. |
| Blending in OIT (`blending` of `@margoengine/oit`, 2026-11-06) | By method: additive, multiply and native transmission, with maps and attenuation, each draw the sorted path's 8-bit pixel at 1x and 4x MSAA (RTX A6000). Splats stop sorting and lose all past `layers` under `ExactOIT`. `MomentOIT` falls back to the camera's near and far with an effect added; many layers close together are untested there. | Use `OIT` with `blending` for additive clouds and sprites, and keep splats on their own sorted draw (leave them out of the pass) unless glass must cut through them. |

Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) with a generated particle; time not measured; the WebGL2 route was not run. | Points are recorded into a fixed ring per slot so the draw needs no per-frame list or CPU work, and slot order keeps the same frames giving the same pixels. | Ribbons are not fogged, softened (`softness`) or drawn in an order-independent pass (`@margoengine/oit/vfx` draws the billboards only); a ribbon has a constant width and is drawn even where its particle is off screen. | Fewer `points` or a longer `interval` for long trails; keep ribbons to thousands of particles. |

By method: every mesh corner runs for every on-screen particle (36 corners for a cube); one indirect instanced draw over the billboards' list. Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) with generated cubes and boxes; time not measured; the WebGL2 route was not run. | The draw reuses the billboards' culled list and copies its count on the GPU, so it costs no read-back and no extra list. | Opaque only (particle alpha ignored), one directional light plus ambient, no fog, softness or shadows; under `@margoengine/oit/vfx` the meshes keep their own draw outside the order-independent pass; a mesh larger than its particle's `size` would be culled early. | Keep meshes to tens of triangles for many particles; use billboards for soft or see-through particles. |
| Sub-emitters (`@margoengine/vfx/events`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `count` 1 to 64 children an event, else `RangeError`. By method: per pixel on the billboards, one ball normal from the disc, the sun with wrap lighting, clustered lights at roughness 1 and no metal, sun shadows at the ball's surface point. Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) against CPU sums for a generated particle; time not measured; the WebGL2 route proves the same lit pixels through weighted OIT; ExactOIT is WebGPU-only and is required to reject compatibility devices before shader creation. | The clustered and shadow shaders' own WGSL is reused, so lit particles take the same falloff, cones and shadow filtering as lit meshes, and the lights share one bind group (group 1, or 2 under an order-independent pass) within the four groups every device offers. | Each lit pixel walks its cluster's light list, so many overlapping lights cost more than unlit particles; particles cast no shadows and do not shadow each other; ribbons and mesh particles are not lit by it; under an `ExactOIT` pass the fragment stage uses the pass's two storage buffers on top of the clustered ones. | Keep lit smoke to large, few particles; use unlit additive particles for sparks and fire. |
| Velocity stretch (`@margoengine/vfx/stretch`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `factor` 0 to 10 seconds of travel, else `RangeError`. By method: the length follows the particle's speed in 3D, the direction its motion across the screen; spin is ignored on a stretched particle; a particle moving straight at the camera stays round. Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) against a CPU-drawn stretched disc; time not measured; the full features check also proves the WebGL2 GPU route with the same CPU pixels. | Force fields (`@margoengine/vfx/fields`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: up to 16 attractors with finite position and strength and `radius` above 0; a field of whole cell counts, at most 2,097,152 cells, a `Float32Array` of 3 finite values a cell and `halfSize` above 0 on every axis, else `TypeError` or `RangeError`; the field and its cells cannot change after attach, else `TypeError`. By method: every particle sums every attractor each step; the field is trilinear between cell centres, holds the edge cells' value out to the box faces and is zero outside. Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) by reading back generated particles against a CPU stepping; time not measured; the WebGL2 route was not run. | One uniform and, with a field, one storage buffer of 16 bytes a cell (32 MiB at the limit). | A field is a fixed grid in one box; finer detail needs more cells. | Bake a field once and move its box; use turbulence for small-scale noise. |
| Skinned emitters (`@margoengine/vfx/skinned`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: 1 to 262,144 triangles, vertices of 20 floats, indices inside the vertices, a 16-number `transform`, `speed` -1,000 to 1,000, else `TypeError` or `RangeError`; not with `emitter`; the primitive is fixed at attach. By method: births pick triangles by bind-pose area and points in the pose last copied; the copy is recorded when the particles simulate, so a deformer dispatched later in the frame lags one frame; births do not inherit the surface's motion. Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) with a generated posed buffer; time not measured; the WebGL2 route was not run. | One copy of 80 bytes a vertex a frame and one storage buffer, so the simulation stays within eight. | Area by bind pose keeps the emission even as limbs stretch, but a strongly stretched triangle emits more thinly. | Attach the deformer before the particles, or accept the frame of lag; add a parent's velocity through `velocity`. |
| Flipbook frame blending (`@margoengine/vfx/flipbook`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: needs a texture atlas whose frames are `'lifetime'`, else `MargoError` `'state'`; a value that is not an object is `TypeError`. By method: the blend is the fractional part of age times the frame count, the next cell is the following frame, and the last frame holds (it does not wrap); both cells are sampled with the texture feature's half-texel inset and mixed. No new binding. Proved on one GPU (the features check) against the CPU mix, within 2 of 255; time not measured. The WebGL2 route's translator accepts the billboard (two cell samples and `mix`, and the counted-instance wrapper still applies); a pixel comparison on that layer was not run (LIM-569). | Two texture samples per lit fragment instead of one, and two flat interpolators (the next cell and the fraction). | A random-frame atlas cannot blend, because a random frame has no next frame in time. | Use `'lifetime'` frames; leave `flipbook` off for a hard step from cell to cell. |
| Flipbook pixels on the WebGL2 layer (`@margoengine/vfx/flipbook`, 2026-11-08) | Measured: the features check's two particles, held at atlas positions 0.5 and 1.5 of a 2 x 2 solid sheet, read 111,111,0 and 0,79,79 through the WebGL2 compatibility layer on an RTX A6000, within 1 of the CPU mix 112,112,0 and 0,80,80 (LIM-576). | The billboard fragment shader is the same mix, translated by the compatibility layer. | One GPU (RTX A6000, ANGLE D3D11); time not measured. | `node packages/vfx/test/flipbook-webgl2.mjs` under the GPU lock. |

By method: the first `count` live slots in slot order become point lights (range is not scaled by size, decay is 2); each live particle is a sphere impostor in the directional shadow map. Proved on one GPU (NVIDIA Ampere, the features check): four slots with count 2, range 5 and intensity 3 pack a header of 2, 5, 3 and lights at (1, 2, 3) red and (4, 5, 6) blue; a unit sphere at the origin writes depths 0.45005, 0.48770 and 1.00000 into a 64 x 64 map, within 1e-4 of the orthographic sphere; time not measured. The WebGL2 route has the same proof (LIM-594). A centre at clip depth 1.02 still writes front depth 0.97004885 within 1e-4, while a wholly out-of-range sphere leaves the clear depth. Proved on one GPU (NVIDIA Ampere, the features check): one particle, velocity (1, 0, 0), force (0, 2, 0), drag 0.5, four steps of 0.05 s, position (0.18781, 0.04877, 0.00000) and velocity (0.90369, 0.38525, 0.00000), within 1e-4 of the CPU integration; time not measured. The WebGL2 route has the same proof (LIM-595). Proved on one GPU (RTX A6000) by reading back generated particles against a CPU stepping of the same frames; time not measured; the WebGL2 route was not run. | Colliding with the depth the scene already drew needs no colliders and costs one dispatch over the listed particles a frame. | Hidden or off-screen surfaces never collide; collisions lag the scene by one frame; a depth texture drawn before the simulation (order below -8) pairs this frame's depth with last frame's camera. | Keep `DepthTexture` at its default order; use the ground plane or `obstacles` for surfaces that must hold off screen. |

A real sky from a KTX2 file (`cube-uastc-zstd.ktx2`, compressed on the GPU) and an equirectangular image stay themselves as a mirror, and float faces keep values over 1. | GGX integration happens on creation, not per frame. | The result does not follow later changes to the cube. Targets are single-sampled, so edges in them are not antialiased. `velocity()` covers camera motion unless a mesh passes its previous position, and follows one camera: in a multi-view frame it holds the last view's matrix. Multi-view frames draw each view into its rectangle of the targets, but only a full-canvas view has a check, and `screenUV` is relative to the whole target. Formats must be filterable colour formats; `ScenePass` refuses `rgba32float` and integer formats. | The pass runs before the colour pass so a surface can read it in the same frame with no one-frame lag, which is what costs the second draw. `sceneColor` needs the opaque phase finished, so it costs a full-frame copy and sees no other blended surface. | Each model draws a second time and `color` shades it twice; edges in the targets are not antialiased. | Workaround: add the models that matter, and leave out what a surface need not see. |

| SciFiHelmet with default shadows (accepted difference in work, 2026-11-05) | Measured (`helmet`, RTX A6000, Edge 156, 5 runs, `--no-gate`, other jobs running, diagnostic): 0.33 ms GPU a frame against three's WebGL 0.08 and WebGPU 0.07 (0.78 when first measured). Like for like (`helmet-filterpcf-cascades1`, `'pcf'` and one cascade, the setup closest to three's one map): 0.074 ms against 0.085 and 0.068. CPU 0.12 ms against WebGL 0.09 and WebGPU 0.19. | three's side draws one map with one fixed filter. Margo's default draws four cascades and PCSS, so a penumbra widens with the distance to its blocker. Of the 0.33 ms, about 0.12 is the frame with `'pcf'` and four cascades, 0.10 is the pass that finds each layer's nearest depth (it reads all 64 MiB of the four 2048 maps, so memory bandwidth sets its time), and 0.10 is the blocker reads and comparison taps. | Contact-hardening shadows and sharper texels near the camera by default, for about 0.25 ms on this scene. Like for like (the published fitted edge term with no clip, patched in as a measurement): 0.26 ms. CPU 0.09 ms against 0.61 and 0.83. | Every fragment runs two integrals per light with an exact arc-tangent edge term, held to 1e-5 relative, and clips a quad to the surface's horizon. The fitted term is 1.5e-4 off, and with no clip a quad crossing the horizon reads 0.069 where the true form factor is 0.006. | Exact area light shading at every roughness, for about 0.08 ms per light here. | Fewer lights: every light costs every fragment, as the lights have no range. |
| USDZ export (2026-11-05) | Measured (`p-export-usdz`, 1,000 nodes of one box, RTX A6000, Edge 156, 3 runs, `--no-gate`, other jobs running, diagnostic): 4.4 ms against three's WebGL 3.2 and 496,187 B against 444,202 B (16.0 ms and 2.46 MB before entry 69). Every mesh is in its own space under `xformOp:transform`; a geometry two or more nodes draw is written once as a class prim that each node references, and nodes that mirror it share one reflected shape, so no matrix keeps a negative determinant. Against the writer that kept single and mirrored meshes in world space, 25 real Khronos models range from 4.6% smaller (ToyCar) to 2.9% larger (NodePerformanceTest, 10,000 nodes each with a matrix of long decimals), so local space is about what the file holds, not its size; against writing every node as its own copy, ABeautifulGame is 91.4 MB in place of 165.9 MB. Load time in AR is not measured. Only `references = </Path>` within the layer is read back. | A loaded textured model keeps its images on the GPU, and a USDZ holds only what the format can carry. | Vertex colours are written for any USD reader but Quick Look draws them grey ([known bug](known-bugs.md#usdz-vertex-colours-draw-grey-in-quick-look)). | `exportUSDZ` of a loaded textured model refuses, as its images are on the GPU; its GLB bytes go in instead, and `measureUSDZ(bytes).skipped` names what a USDZ cannot hold. |
| USDZ animation (`@margoengine/exporters/usdz-anim`, 2026-11-06) | A joint with a shear in the nodes above it is refused. At most four influences for each vertex. A clip past 100,000 frames is refused. GLB input takes PNG and JPEG images and no Draco or meshopt. The mirroring of a node is read at frame 0. | By method: one clip for each file, as USD has one timeline. Scales in a `SkelAnimation` are half floats, by USD's schema: 2^-11 relative for each joint, so 5.9e-3 of the model's size at worst for a chain of 12. | Tracks are sampled at `fps`, default 30: a STEP key turns over one frame, and a clip that ends between two frames holds its last pose on the next one, so it runs up to one frame longer. Measured worst 3.1e-4 of the model's size, inside the 5.9e-3 that half-float scales allow, and 4.6e-7 on clips that keep scale 1. Morph weights are not written and are named in `skipped`, with `KHR_animation_pointer` channels. Quick Look shows a file at real size and the default is the model's true size, which glTF says is metres: `height` measures the rest pose, and a clip can reach further, which `bounds` in the result says. The first real iPhone test (Fox-Run, 2026-11-06) failed on size. After the fix Fox-Run and the Robot files looked right on one iPhone, 8 of the 76 files were tried, and the rigged figure still floats off the ground ([known bug](known-bugs.md#animated-usdz-the-fix-for-the-first-iphone-failure-awaits-a-retest)); whether the clipping and the failed move had another cause is not known. No anchoring metadata is written: Quick Look places a file without it, and what it does to moving a model is unmeasured. | Fox.glb is 79 m tall and robot-expressive 4.46 m, so `height` is what makes such a model fit a room, and the result's `size` and `bounds` say so first. |
| Blender characters (`@margoengine/blender/anim`) | Enforced ([animation.py](../tools/blender-addon/animation.py), [anim.ts](../packages/blender/src/anim.ts)): up to eight bone influences per vertex, relative shape keys only, one clip per action sampled at every frame (LINEAR), a skinned mesh carries only its Armature modifier (vertex groups only: no envelopes, Preserve Volume or mask group), every vertex weighted to a deform bone, no instanced animated meshes. Matches Blender 5.2.3 within 1.2e-6 m (LIM-389). | Sampling every frame bakes constraints, IK and curve shapes into plain keyframes any glTF player reads. | Object-level animation is not exported, and other modifiers must be applied first; every joint has a channel in every clip, so files grow with bones times frames. | Bake object motion onto a bone; apply modifiers before export. |

Matches the mechanics within 1 cm at rest (LIM-396). | Implicit shapes are what KHR_implicit_shapes carries and what every solver handles fast. | No mesh or convex-hull colliders from tags, no joints, no Blender rigid body or light objects (lights and cameras come with the scene option). | Model a simple collider mesh and tag it, or add bodies in code with `@margoengine/physics`. |

Other surface shaders bake as a dielectric from Cycles' diffuse colour and roughness passes. Matches the node maths within the sampling and rounding bound at 32 x 32 (LIM-401). | A bake is the material seen along each surface normal: Cycles' own bake passes, resampled to the mesh's UV layout. | Effects that depend on the view or the light (Fresnel, Layer Weight, Light Path) are frozen at their value along the normal; detail finer than a texel is lost; transmission, coat, sheen and subsurface fail by name; a metal made of a non-Principled shader exports as a dielectric. | Raise `bake_size`, or use a Principled BSDF for metals and bake view-dependent parts yourself. |

By method: subsurface is diffuse transmission into a volume as thick as the longest mean free path (Subsurface Radius times Scale), with the base colour as the multiple-scattering albedo; transmission has no thickness, so it is thin-walled. By method: versions that arrive during a load are coalesced to the newest (LIM-410). | A whole export per edit keeps one file format and one loader. | A large scene re-exports and re-uploads whole on every edit, so the page lags Blender by the export and upload time; baking runs again on each edit (256 px while live). By method: 683 lm/W converts a light's power (over 4 pi sr, in candela) and a sun's strength (in lux); Spot Blend becomes the inner cone as outer times (1 - blend). | Blender curves, points and volumes (add-on and `@margoengine/blender/curves`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced ([curves.py](../tools/blender-addon/curves.py), [curves.ts](../packages/blender/src/curves.ts)): off by default; a file with shapes (header flag 2) loads only through the curves entry, and a scene with both animation and shapes fails to export by name; custom NURBS knots fail by object name. | Blender add-on checks (2026-11-06) | By method: run by hand in Blender 5.3 Beta through `npm run modern:test:blender` with `MARGO_BLENDER` set (LIM-287), and in Blender 5.2.3 LTS on the Beelink (2026-11-07, LIM-389). | OBJ, STL and PLY of a loaded model (2026-11-06) | By method: the shape at the nodes' current pose, four skin influences. DAE and USDZ of a loaded model are not covered; they still refuse. `Kd` is written sRGB and `map_Kd` is meant to multiply it. | OBJ has no other maps, and `exportOBJ` is synchronous, so it can write bytes it holds but not encode pixels. MTL names no colour space. | Blender reads `Kd` as linear, so a flat colour shows lighter there, and drops `Kd` once `map_Kd` is linked, so a tinted texture shows untinted: SheenChair's orange velvet is white. Three.js-style readers multiply. | `exportModel` keeps everything. `upAxis: 'z'` keeps STL and PLY coordinates as they are; `parseSTL` and `parsePLY` read a file as written, so a default export reads back on its back. |

With supports off 22 of 26 do: InterpolationTest, LightsPunctualLamp, MetalRoughSpheres and PointLightIntensityTest stop at "no extrusions in the first layer", as each has a flat sheet or a thin base below parts that float clear of it, so nothing is on the bed at the first layer's height. | By method: core 3MF with the materials extension's colour groups, in one model part. A scene of several nodes is one object, every part baked in place, because PrusaSlicer reads each build item and each component as an object of its own and crashes on overlapping ones; the cost is a part drawn many times stored many times (ABeautifulGame 108.6 MB, 39.0 MB with `assembly: false`), and `assembly: false` keeps one object per geometry for separate prints. The real print-ready 3DBenchy through `export3MF` reads manifold and slices at its true size to the same 160 layers as its STL, filament 4,446.13 mm against 4,445.94 mm. Attention holds its [..., t, s] scores. Speed is not measured. | Convolution is a gather and a matrix product, and backward is the gather through the inverse table, so it uses no atomics and runs on every backend, the CPU included, at the price of memory. | A large image or a long sequence runs out of GPU memory before it runs out of time (`failed` on the next wait). `Embedding` gradients add atomically, so their rounding may vary on a GPU. Speed is not measured. | Each round runs over every edge at once with an atomic minimum, so a graph needs no frontier queue, the code is the same on the GPU and the CPU backend, and negative weights work. | A round visits every edge whether or not its source changed, so a long thin graph costs edges x its depth: a frontier-based search would do less work there. Not included: strongly connected components, minimum spanning trees, centrality. | Use whole or dyadic weights for exact sums; raise `checkEvery` on deep graphs to read back less often. |

Results last for the context, or across sessions with `store`. | Measuring beats a fixed rule because the best size depends on the GPU and the kernel; timed runs take turns so drift is shared, and the median drops spikes. | The first call pays for those runs and for compiling every candidate. A result holds for the input size measured: name it in `signature`. Browsers round GPU timestamps (steps of 0.066 ms in the Chrome tested), so sizes of a short kernel can tie. Browsers without `device.adapterInfo` (and browsers that hide adapter details) let different GPUs of a store share an entry, which costs speed, never correctness. Driver updates are not seen: use `refresh`. `tuneCompute` covers the context's `reduce`, `scan` and `sort` only: `sum(axis)`, segmented scans and the element-wise kernels keep their fixed sizes, and the CPU backend, `useSubgroups` and the layer's sort ignore the table. The gain depends on the GPU: 8 to 13 percent on a sort on an Apple M6, none on the RTX A6000, where the default 256 is already the best. | Tune once at start-up, or ship a `store`. |
