| Trails (`Trail` in `@margoengine/lines/trail`) | Enforced: 2 to 65,536 points (`maxPoints`, default 256); past that the oldest point goes and the far end jumps. By method: a trail is a line, so it faces the camera unless `normal` lays it flat (2026-11-07), and a flat trail is one strip with a per-point `normal` it does not have: it lies in a plane facing that one direction, so it does not follow a hill, and its segments are not joined at a corner; a flat trail takes no dashes; a dashed trail ignores `tailWidth`, because dash distances use the value widths are stored in. | One line keeps joins, antialiasing, fog, clipping and picking for free. | A tyre track seen from low down looks like a tube, not a flat strip. | Raise `maxPoints`; build a flat strip as a mesh with `@margoengine/splines/mesh`. |
| Massive line batches (`LineBatch` in `@margoengine/lines/batch`) | Enforced: 67,108,864 segments a batch; one draw per 8,388,608 segments at the default 256 MiB `maxBufferSize`. By method: independent segments with square ends: no joins, round caps, dashes, fog, clipping planes, depth writing or picking; colours at 8 bits a channel; a CPU copy of 32 bytes a segment stays for device loss. | Six vertices and 32 bytes a segment, where `Line` draws 78 vertices and reads a storage buffer whose binding size limits it. | Translucent corners are darker where segments overlap; memory is 64 bytes a segment across CPU and GPU. | Use `Line` where those features matter. |
| Hidden lines (`HiddenLine` in `@margoengine/lines/hidden`, `behind` and `depthBias` on `LineMaterial`) | By method: the test is against scene depth, so surfaces that write no depth hide nothing; `depthBias` (default 0.001 of the distance) decides what counts as on a surface, so an edge closer behind a surface than that shows as seen. Dashes run in world units. Hidden lines do not pick. | A depth test is one draw with no mesh analysis, on any scene; exact CAD hidden-line removal would need the geometry on the CPU. | A very thin wall can show an edge behind it as seen. | Lower `depthBias`, or raise it if a far model flickers. |
| Alpha to coverage on lines (`alphaToCoverage` on `LineMaterial`) | By method: works with MSAA only and falls back to blending without it; opacity comes in steps of one sample, four at 4x MSAA; lines of equal opacity use the same samples, so the later replaces the earlier at a crossing. | MSAA hardware turns alpha into a sample mask, which is what makes it independent of draw order. | Fine opacity changes band at low sample counts. | Leave it off and blend, or raise the sample count. |
| Filled helpers (`solidHead` on `ArrowHelper`, `fillOpacity` on `PlaneHelper`) | By method: a fill is flat colour with no lighting and does not pick. An opaque fill writes depth; translucent fills blend in the order added. | Helpers are guides, so they stay readable in any light and need no material or mesh. | A solid head has no shading, so its shape reads from its outline. | Use a mesh from `@margoengine/core` where lighting matters. |
| Video output (`@margoengine/headless/video`, `/ffmpeg`, 2026-11-05) | `encodeVideo` builds the whole file in memory and needs WebCodecs: browsers and workers have it, Node does not. WebM takes VP8, VP9 and AV1; MP4 takes H.264, VP9 and AV1. WebM times are whole milliseconds, as its default timestamp scale gives. Alpha is dropped. Frames are 8-bit RGBA, so no HDR video. Sound is Opus in WebM and AAC or else Opus in MP4, one or two channels, and comes as plain samples held in memory. | A file built whole has every size known up front, so nothing is patched afterwards and the MP4 index can sit first for playback before the download ends. WebCodecs is the only encoder a page has; shipping one would be a large download. | A very long capture must fit in memory, as compressed bytes only. In Node, `encodeWithFFmpeg` streams to a file instead, and needs ffmpeg installed. The H.264 and VP9 arguments and the rawvideo input ran on real ffmpeg 9.0.2 (`npm run test:real-ffmpeg --workspace @margoengine/headless`): 12 frames each, BT.709 tags read back, colours within 2 of 255 levels. That run needs ffmpeg, so the unit tests keep a stand-in program for failures and aborts. | Render in a browser or worker for `encodeVideo`, or use `/ffmpeg` in Node. Pass `args` to choose other ffmpeg settings. |
| Subsurface scattering blur (`SSS` in `@margoengine/post/sss`, 2026-11-07) | By method: screen space, so only what is on screen scatters; one Gaussian per channel stands in for a measured profile's sum of Gaussians (no long red tail); the kernel stops at 64 px each side, so a surface filling the screen close to the camera scatters less than `radius` asks; taps across a depth step larger than `radius`, off the mask or on the background count as the centre colour. The mask is the user's: a material's `subsurface` value does not mark pixels by itself. One `rgba16float` texture at the frame size; GPU cost not measured (LIM-434). | A separable blur costs two passes of `2 x taps + 1` reads, where a 2-D kernel would cost its square. | Soft skin, wax and marble on marked pixels; red bleeds farthest into shadow edges with the default profile. | Raise `taps` for wide kernels; mark pixels with a `SceneTargets` output or a selection mask. |
| Material decals (`MaterialDecals` in `@margoengine/post/material-decals`, 2026-11-07) | By method: a screen-space relight in a PostStack, not a G-buffer write before lighting. The surface comes from a `SceneTargets` capture (one more scene pass); the change is core's lit model (sun plus ambient) for the new surface minus the old, so gltf-pro and materials models get the sun and ambient part only, not their image-based light; the sun's share is read back per pixel from the lit image, so a pixel the unshadowed sun gives nothing (facing away) is treated as lit; transparent effects drawn over the surface are relit with it; the atlas is read at mip 0; 64 decals, no culling; GPU cost not measured (LIM-441). | Engines that write decals into a deferred G-buffer change the surface before any light; Margo's forward renderer has no such buffer, so the relight works from a capture. | Normal, roughness, metalness and base-colour decals on any surface core or the capture draws, with shadow kept. | Keep decals on core meshes for an exact relight; give the atlas texels as wide as the decal's screen size. |
| Rough reflections (`SSR` with `stochastic: true` in `@margoengine/post/ssr`, 2026-11-08) | By method: one GGX-sampled ray per pixel per frame on surfaces rougher than half of `maxRoughness`, averaged over frames by a history reprojected with the surface's motion and clamped to this frame's 3x3 neighbourhood (mean plus or minus one standard deviation), blend floor 1/16; only what is on screen reflects, as for the mirror path. One history per SSR (a second view in the same frame reprojects against it); a moving camera smears rough reflections for a few frames; some noise stays on a still view (mean frame-to-frame change 0.15/255 against 1.28/255 at the second frame); three more `rgba16float` targets; GPU cost not measured (LIM-446). | One ray per pixel keeps the cost of the mirror path; the frames do the averaging. | Rough floors and brushed metal reflect what is on screen instead of keeping only the environment. | Leave `stochastic` off for deterministic frames (screenshots, tests); pair with TAA for a moving camera. |
| Multi-bounce screen GI (`SSGI` with `multiBounce: true` in `@margoengine/post/ssgi`, 2026-11-08) | By method: each frame adds one bounce by reading the light at ray hits from the previous frame's result, reprojected through the previous camera; only what is on screen bounces; the extra bounces trail a moving camera or light by a few frames; a hit hidden in the previous frame takes the light of what covered it for a frame; one history per SSGI (a second view in the same frame shares it); two more `rgba16float` targets and a display pass when SSGI ends the stack; GPU cost not measured (LIM-455). | Bounces accumulate over frames for the cost of one bounce per frame. | Indoor scenes fill with bounced light on a still or slow camera. | Keep it off for a fast-cutting camera, or accept a few frames of build-up after each cut. |
| Anamorphic streaks (`Anamorphic` in `@margoengine/post/anamorphic`, 2026-11-07) | By method: streaks are horizontal only (no rotation); they are built at half width and a quarter of the height, so a streak is about 4 px tall and a light smaller than a pixel still draws one; each streak is the light's own colour times `tint`, with no spectral split; reach is 2 (3^passes - 1) half-width texels each side; two quarter-size and one full-size `rgba16float` targets; GPU cost not measured (LIM-459). | A chain of sparse 9-tap passes reaches far for a few reads per pixel, where one wide kernel would read hundreds. | Bright points and highlights draw thin tinted lines across the frame. | Lower `passes` for short streaks, raise `threshold` so only lights streak. |
| Blur (`Blur` in `@margoengine/post/blur`, 2026-11-08) | By method: 2 radius + 1 texture reads per pixel per pass, so cost grows with the radius (64 at most, 129 reads a pass); the kernel is cut at 3 sigma for the Gaussian; edges clamp to the nearest pixel, so a bright border bleeds inward; `region` is a rectangle with a hard edge; one or two full-size `rgba16float` targets; GPU cost not measured (LIM-472). | A plain separable loop is exact for every radius and needs no mip chain. | Menus behind glass, focus pulls and custom chains blur by any whole-pixel radius. | Keep the radius small on weak GPUs, or chain two Blurs of half the radius for a wider, cheaper look. |
| Lens model (`Lens` in `@margoengine/post/lens`, 2026-11-08) | By method: one radial distortion term (k r^2), no tangential or chromatic part; distortion resamples the frame bilinearly, so it softens it slightly; the dirt is lit by its own glow at an eighth of the frame size (reach about 50 px), not by the glow of `Bloom`; the built-in dirt is a fixed pattern; two eighth-size and one full-size `rgba16float` targets; GPU cost not measured (LIM-474). The Panini projection is a camera (`WideAngle` in `@margoengine/cameras/panini`), not part of this effect. | One term covers the usual barrel and pincushion look; a small glow keeps the dirt cheap. | Wide lenses bulge, and smudges on the glass light up around bright lights. | Pass your own `dirt` texture; set `dirtIntensity` 0 for distortion alone. |
| Billboards (`BillboardLayer` in `@margoengine/sprites/billboard`, 2026-11-08) | `capacity` 256 billboards before a buffer grows; at most the device's `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` / 64 per view. | One instanced draw per view, each view with its own sorted buffer, so its memory is 64 B per billboard per view. | By method: blended billboards sort by the pivot's distance, so two that cross draw one over the other whole; pixel-sized ones take their depth from the pivot; `lockY` applies to world sizes only; texels are read with no filtering. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, by measured pixel extents. Time not measured. | `transparent: false` (cut-out with depth writes) needs no sort; split crossing quads into two billboards. |
| Smooth sprites (`SpriteLayer` `filter: 'linear'` in `@margoengine/sprites`, 2026-11-08) | One copy of the atlas per layer, `rgba8unorm-srgb` with a full mip chain: 4/3 of the atlas size again. | Filtering straight alpha bleeds a transparent texel's colour into edges, and filtering sRGB bytes darkens averages, so the layer keeps a premultiplied, linear-light copy. | By method: mip levels below the first average across neighbouring cells; the copy is made once, so a changed `GPUTexture` atlas needs `setAtlas` again; normals stay exact-pixel; premultiplied 8-bit storage loses colour precision at low alpha. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, against the bilinear formula. Time not measured. | A gutter around cells drawn very small, or `mipmaps: false`. |
| Live grading (`Grade` in `@margoengine/tonemap/grade`, 2026-11-08) | By method: display-referred, on 0..1 colour after the tone curve; under an HDR `ColorOutput` what lies above white passes ungraded; curves are sampled 33 times and read linearly; the wheels are additive offsets with fixed luminance masks (no pivot or range controls); one uniform of 608 B; GPU cost not measured (LIM-477). | Grading after the curve keeps the controls in the 0..1 range artists know from grading tools. | Wheels, curves and hue against saturation move live with no LUT to bake. | Bake a LUT with `setLUT` for a look outside these controls. |
| Sprite shaders (`SpriteLayer` `shader` with `@margoengine/sprites/shader`, 2026-11-08) | One shader per layer; four `params` per sprite; outlines reach 1 to 4 texels; palettes 1 to 16 pairs. | A layer is one pipeline and one instanced draw, so the code is per layer and the per-sprite part is data. A shaded layer packs 64 B per sprite instead of 48. | By method: effects see one texel at a time plus `spriteTexel` neighbours inside the sprite's rectangle, so an outline needs transparent texels around the art; a palette swap matches exact bytes, so it is for `filter: 'nearest'`; WGSL that does not compile shows as a validation error on the first frame, not a throw. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, against values worked out in the test. Time not measured. | Several layers for several effects; your own `spriteShader` for anything else. |
| Classic curves (`curve` `reinhard`, `cineon`, `linear` in `@margoengine/tonemap`, 2026-11-08) | By method: Reinhard is per channel (it shifts hue in saturated highlights) and never reaches white; Cineon is the filmic fit with its 2.2 gamma undone, not a film-stock model; linear clips at white; under an HDR `ColorOutput` they are scaled by the same rolloff as AgX and ACES; not compared side by side with another engine (LIM-479). | They exist to keep the look of scenes ported from engines that use them. | A ported scene keeps its contrast and highlight roll-off. | Use `neutral` or `agx` for new work. |
| Several atlases per layer (`SpriteLayer` with a list of atlases in `@margoengine/sprites`, 2026-11-08) | Up to the device's `maxTextureArrayLayers` (at least 256) atlases, all the same size. | A texture array holds same-size layers, which is what lets one draw read them all. | By method: the layer copies the atlases into its own array (one more copy of each in GPU memory), so a changed `GPUTexture` needs `setAtlas` again; a page sprite packs 64 B (80 B with a shader); `normals` need a single atlas. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, byte for byte. Time not measured. | Pad smaller sheets to one size; a separate layer for normal-mapped sprites. |
| Custom tone curve (`CustomCurve` in `@margoengine/tonemap/custom`, 2026-11-08) | By method: a JavaScript curve is per channel, sampled at 128 points over 2^-12 to 2^8 (log2) and held above; a WGSL curve is compiled into the presentation shader, so a WGSL error shows as a GPU validation error; shader-node graphs are not taken directly; one uniform of 592 B; GPU cost not measured (LIM-481). | Tonemap does not depend on `@margoengine/shader`; sampling keeps a JavaScript curve live without a recompile. | Write a node curve as WGSL. | Use `wgsl` for exact or per-pixel curves. |
| Metering modes (`autoExposure` `metering`, `method` in `@margoengine/tonemap`, 2026-11-08) | By method: 4,096 samples on a 64 by 64 grid (a spot narrower than a few pixels resamples the same pixels); the histogram has 64 bins over -12 to 12 stops, so its level moves in 0.375-stop bin centres weighted by the kept share; centre weighting has a fixed sigma of a quarter of the view height; the mask is read by nearest texel; GPU cost not measured (LIM-482). | One workgroup keeps metering to one dispatch with no readback. | A finer histogram or more samples would cost more threads or passes. | Use `mean` for smooth adaptation, `histogram` for scenes with small lights. |
| 2D skeletal animation (`@margoengine/sprites/skeletal`, 2026-11-08) | Four bones per vertex; skinning on the CPU every frame for every visible mesh, about 20 B per vertex uploaded each frame. | Linear blend skinning is a weighted sum per vertex, cheap enough on the CPU for character meshes, and it keeps skinning testable in Node. | By method: linear blend skinning collapses a joint bent far (each vertex lies between its bones' rigid copies, so a sharp bend loses area), so give bends a shared, half-weighted vertex row; texels read exactly with no filtering, single-sample target; clips are linear keys only (no curves, events or blending between clips). Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, byte for byte. Time not measured. | More grid rows at joints; your own pose code that sets `Bone` fields before `skeleton.update()`. |
| Local exposure (`LocalExposure` in `@margoengine/tonemap/local`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the bilateral blur is separable (horizontal then vertical), an approximation of the 2D filter that can leave a faint halo along thin bright lines; quarter-resolution base (4 by 4 blocks); the whole source is filtered, not one view rect; three r32float textures (about 1.5 MB at 1920 by 1080); GPU time not measured (LIM-486). | Separable passes keep the cost linear in the radius. | Very fine structures are not separated into their own regions. | Lower `edges` for sharper region borders. |
| Sprite masks (`SpriteLayer` `mask` in `@margoengine/sprites`, 2026-11-08) | One rect or circle per layer, hard edged. | The test runs per pixel in the layer's fragment shader against one uniform, so a mask costs no extra pass or texture. | By method: a pixel is in or out by its centre, so edges step by whole pixels and do not antialias; a turned or scaled shape needs a rect or circle (no polygons or image masks). Every layer's uniform grew from 16 to 48 B for it. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, pixel for pixel. Time not measured. | Draw into a `target` and composite it with your own mask pass for soft or image masks. |
| Isometric, staggered and hexagonal maps (`@margoengine/tilemap/iso`, `/hex`, 2026-11-08) | The cells in view are written each frame, 48 B each; one draw per run of one tileset in paint order. Oblique maps, and infinite maps that are not orthogonal, are refused by the Tiled importer; `fromTMX` (TMX through `@margoengine/loaders`) reads orthogonal maps only, as the loader does not carry the orientation. | Drawing in paint order across the whole view is what lets tall tiles cover the cells behind them; a cached 64 x 64 chunk per draw, as `TileMap` uses, would draw a tall tile on a chunk's edge under its neighbour's cells. | By method: the CPU cost grows with the cells in view (not measured); a turned hexagonal tile's edge pixels fall where rounding puts them; a tile wider than its cell may overlap a cell drawn later in the same row. Proved on one GPU, an RTX A6000, pixel for pixel against images painted from the layout rules. Time not measured. | Orthogonal maps draw with `TileMap` and its chunk cache; split a huge view into several targets. |
| Grid pathfinding (`@margoengine/tilemap/path`, 2026-11-08) | Up to `maxCells` (default 1,000,000) cells searched per call; arrays of the grid's size (about 21 B per cell) made per call. | A tile map is a grid, so a plain heap search over its cells needs no navigation mesh. | By method: one search per call on the calling thread (no incremental or hierarchical search, no jump point pruning); costs are per cell entered, not per edge; a staggered map's left and right cells in one row are two steps apart. Time not measured. | For big open worlds use `@margoengine/navmesh`; for many units to one goal reuse one `flowField`. |
| Field of view and fog of war (`@margoengine/tilemap/fov`, 2026-11-08) | One scan of up to `max(width, height)` rows per octant per viewer on the calling thread; a mask of `width * height` bytes per call (or the `into` mask), `FogOfWar` keeps two. | Shadowcasting visits only the cells that light can still reach, so a closed room costs its own cells. | By method: sight is from the viewer's cell centre and not symmetric; orthogonal and isometric grids only (staggered and hexagonal refused); no partial light or light levels; the fog image is one pixel per cell, drawn by the caller. Time not measured. | For light levels use `@margoengine/voxel/light-rgb` or a lighting pass; for hex fog test cells with `@margoengine/tilemap/hex` lines. |
| Pixel-art scaling (`@margoengine/pixel/scale`, 2026-11-08) | One pass a canvas view (or one into `target`), 4 texel reads a pixel for `'sharp-bilinear'` and up to 20 for `'edge'`; the picture covers whole screen pixels. | Covering each screen pixel by its share of each texel keeps art pixels flat and even at any size. | By method: two texels a side are mixed, so below 1 times the picture is not averaged over all it covers; `'edge'` below 2 times draws as `'sharp-bilinear'`; colours mix as stored (sRGB values), not in linear light; texels past the edge repeat the edge. Time not measured. | Draw the art at the size you need below 1 times. |
| CRT look (`@margoengine/pixel/crt`, 2026-11-08) | One pass a canvas view (or one into `target`), 9 texel reads a pixel. | Working from the art's own rows keeps one scanline per art pixel at any screen size. | By method: a stylised look, not a model of a real tube (no beam spread with brightness, no persistence, no interlace, no colour bleed); one grille pattern (three columns); the art row under each pixel is taken whole, not mixed with the next; colours as stored, not in linear light. Time not measured. | For a measured tube model use a dedicated post pass. |
| Model outlines (`@margoengine/toon/models`, 2026-11-08) | One render pass a view for every outlined primitive, one hull draw a primitive and its instances (two pipelines: plain and mirrored nodes). | The hull reuses the vertices the model already has on the GPU, deformed ones included, so it costs one more vertex pass of the outlined triangles. | By method: an inverted hull, so the line follows vertex normals: hard-edged meshes with split normals show gaps at their creases; a constant world-unit width, thinner with distance; no line on point and line primitives; GPU-culled draws still draw their hull; one width and colour for every model. Time not measured. | `@margoengine/post` `Outline` for a screen-space line of fixed pixel width. |
| Palette cycling and ramps (`@margoengine/pixel/palette`, 2026-11-08) | `cyclePalette` and `mixPalettes` run on the CPU per call; `setSwap` writes one 256 x 1 row at most each frame. | Rotating the drawn colours animates water and fire without repainting a pixel, and the matching stays. | By method: cycles rotate the colours drawn, not the matching (a pixel keeps its index); one rate per run, no ping-pong or easing; mixing is linear in Oklab between two palettes. Time not measured. | For more than two palettes over a day, mix the neighbouring pair. |
| Hatching and halftone (`@margoengine/toon/hatch`, 2026-11-08) | Core meshes only, one shader patch: up to 4 stroke layers or one dot a pixel, in the mesh's own fragment pass. | A screen-space pattern needs no textures or UVs and keeps the same stroke width at any distance. | By method: the pattern is fixed to the screen, so it slides over a moving surface (no texture-space strokes); tone is Lambert n.l of the sun only (clustered lights and shadows are not inked); hatching steps by whole layers; the paper takes the full lit colour without the 1/pi of Lambert, as `ToonShading` does. Time not measured. | `@margoengine/shader` `halftone` for a screen effect over everything; a `NodeMaterial` lighting function for glTF-pro and materials meshes. |
| Text atlas eviction (`@margoengine/text`, 2026-11-08) | `TextRenderer.evictUnused()` and `GlyphAtlas.evict()` free texture array pages whose glyphs are no longer in any active visible `Text` block (LIM-572). When `maxPages` is full, `place()` evicts an unused page before failing. | For chat and user-generated text: keeps memory capped without leaking full atlas pages when old messages are removed or modified. | By method: only pages without active visible glyphs are evicted; if all pages contain active visible glyphs, `place()` throws `RangeError`. Time not measured. | Call `evictUnused()` after disposing old messages, or raise `maxPages` / `atlasSize`. |
| Per-glyph animation (`@margoengine/text/animate`, 2026-11-08) | `AnimatedText` drives position, scale, rotation, color and opacity per glyph quad over time (LIM-577). | Typewriter reveals and wave/bounce/color procedural motion while keeping global typography and line wrapping. | By method: each non-whitespace glyph is an individual `Text` block sharing the parent font and renderer, with individual model matrices and uniform colors; whitespace glyphs advance layout without GPU quads. Time not measured. | Use plain `Text` for static blocks with one draw call. |
| Extruded 3D text (`@margoengine/text/extrude`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: nonzero fill, complete cap triangulation or a coded error; steps/curveSegments integers 1..256, bevelSegments 0..64; TrueType depth 32, 4,096 components and 200,000 raw points per glyph; Type 2 depth 10, 48 operands, 96 stems and 100,000 executed tokens; 16,384 flattened contour vertices per glyph and a conservative 1,000,000-vertex mesh budget per block. LIM-587 proves every Source Sans 3 Regular glyph in TTF/CFF1 watertight, with independent outline reference checks for ten glyphs per font. | Solid text through core Geometry on WebGPU and native WebGL2, with output pixels asserted. BevelOffset moves cap outlines; bevelSize expands the body. | Unicode cmap 4/12, unhinted glyf or name-keyed CFF1 only; no shaping, CID CFF, CFF2, variable/SVG/bitmap-only outlines. Relative boundary weld is 1e-12 of glyph extent; output positions are Float32. Bevel lengths are world units: size/thickness positive together, thickness at most half depth. Collapsed, reversed or intersecting offset rings throw code failed. Depth 0 is planar caps. CPU work is not timed. | Reduce bevels for thin strokes; cache generated meshes or use the SDF entry for screen text. |


| Limit | Starts at | Why | What you see | Escape hatch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| glTF download budget | Enforced: gltf-pro reads at most `maxDownloadBytes` (default 64 MiB, 67,108,864 B: `assetLimit`) of JSON, buffers and images per load, and decoded accessors, packed geometry and decoded images may each use 4x that (256 MiB by default); `@margoengine/gltf` reads at most 64 MiB ([asset.ts](../packages/gltf-pro/src/asset.ts), [loading.js](../packages/shared/loading.js); [accessors.test.js](../packages/gltf-pro/tests/accessors.test.js)). Khronos ABeautifulGame (a 43 MB GLB whose 33 2048x2048 textures decode to 528 MiB) is over the default image budget. | Bounds memory for any URL an app is handed. | gltf-pro names the option for the download and packed-geometry budgets, e.g. `RangeError: glTF exceeds download budget (67108864 bytes; raise the maxDownloadBytes option)`; decoded accessors and images over 4x throw `RangeError: Decoded glTF exceeds byte budget` or `RangeError: glTF images exceed decoded size limit`; the light loader throws `glTF download byte budget exceeded` or `glTF buffers exceed 64 MiB`. | gltf-pro's `maxDownloadBytes` option (ABeautifulGame loads with 160 MiB, [khronos-gallery.js](../examples/khronos-gallery.js)); for the light loader, use gltf-pro. |
| Malformed and hostile files | Enforced at each parser's trust boundary (glTF, gltf-pro with its parse worker and progressive stream, `.margo`, Draco, KTX2, meshopt, HDR, EXR, UltraHDR, FBX), checked by seeded fuzzers (`npm run fuzz`, [targets-formats.js](../scripts/fuzz/targets-formats.js); regressions in [fuzz-formats-regressions.test.js](../tests/fuzz-formats-regressions.test.js)): every input ends in a result or the package's own error. Limits added with them: a gltf-pro document nested deeper than 256 levels ([asset.ts](../packages/gltf-pro/src/asset.ts)); an EXR chunk that decodes to more than 256 MiB, or chunks too small to hold the image ([exr.ts](../packages/environment/src/exr.ts)); a Draco or KTX2 decode running longer than 10 s plus 4 s per MiB of input ([pool.ts](../packages/decoders/src/pool.ts)). How long large valid Draco and KTX2 files take to decode: not measured. | Files from a URL are untrusted: a malformed one must fail with an error the app can show, not crash, hang, read outside its bytes or claim memory its size cannot justify. | `TypeError: Invalid glTF JSON: nested deeper than 256 levels`, `RangeError: Unsupported EXR: a chunk decodes to more than 256 MiB`, `RangeError: Invalid EXR: chunks are too small for the image size`, `MargoError: Draco decode timed out after <limit> ms (corrupt data?); the decoder worker was restarted`. | The decoders' `timeout` option (`Infinity` turns the limit off). No real glTF comes near 256 levels; EXR: fewer channels per file. |
| Vertices per primitive | Enforced: gltf-pro takes up to 1,000,000 vertices per primitive (index accessors are bounded only by the decoded budget); the light `@margoengine/gltf` loader up to 1,000,000 vertices in total ([accessors.test.js](../packages/gltf-pro/tests/accessors.test.js): 1,000,002 indices decode, 1,000,002 vertices are refused). | Vertex buffers are also bound as storage buffers, and indices pass through float32, exact up to 2^24. | gltf-pro names the location: `glTF mesh 0 "part" primitive 0: Expected bounded triangle geometry`; the light loader: `RangeError: glTF exceeds one million vertices`. | gltf-pro instead of the light loader lifts the total; a primitive over 1,000,000 vertices must be split at export (for CAD, a coarser `margo-convert --linear-deflection`). |
| Dash caps (`dashCap` on `LineMaterial`) | By method: a cap reaches half the line width past its dash in pixels while dashes stay in world units, so a gap narrower than the width closes up. A cap stops at the line's own ends, and picks count gaps as line. | Ends are drawn in the fragment, with no extra geometry per dash. | Dotted lines keep the dot width on screen while their spacing follows perspective. | Widen `gapSize`, or use `worldUnits` widths. |
| Adaptive point size (`adaptiveSize` on `OctreeCloud` in `@margoengine/points/octree`) | By method: density is per node and assumes a surface (points per cube face), so a volume of points gets smaller points than its gaps need. Sizes are written on the CPU, at most 500,000 points a selection, when the nodes drawn under a node change. A random sample still leaves gaps: 98 percent of the pixels covered at the default `scale` of 1.5 in the browser check. | Point sizes already have a channel, so this needs no second shader. | Far parts look solid, with larger and softer points. A big camera jump takes a few frames to settle sizes. | Raise `scale`; set `maxPixelSize` to cap the growth. |
| Surfels (`SurfelPlugin` in `@margoengine/points/surfels`) | Enforced by method: opaque, always tests and writes depth, no textures, no `PointsPlugin.pick`; WebGPU only. Scene depth holds each disc pushed back by up to `blendDepth` radii. Each point draws twice, plus one full-screen pass. Extra targets take 12 bytes a sample: about 100 MB at 1920 x 1080 with 4x MSAA. Speed is not measured. | Weighted colour needs a sum and a divide, so it cannot be one blended draw; pushing the scene's own depth keeps meshes in the same test. | About twice the point cost of `PointsPlugin`. Something drawn later that lies within the push of a surface can show through it. | `PointsPlugin` where speed matters more; a smaller `blendDepth` for thin layers close together. |
| View offset (`CameraProjection.setViewOffset`, 2026-11-05) | By method: the frustum is built for the full image and the view shows one rectangle of it, so each tile or monitor must be rendered as its own frame at the rectangle's size. Effects that read the whole image (bloom, SSAO, TAA history, DOF blur) see only the tile, so seams can show where they reach across a tile edge. `fit` still frames the canvas, not the full image. | Core builds one matrix per view; the offset remaps that matrix, so culling, picking and every pass read the same view, with 0 core bytes. Screen-space passes cannot see pixels the frame does not contain. | Tiles of a screenshot match the full render for geometry; a wide blur or screen-space shadow can differ by a few pixels near a tile edge. | Render tiles with overlap (offset the rectangle outward, crop after) or keep post effects off for the tiles. |
| Focal length and film gauge (`CameraProjection.focalLength`, 2026-11-05) | By method: the lens sets the field of view only. It models a pinhole camera with a rectangular sensor, so no lens distortion, no crop of the sensor to the image beyond `sensorFit`, and no sensor shift. `camera.fov` is overwritten each frame while a focal length is set. | A projection matrix has one field of view; distortion and shift belong to wide-angle and off-axis projections in their own entries. | Setting `camera.fov` by hand while a focal length is set has no lasting effect. | Clear `focalLength` (set it to `undefined`) to drive `camera.fov` again. |
| Potree folders (`PotreeSource` in `@margoengine/points/potree`) | Enforced: reads 2.0 with encodings `DEFAULT` and `BROTLI`, and 1.4 to 1.8; other encodings and older versions throw `unsupported`. By method: hierarchy pages read when `load` reaches them (`hierarchy: 'eager'` reads all at open); 1.4 has no pages, its hierarchy is in `cloud.js`. The layout of a `BROTLI` node was measured on real folders, as no public text gives its bit order: every point of 3,954,696 fell in its node and the colour range matched the metadata's. `OctreeCloud` draws position and colour; other attributes reach the caller through `load`, or draw through `colorBy`. | Each node is its own request, and a page costs one more request on the way down. A leaf of 1.7 states 0 points, so its `count` is 0 until its file is read and the point budget cannot count it before that. | A `BROTLI` node decodes on the calling thread, measured at 3,954,696 points in 1.7 s (whole folder, local files, busy machine, diagnostic). | Pass a `brotli` that runs in a worker, or convert with encoding `DEFAULT`; older versions need converting. |
| Brotli decoding (`@margoengine/decoders/brotli`) | Enforced: output stops at `maxBytes`, 1 GiB by default. By method: a stream decodes whole, on the calling thread; large-window Brotli, which RFC 7932 does not define, is refused. | The output buffer doubles as the sliding window, so nothing streams out early. | A stream takes its whole output in memory. | Split data into several streams, as Potree does per node. |
| bzip2 and xz decoding (`@margoengine/decoders/bzip2`, `/xz`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: output stops at `maxBytes`, 1 GiB by default. By method: a stream decodes whole, on the calling thread. `.xz` reads the LZMA2 filter only, so BCJ and delta filters are `unsupported`. bzip2's randomised blocks are `unsupported`. Speed is not measured. | Browsers offer neither format; two file formats store them inside a file. | A damaged `.lzma`, or an `.xz` written with no check, shows only if the stream breaks. | Pass a low `maxBytes` for untrusted files. |
| Off-axis and oblique projection (`@margoengine/cameras/offaxis`, 2026-11-05) | By method: one flat screen per camera, and the eye must be in front of it. The canvas aspect is ignored. An oblique near plane keeps the frustum sides and moves only the near plane, so depth precision can drop as the plane turns edge-on to the view (not measured), and the camera must stay on the clipped side. | Core builds one matrix per view. A curved or multi-panel display is several flat screens, each its own view. Putting the plane into the matrix needs no clip plane in any shader. | Wrong or stretched pictures if the canvas shape differs from the screen. Z-fighting at long range with a strongly slanted plane. | Give each panel its own camera and view; keep the plane as square to the view direction as the effect allows. |
| Octrees under a moving transform (`OctreeCloud`) | Enforced: `matrix` or `attach(mesh)` places every node, and `select` takes a world view; the size test uses the longest stretch of the matrix. By method: a skewed matrix gets that same single stretch, so node sizes on screen may be high, never low. | Node selection runs in cloud space with the view and planes brought across. | A squashed octree may load more nodes than its look needs. | Keep the scale even, or raise `minNodePixels`. |
| Physical exposure (`@margoengine/cameras/physical`, 2026-11-05) | By method: exposure is relative, in stops against `referenceEV`, because the engine's light is not calibrated in photometric units. Depth of field and motion blur are those of the `DOF` and `MotionBlur` effects, so their limits apply (a maximum blur radius, WebGPU only for `DOF`). The thin-lens formulas assume a circle of confusion on the sensor, a pinhole lens elsewhere. | A scene lit with arbitrary intensities has no absolute luminance to meter, so the honest unit is the stop. | Changing ISO or shutter changes only the brightness (no sensor noise), and a very open aperture is capped by `DOF`'s `maxRadius`. | Set `referenceEV` to the setting that should look as the scene was lit; raise `maxRadius` for a wider aperture. |
| GPU picking of points (`PointPicking` in `@margoengine/points/pick`) | Enforced: picks what the view last drew, through the Picking overlay of `@margoengine/selection`, so meshes in front block a point and clipping planes, discs and alpha cuts apply. By method: a transparent point in front of an opaque one wins the pick when it was drawn. | The pick pass reuses the drawn view and the shared ID tile. | A cloud that has not been drawn yet cannot be hit. | Pick after a frame. |
| Wide-angle projections (`@margoengine/cameras/panini`, 2026-11-05) | By method: one rectilinear picture is resampled, so the source must be drawn at a wide field of view first (about 120 to 150 degrees across at most) and the rest of the sphere is not available. The stage runs after tone mapping, on display colour, and needs WebGPU and a perspective camera. Picking and screen-space overlays still use the source picture: map them with `WideAngle.map`. | A matrix cannot do these projections, because they bend straight lines. | A fisheye or Panini wider than the source shows black or is cropped (`crop`). Pixels at the far edge of a very wide source are stretched before they are resampled, so the sides are softer than the middle. | Draw the source wider, or use `crop: true`; for a full circle render a cube map with `@margoengine/environment` and sample it yourself. |
| Triangle order at import (`optimize` on `loadGLTF` and friends, `reorder` in `margo-convert`, 2026-11-07) | By method: `BLEND` primitives keep the file's triangle order, points and lines are skipped, and a primitive with one vertex per corner gains nothing (Fox: ACMR 3.0 before and after). `optimize` reorders indices only, as a load's vertices are shared with skins and morphs; it needs `@margoengine/geometry` and keeps `loadGLTFInWorker` on the main thread. `margo-convert` also moves the vertices. Measured on a shared machine, diagnostic, Node: BoomBox's 6,036 triangles ACMR 0.874 to 0.769 in 9.0 ms, Corset's 18,324 triangles 0.865 to 0.763 in 9.2 ms. No frame time is measured on any GPU (gap C14). | Off by default in a load, on in every preset: a model built once should not pay at each load. | A file that is already well ordered pays the load time for little. | Run `margo-convert` once, offline. |
| Wide-angle projections from a cube (`@margoengine/cameras/wide-cube`, 2026-11-07) | By method: up to 360 degrees across for equidistant and equisolid, below 360 for stereographic, 180 for orthographic, and below Panini's own fold (`acos(-d)` from the centre for `distance` d up to 1, `acos(-1/d)` above). Needs WebGPU, `@margoengine/post` and `@margoengine/environment`. Not measured: the cost, or sharpness against a flat picture. | Each direction reads a cube, so the sphere is complete, at six scene draws a frame on top of the main draw the stage replaces. | Faces run the scene passes only, so HDR effects ahead of the stage do not reach the picture. Picking still uses the plain main picture: map a pointer with `WideAngleCube.map`. Each face is drawn apart, so screen-space looks can show at the seams. | `facesPerFrame` below 6 spreads the capture, and the picture then lags. |
| HDR point colours (`colorFormat: 'float'`) | Enforced: 16 bytes a point against 4; a colour must be finite and 0 or more, or `RangeError`. By method: an 8-bit canvas still clips above 1 at the end. | Four float32 a point keep the values through the shader. | Colour memory and upload are four times RGBA8. | Keep the default `'rgba8'` for colours inside 0..1. |
| Mipmaps on point and sprite maps (`mipmaps`) | Enforced: an uploaded image gets a full chain, default on for points, off for sprites. By method: sprite atlases are left without mips as levels bleed between cells; `alphaTest` tests the filtered alpha. | One chain a map, made at upload. | An atlas sprite drawn small still shimmers, and a video map pays for a chain per upload. | Use a gutter around atlas cells with `mipmaps: true`, or `mipmaps: false` on a video. |
| Materials per part (`CulledParts` in `@margoengine/culling/parts`) | By method: a batch is one geometry, and each part's record holds its own colour, roughness, metalness and sidedness. No textures per part. | Core's `PbrMaterial` has no textures, and the record is what the draw reads, so colours cost no extra batch or draw. | A CAD assembly with hundreds of colours stays one batch per shape. A batch mixing single-sided and double-sided parts leaves the back-culled pipeline. | Build batches with `groupParts`; keep sidedness uniform inside a batch where the back-culled pipeline matters. |
| Occlusion for core meshes (`CulledMeshes`, `occlusion: true`, `@margoengine/culling`) | By method: occluders are last frame's kept instances of at least `occluderSize` pixels (default 8), redrawn with this frame's camera; only core's own batches occlude; a frame whose shader moves or discards surfaces (section planes) culls by frustum alone. | Hi-Z needs a depth the package can read, and core's depth target has no texture binding, so occluders are drawn a second time. | Hidden instances stop drawing from the second frame; the image matches core's. Each frame pays one depth pass and one pyramid. | Leave it off on open layouts, where the pass costs more than it saves. |
| GPU culling of models (`CulledModels` in `@margoengine/culling/models`) | By method: covers instance rows and plain primitives, against the frustum, screen size and, with `occlusion`, the occluders you add (Hi-Z). Skinned and morphed primitives are not culled, and neither is a primitive gltf-pro joined into an instanced batch; a displacing shader needs `padding`; the WebGL2 layer culls nothing. | Bounds of a deformed primitive are only known on the GPU. WebGPU has no multi-draw, so a plain primitive keeps its indirect draw call even when culled. | Rows cost no CPU per frame; each instanced primitive costs one uniform write and three dispatches. A plain primitive costs 96 bytes of upload a frame and one draw call: the GPU work goes, the CPU cost still grows with primitives. Each occluder draws depth-only every frame. Not timed. | Give repeated objects `instances`; give `addOccluder` only the large surfaces. |
| Occluders that are not parts (`addOccluder` on `CulledInstances` and `CulledParts`) | By method: takes core meshes, gltf-pro models, materials meshes and `Terrain`, drawn whole each frame. Back faces, cut-outs, blended, transmissive, displaced and clipped primitives do not occlude; a material extension that moves vertices cannot be detected. Not on `CulledMeshes`, which runs before the frame's deformers. | Core's depth has no texture binding, so an occluder is drawn a second time, and it must be a surface the frame really shows. | Parts behind a building stop drawing; the image is unchanged. Each occluder costs its triangles again every frame. | Add large things only, and never a model whose extension moves vertices. |
| Hi-Z with `bounds: 'box'` on slanted occluders | Any long part behind a surface seen at an angle | The test compares the part's nearest corner with the farthest occluder depth in its screen rectangle, and a slanted surface spans a range of depth | The part draws though it is hidden: extra work, never a wrong pixel | Split long parts, or accept it |
| `CascadeParts` and `CascadeInstances` lists | One u32 per slot per cascade, up to the GPU's storage binding size | Each cascade's list can hold every caster | Past the binding size, or on the WebGL2 layer, every cascade draws every caster, as the plain classes do | Use fewer cascades, or split the parts |
| `LodInstances` | 1 to 4 levels; 96 bytes per instance per level, plus the 96-byte source record | Each level's list can hold every instance, and one thread appends to one of them | Shadows: not cast, as `shadows.add` takes no `LodInstances`. WebGL2 layer: every shown instance draws the first level. Instances of a level draw in no fixed order, which only shows between exactly coplanar faces | Use `CascadeInstances` for casters of the first level |
| `MeshletMesh` | One mesh, any number of parts up to the device's storage binding size: the mesh's index memory once, again per part for the camera, and four more times per part once it casts shadows, plus 40 bytes per cluster. Two 16-byte frames and 20 bytes of draw arguments per part and list | Kept clusters of a part are written to that part's region, so one indirect draw draws it and no vertex pulling is needed under core's shader | CPU cost grows with the parts: one indirect draw each, kept or not. Hi-Z depth comes from whole parts drawn as occluders, not from last frame's clusters. A pick draws the whole mesh. On the WebGL2 layer the whole mesh draws | Use `CulledParts` for many small copies; split a large scene into several `MeshletMesh` |
| Attributes in generated LOD levels (`autoLOD`, `hierarchicalLOD`, `simplifyAttributes`) | Per primitive: mesh-edit's limit of 200,000 vertices or faces. A level is a subset of the source's vertices, so a position can only be one the source has. Triangles mesh-edit cannot take (a third face on an edge, a flipped neighbour) simplify as separate sheets, up to 4, then alone. | Vertex-subset collapses keep every attribute exact with no interpolation; the price is less accuracy per triangle than placing new vertices. Primitives simplify one at a time, so an open edge between two primitives can slide apart a little. | A core mesh's levels hold positions and normals only, as core geometry does. A materials mesh's levels do not follow a material swapped on the source later, and `shadows.add` takes each level on its own. | Give the source's borders the same vertices in one primitive, or use `Imposter` for the farthest level. |
| Generated levels of loaded models (`autoLOD`, `hierarchicalLOD` on a `loadGLTF` model) | `hierarchicalLOD` refuses skinned and morphed models; `autoLOD` levels them and needs WebGPU for that. Per primitive: mesh-edit's 200,000 vertices or faces. Vertex memory of the default three levels: about 0.875 of the source's, on top of it; GPU textures are shared. | A level is a model of the same file, so shadows, picking and materials work as on any model, at the cost of a model per level. | A level of a file with animations follows the source's drawing nodes in plugin order, so an `Animation` attached after `autoLOD` shows a frame late there; a file with no animations keeps the transforms of the call. Simplifying ignores bone influence: a collapse keeps one end's weights exactly, but a thin joint can lose shape in a deep pose. A flat-shaded level has new normals, the one thing in a level that is not the source's. Points and lines stay on level 0, and out of a far model. `selectVariant` on the source does not reach its levels. | `MSFT_lod` levels in the file (`LOD.fromModel`), or an `Imposter`, for hand-made levels. |
| Baked LOD levels (`bake` on `autoLOD`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: a materials mesh or loaded model only, a core mesh throws. By method: a map the source has of its own stays, and that kind is not baked; a primitive whose UVs leave the unit square or overlap on over 2% of its texels gets no maps; `'normal'` and `'ao'` only, so colour detail lost by a level stays lost. Each lower level's maps have half the last one's size, down to 32. The bake runs on the calling thread. Maps hold CPU copies with every mip: about 1.4 MB per primitive at the default 512. | Baking on the primitive's own UVs needs no new layout and draws through the same material. | A tiled or mirrored model gets nothing from `bake` until `unwrapMesh` gives it a clean layout. `hierarchicalLOD` does not bake. | Lower `size`, or bake once and save the model. |
| Dithered level cross-fade (`LODFade`, `@margoengine/lod/fade`) | WebGPU only: instant on WebGL2. Instant to or from an `Imposter` and for `hierarchicalLOD`. One transition per `LODSystem`. | Each pixel comes from one level by a fixed noise threshold: no blending, no sorting, depth stays right, at the cost of both levels drawing for `duration` (default 0.25 s) and a storage read per core-mesh pixel while attached. | Shadows see both levels during a fade. A model's first fade compiles its pipelines unless `prepare` ran. | Default of `LODFade` off: levels pop. |
| Fuller imposters (`LODOptions.color`, `emissive`, `frame`; `ImposterOptions.emissive`, `pose`, `margin`; `ImposterFrames`; `shadows.add(lods)`) | Instance record 96 bytes (was 64). `ImposterFrames`: `count` atlases, at least 2, a draw per frame in use. Baked emissive stops at 1 (RGBA8). Lighting effects: the `direct` hook only. | Colour and emissive ride on the one instanced draw, and shadows use the atlas instead of a mesh. | An imposter loses self-shadow within `r*(2/(frames-1)+4/frameSize)`. Casters are not culled per cascade. A skinned source needs `margin` for poses past its bind-pose bounds. | Keep a mesh level at the range where these matter. |
| Streamed levels (`LevelStream`, `@margoengine/lod/stream`) | Default 4 fetches at once and 256 MiB kept; a level on show is never freed, so `resident` can pass `maxBytes`. The default count holds vertices and indices, not textures. | A wish for a level that is not there holds the level on show, so nothing pops out, at the cost of a coarse level per object. | A fetch unasked for over two frames is aborted and starts over when wanted: partial bytes are not kept. No automatic retry: a failed level waits for `retry()`. | `bytes` per level to count textures; load up front for scenes that fit. |
| Moving sources of a far mesh (`HierarchicalLOD.update`, `follow`) | A cell keeps its meshes and centre. A rebuild merges the whole cell again, and simplifies it with a `ratio` below 1. | Far meshes stay plain meshes, so nothing is paid per frame for what stands still beyond comparing matrices. | `follow` runs a frame behind the sources and only for cells on their far mesh; a cell coming into range shows its old far mesh for one frame. | `update()` when you know something moved. |
| Selection outlines on imposters (`LODSystem.drawSelectionMask`, `SelectionMaskSource`) | An object counts as selected through its anchor or a level; a part of a model level does not. | The mask redraws only selected imposters, from a buffer of their own, with the imposter's own shader, so the outline sits on the imposter's pixels. | The mask pass runs the full imposter fragment shader for the selected objects. | Select the anchor or the model. |

## Geometry

| Limit | Starts at | Why | What you see | Escape hatch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NURBS surfaces (`@margoengine/geometry/nurbs`) | Enforced by method: no trimming curves, knot insertion or degree elevation; `NurbsCurve.tessellate` halves a span at most 12 times. | Trimming needs a boundary representation, which this engine does not carry; revolves, control nets and whole patches do not. | A trimmed patch meshes untrimmed. | Cut the mesh with `@margoengine/mesh-edit`. |
| Mesh surface sampler (`@margoengine/geometry/sample`) | Enforced by method: points are independent random draws, so they can clump; there is no minimum spacing. Vertex weights are linear across each triangle. | Independent draws cost one search each and need no neighbour lookups. | Two points can land close together. | `@margoengine/scatter` for spaced placement on terrain. |
| Mesh optimisation (`@margoengine/geometry/optimize`) | Enforced by test ([optimize.test.js](../packages/geometry/tests/optimize.test.js)): shuffled grids, spheres and knots end under 0.8 cache misses per triangle on a 16-vertex first-in first-out cache (3 before); nested shells lose over 40 percent of their overdraw while the miss ratio stays within 6 percent. Those are stand-ins for a GPU: frame time on a real one is not measured. | The cache model is a simulation; real GPUs differ in cache size and batching. | Triangle order changes, so a transparent mesh that relied on it can look different. | `overdraw: false` keeps the sort off; leave the mesh out to keep its order. |
| Bending along a curve (`@margoengine/geometry/flow`) | Enforced by method: frames are sampled `segments` times (default 256) and interpolated, and only vertices move. A shape wider than the radius of a bend folds over itself. | Sampling once makes each vertex a table lookup. | A coarse mesh cuts corners; a tight bend pinches the inner side. | Add segments along the axis; raise `segments`. |
| Extrusion UVs (`uvGenerator` of `@margoengine/geometry/extrude`) | Enforced by method: the generator runs once per triangle corner on the CPU, and wall `u` comes from the outline before the bevel widens it. | Walls and caps are separate triangles, so a corner can differ per surface. | A texture on a wide bevel stretches a little across `u`. | Use `distance` and your own `u` from `shape`. |
| Split tangents (`splitTangents`, `computeTangents` with `split`) | Enforced by method: checked against Blender's MikkTSpace on two meshes, to a dot product above 0.9995 per corner with matching signs. It follows the published grouping and is not a port of the reference library, so a mesh with unusual degenerate triangles may group differently. `w` assumes v up. | Clean room: written from the paper. | A rare corner could differ from a bake on degenerate geometry. | Remove zero-area triangles first; flip v on glTF UVs. |
| Baked poses (`bakeMorphTargets`, `bakeSkin`) | Enforced by method: four joints per vertex, linear blend skinning, Float32 output. | One pose on the CPU for picking, physics or export; the same mix glTF defines. | Linear blending collapses at sharp twists, as it does on the GPU. | Bake several poses, or split the joint. |
| Delaunay triangulation (`@margoengine/geometry/delaunay`) | Enforced by method: adds no points of its own, so thin outlines keep thin triangles; crossing constraints and self-crossing rings are refused. Exact tests fall back to whole-number arithmetic, which is slower on grids and other exactly cocircular input. | Keeping the input points is what stops T-junctions; refinement would add vertices. | Cocircular input costs more time. | Add inner points with `points`; split crossing edges. |
| Colour conversion (`@margoengine/math/color-space`) | Enforced: four named spaces (`srgb`, `srgb-linear`, `display-p3`, `display-p3-linear`), D65 white only; any other name throws `TypeError`. Results are not clamped. | Those are the spaces WebGPU canvases and CSS `color()` share, and unclamped values convert back unchanged. | A colour outside the target space has channels below 0 or above 1. Below 0 the transfer curve runs on as its straight segment, not mirrored. | Call `clipToSRGB` on linear sRGB to bring a colour inside sRGB. |
| Oriented boxes (`@margoengine/math/obb`) | Enforced: non-finite values, a negative half size, a matrix that is not a rotation and a negative or non-finite `epsilon` throw. `applyMatrix4` throws `RangeError` for a projective matrix, a zero scale axis or a scale that would shear the box. `intersectsOBB` pads the axis cosines by `epsilon`, 1e-6 by default. | A sheared box is no longer a box. Near-parallel edges have a cross product close to zero, and the pad stops them reporting a false separation. | Two boxes closer than about `epsilon` times their size can report an overlap; a real overlap is never missed. | Pass 0 as `epsilon` for an exact test on boxes with no near-parallel edges, or bound a sheared object with `Box3`. |
| Segments and triangles (`@margoengine/math/line3`, `/triangle`) | Enforced by method: two directions count as parallel, and a triangle as degenerate, when the sine squared of their angle is below 1e-14 (float64 rounding). `Triangle.containsPoint` tests the point's projection on the plane, not its height above it. | Below that angle the closed forms lose all their digits, so the parallel and collapsed cases take their own paths. | A sliver thinner than about 1e-7 radians gets null barycentric weights. | Use `closestPointToPoint`, which still answers for a degenerate triangle; check `distanceToPoint` when height off the plane matters. |
| Polygon booleans and offsets (`@margoengine/geometry/polygon`) | Enforced by method: all points go onto one grid of about 2^26 steps across the input. A vertex at a crossing moves up to half a step, edges closer than a step touch, and parts under two steps wide are dropped. | One exact grid is what makes every test safe: no epsilon to tune, and no failed or crossing output at any scale. | Two features 1e-8 of the input's width apart merge. Very large plans with very fine detail lose the detail. | Run distant parts separately, or scale up the part that needs the detail. |
| Project and unproject (`@margoengine/math/project`) | Enforced by method: core's `camera.matrix` is Float32 and holds zeros until the first frame; a point at or behind the camera plane returns null; a matrix with no inverse throws `RangeError`. | One matrix serves every camera, orthographic included; null keeps a point behind the camera from showing up mirrored on screen. | Results carry float32 precision, not float64, and depth z follows the camera (WebGPU near = 1, far = 0; WebGL near = -1, far = 1). | Pass your own `{matrix}` as a Float64Array for float64 results. Take depth from `projectPoint` instead of hard-coding it. |
| Spherical harmonics (`@margoengine/math/sh`) | Enforced by method: three bands, 9 RGB coefficients, CPU only. No rotation of a coefficient set. | Three bands hold diffuse light to within a few percent, and match the layout `@margoengine/environment` reads. | A single point of light overshoots: a distant light reads 17/16 of its irradiance facing it and 1/16 facing away, where the truth is 1 and 0. Results are not clamped. | For sharp light use a real light and keep SH for the soft part. To turn a probe, project it again from rotated samples. |
| Sweep and loft (`@margoengine/geometry/sweep`) | Enforced by method: sections stay flat and are matched by their share of the way round, with no point-to-point control. Holes of a loft pair by their order, so every section has the same number. | One rule fits any pair of outlines and keeps every corner. | A twisted quad is two flat triangles, so a twisted solid loses volume in proportion to the step (0.4 percent at 128 steps for a quarter turn). A sharp bend in a path pinches the profile. | Raise `steps`, round the path, or pass 3D rings with matching points and `align: false`. |
| Capsules (`@margoengine/math/capsule`) | Enforced: a negative or non-finite radius and any matrix beyond rotation, translation and uniform scale throw `RangeError`. `pushOutOfTriangle` resolves one triangle at a time, and returns null for an axis lying in a degenerate triangle. | Under other matrices the shape stops being a capsule. One triangle at a time keeps the type a shape, not a physics solver. | Several triangles at once (a corner) need repeated passes in the caller, and a push out of one triangle can move into the next. | For a full character controller with sliding and stepping use `@margoengine/physics`. |
| Tessellation (`@margoengine/geometry/tessellate`) | Enforced by method: `maxTriangles`, 5,000,000 by default, stops a tiny limit. Edges are measured in the mesh's own units, not on screen. | Plain edge length needs no camera and keeps neighbours in agreement. | Long thin triangles add many vertices along their length. Flat areas are split as finely as curved ones. | Pick the limit for the deformation, or tessellate only the part that bends. |
| GPU geometry (`@margoengine/geometry/gpu`) | Enforced by method: WebGPU only, `attach` throws `unsupported` on WebGL2; 1,000,000 vertices per item, as core. Tube and ribbon frames are walked by one GPU invocation, so that step grows with `segments`. Speed is not measured. | Each frame depends on the one before it. | CPU raycasts and exports see zeros until `read()`. Control points written by your own compute pass are lost with the device. Default bounds are never culled. | Pass `bounds`; keep `segments` to what the curve needs; call `setPoints` again after a restore if you drive points on the GPU. |
| Exact booleans (`@margoengine/mesh-edit/boolean-exact`) | Enforced by method: every side test and all topology are exact, then corners round to float64. Faces thinner than about 4e-9 of their length, or a few float64 steps, fold away; `collapsedFaces` counts them. A single surface must not cross itself; overlapping shells of one input are joined first. Only faces in the box where the two bounds overlap go through the exact trees, and work there grows with their number squared: measured on two spheres of 10,224 triangles, one operation takes 7 to 11 s at half overlap, 2 to 3 s with a cutter a fifth the size, under 1 s when only the edges overlap (it was 40 to 75 s on 10,368 faces). A surface with nothing in that box, such as a cutter wholly inside a solid, takes the whole-mesh path. | `PolygonMesh` stores float64 corners and every face must pass the validity check, so a true result thinner than that has no valid float64 face. | A closed valid mesh that can differ from the exact solid by about 4e-9 of a face length; `MargoError` `failed` for a surface that crosses itself. | `booleanMeshes` in `/modeling` is faster on clean inputs; scale thin features up, or split large meshes. |

By method: neighbors weigh equally, so uneven triangles drift along the surface. | Equal weights need no angle data and are what Taubin's method describes; cotangent weights fail on the thin triangles scans have. | Volume stays within 2% on a noisy sphere over 20 passes; very uneven meshes change shape slightly along the surface. | Run `remeshMesh` first. |

By method: isotropic output is triangles, or with `quads` mostly quads from a greedy matching with an augmenting search, with triangles left where seams and creases leave them no partner: on a rounded cube at `targetLength` 0.15, measured, 81.5% of triangles pair with seams kept (503 quads, 228 of 1,234 triangles alone) and 100% with `preserveSeams: false` (627 quads, none alone); a warped quad of either remesher is stored as two triangles (`quads` keeps the topology), parity counts an overlap of two parts as outside, and UVs do not carry through voxels. | Faces must be planar, and a grid has no link back to corner UVs. | `RangeError` above `maxCells`; `TypeError` for open input; `nonManifoldEdges` above 0 where two sheets pass within a voxel. | Raise `voxelSize` or `maxCells`; `fillMeshHoles` closes input; `booleanMeshesExact` unions overlapping parts first. |

By method: output is triangles; a triangle covering more than 512 grid cells is tested by every ray. Measured (Node 26, one run, 199,712 triangles, other lanes running, diagnostic): a pick 10 ms before, 0.01 ms now; a dab of radius 0.08 on a unit sheet 21 ms, 1.4 ms; a stroke's undo step 90 ms, 1.6 ms. In a page, a stroke on 62,208 triangles uploaded 75,120 of 2,870,320 bytes. | A hash grid changes only where something crosses a cell, while a BVH needs a whole-tree refit per dab and a rebuild per split. Same output as testing everything: compared byte for byte over 1,273 checks. | With dynamic topology each change of triangles rewrites the whole mesh on the GPU, because gltf-pro's ranged write keeps the indices. A vertex dragged so far that a ray crosses more cells than the mesh has triangles falls back to testing every triangle. | Sculpt a part of a mesh per session above 200,000 faces; use fixed topology while the mesh is large. |

By method: one `PBRInstances` per glTF node of the tile, float32 rows relative to the mean instance position, so a tile wider than about 30 km loses millimetres; the batch table is read but not used for picking or colours; an external glTF resolves against the tile's first content URL. | i3dm places instances in the tile's frame; the rows are float32 on the GPU. | Instances of a very wide tile move by up to a few millimetres; picking returns the tile's model, not one instance. | Split very wide i3dm tiles; read `content.batchTable` yourself for per-instance data. |
