| Terrain height and ray queries (`@margoengine/terrain/query`, 2026-11-07) | By method: the bilinear surface the vertex shader samples, on the CPU; the drawn clipmap is triangles at its cell spacing that morph between rings, so a drawn point can differ from the answer by that triangulation's error (not measured); normals are the shader's central differences, not a terrain's own `normalmap`; nothing outside the heightmap's rectangle; the grid is 2 to 16,384 samples a side; `TerrainQuery.read` reads once and needs `COPY_SRC` on the heightmap; memory is the heights plus two thirds of their count in floats (2.8 MB at 1,025 x 1,025) (LIM-426). | One exact surface that every ring approximates; a CPU tree answers a ray without a GPU round trip. | A foot can float or sink by the ring's triangulation error on coarse rings; edits to the GPU heightmap are not seen until you `refresh` your copy or read again. | Match `cellSize` to the texel spacing so the finest ring's vertices sit on texels; call `refresh` after each edit. |
| Terrain layer materials (`@margoengine/terrain/layers-pbr`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `normal` 0 to 2, `occlusion` 0 to 1, `roughness` 0 to 2 (result 0.02 to 1), `heightBlend` 0 to 1; maps are `rgba8unorm` or `rgba16float` arrays with one layer per terrain layer. By method: dielectric only (B of the surface map is ignored); AO darkens the terrain's own ambient term, not image-based light another effect adds; height blending is one pass (each weight times 2^(8 heightBlend (height - 1))), not a two-pass depth cut-off; where no layer's rule applies the vertex normal and terrain roughness are used; SSR's capture keeps the terrain's normal and roughness; it must be attached before an effect that replaces the terrain shader; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-430). | A lighting effect patch keeps the root terrain entry and its shader unchanged for everyone who does not import it. | Reflections on wet rock show no normal-map detail; IBL is not occluded. | Bake AO into the colour for IBL scenes; give layers that need sharp height cuts a high `heightBlend`. |
| Terrain height streaming (`@margoengine/terrain/stream`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `tileTexels` 2 to 1,024, an even `window` of 2 to 16 tiles whose texels fit the device's 2D texture limit, `weights` 0 to 4 (4 layers each), `concurrency` 1 to 64; tiles are r32float heights and rgba8unorm weights. By method: the window follows the engine camera only and moves when the camera is more than 0.75 of a tile from its centre, so ground at least (window / 2 - 0.75) tiles away on each axis is in it and nothing past the window is drawn (no far ring of lower resolution); exposed tiles show `fill` until they load; a failed tile keeps `fill` until it leaves the window (no retry); no normal map or tint texture; painted weights sit where any terrain's weights sit over its bounds (texel centres, up to half a texel from the height samples), so a window move shifts them by 1 / window of a texel; memory is three window-sized copies of 4 bytes a texel plus three per weight texture; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-435). | Scrolling with GPU copies keeps the root terrain shader, which clamps rather than wraps, unchanged for everyone who does not stream. | A world bigger than the window shows only the window's ground; far mountains past it are not drawn. | Pick a window and tile size whose half-width covers the view distance, or draw far ground with `@margoengine/biomes/far` or a skybox. |
| Terrain sculpt brushes (`@margoengine/terrain/sculpt`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: an `r32float` heightmap and `rgba8unorm` weights created with usage 15 (storage, copy source and destination, sampled); `radius` and `amount` above 0, `strength` 0 to 1, `layer` within the terrain's layers, `undoLimit` 0 to 1,000 (default 32). By method: brushes work in the heightmap's texels (one dab is one compute pass over the brush's rectangle, at most one submit per dab and weight texture); paint moves weights at least one 8-bit step a dab; `heights` and `weights` follow the GPU only once a stroke's rectangle is read back (`flush()`), and `flatten`'s default height is read from them when the stroke begins; undo and redo wait for pending read backs and must be awaited before the next dab; an rgba16float or rgba8unorm heightmap is not sculptable; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-437). | A copy of the brush rectangle is read and the terrain's texture written, so no texel is read while another invocation writes it, on any GPU. | A dab right after `undo()` without awaiting it can be overwritten by the undo. | `await sculpt.undo()` before the next dab; use an r32float heightmap for terrain that is edited. |
| Terrain holes (`@margoengine/terrain/holes`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: an `r8unorm` or `rgba8unorm` one-layer mask (R below 0.5 is a hole), 1 to 8,192 cells a side in `TerrainHoles`. By method: holes are whole mask cells, cut by cell centre, and their rims are vertical cuts through the surface (no cave walls or skirt: a mesh draws the opening's sides); the clipmap's vertices are not removed, so a hole costs what ground costs to draw; a `TerrainQuery` ray is exact with one mask cell per heightmap cell or coarser by a whole factor, and with a finer mask skips a cell hit inside a hole whole; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-456). | A per-fragment mask test in every pass keeps shadows, reflections and occlusion in step with what is drawn, at one texel load a fragment. | A hole has no walls: through it, the ground beyond and below shows, but not the sides of the opening. | Model cave mouths and tunnel walls as meshes around the hole. |
| Terrain runtime virtual texture (`@margoengine/terrain/rvt`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `texel` above 0, `size` 256, 512, 1,024, 2,048 or 4,096, 1 to 12 levels, `steep` 0 to 1, up to 4,096 decals, `rgba8unorm-srgb` or `rgba16float`; not with `TerrainMaterials`. By method: the cache holds colour only and is baked top down, so ground steeper than `steep` and ground past the coarsest level draw with the terrain's own shader; `bump` relief shows only there; edits to heights, weights, tint or streamed tiles show after `invalidate(area)`; a road is a decal a segment, so sharp bends leave a gap on their outer side; decal edges blend over about two pixels; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-463). | Baking the layer blend once into a camera-centred cache makes a pixel of ground cost one or two samples whatever the number of layers and decals, and lets other materials match the ground they stand on. | The cache is colour only: normals, roughness and relief come from the terrain's own shader where it draws. | Keep `steep` below the slopes that stretch; call `invalidate(area)` after sculpting or painting; give roads more points at sharp bends. |
| Terrain near displacement (`@margoengine/terrain/displace`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `depth` 0 to 1 (per layer too), `distance` above 0, `steps` 4 to 64; needs `TerrainMaterials`; shadows need square, power-of-two surface maps. By method: parallax only, so the ground's outline against the sky stays the clipmap's; top projection only, on ground no steeper than about 57 degrees; the view ray reads mipmaps at the pixel's footprint and the shadow ray the full-size map, so far relief shadows alias; a shadow ray stops after 160 steps and counts the rest as lit; where layers of different scale overlap the coarser are bounded by up to four of their own texels; shadows darken the sun only; proved on one GPU, a Radeon 680M; cost not timed (LIM-483). | Near ground shows the depth, occlusion and self-shadow of its height maps without more geometry, and a maximum mipmap keeps the shadow ray from stepping over thin ridges. | The relief is drawn, not built: it casts no shadow on other objects and no other object shadows into it, and physics and `TerrainQuery` see the clipmap's surface. | Keep `distance` to the range where the relief is seen; pass `shadows: false` for maps that are not square and a power of two; keep `depth` to the real relief's size. |
| Terrain deformation (`@margoengine/terrain/deform`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `size` 64 to 2,048 texels within the device's 2D texture and storage limits, `texel`, `depth` and `distance` above 0, `rim`, `softness`, `darken` and each `layers` entry 0 to 1, `fade` 0 or more, `steps` 4 to 32; a print's length and width up to 94 texels; up to 1,024 prints pressed a frame (the rest wait). Unmeasured: cost, other GPUs (LIM-497). | Prints are shaded, not built: a fragment-level relief needs no clipmap cells finer than a footprint and costs one compute pass a changed frame. | By method: prints are shading only (parallax and normals), so the ground's outline, shadows, SSR capture and `TerrainQuery` keep the undeformed surface; a print is kept only while the map's window holds it and lost on device loss; depths are stored as half floats (0.06 mm at 10 cm); a print's walls are not shadowed by the sun; softness by layer needs painted weights. | Keep prints within half the map of the camera (or `follow` the player); give rock layers softness 0 with painted weights. |
| Mesh to terrain blending (`@margoengine/terrain/blend`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `height` finite and above 0, `noise` and `normal` 0 to 1; the virtual texture attached to the same engine first; five group-0 bindings from 40. Unmeasured: cost, other GPUs (LIM-502). | The ground colour comes from the virtual texture the terrain already bakes, so a mesh's foot reads exactly the colour the terrain draws there with no second copy of the layer rules. | By method: only colour, roughness and normal fade (the mesh's shape and shadow stay); only core's standard material; no fade past the cache's reach; the heightmap's normal, not a terrain `normalmap`. | Keep the mesh's base within `height` of the ground; use one virtual texture whose reach covers where meshes stand. |
| World-space and XR panels (`@margoengine/ui/world`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: width, height and `pixelRatio` finite and above 0; a unit-quaternion rotation; the panel texture within the device's 2D texture limit. By method: flat rectangles only; drawn with depth test and no depth write, so transparent effects drawn after a panel are not hidden by it; one texture and one pair of glyph atlases per panel; one-sided by default. Unmeasured: cost, texture memory, other GPUs, a real XR headset (LIM-510). | A texture per panel keeps a static menu free: it is redrawn only when its UI changes, and every view and eye samples the same one. | A one-sided panel vanishes from behind and takes no input there; a panel's `Ui` resized by `UiRenderer` too would be stretched. | `doubleSided: true`; give each panel its own `Ui` that only `WorldPanels` draws. |
| Wrapping flex rows (`wrap` in `@margoengine/ui`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `wrap` true or false; rows only (a column ignores it). By method: lines break by each child's base size and margins, before `grow` and `shrink`; every line is as tall as its tallest child; lines stack down from the top of the row (no `align-content`); a wrapping row scrolls vertically, not along its main axis. Unmeasured: layout cost on large lists (LIM-513). | Breaking by base size first is the flexbox order (CSS Flexible Box Layout, multi-line containers), and it keeps a line's break independent of how the next line grows. | A growing child never pulls the next child up onto its line; a column of tags does not wrap into a second column. | Put fixed or `minWidth` sizes on children that must stay together; use `direction: 'grid'` for equal cells. |
| Arabic and Indic shaping in UI text (`ligatures`, `direction` in `@margoengine/ui`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `ligatures` true or false; `direction` `'auto'`, `'ltr'` or `'rtl'`. By method: shaping is the browser's, one word per run, so no join or ligature forms across a space; the paragraph direction is the first strong letter's (Unicode bidi rules P2 and P3) or the set direction. Not done: toggles, slider values, list details and rows of widgets keep left-to-right placement; layout is not mirrored. Unmeasured: shaping speed on long texts (LIM-514). | A run per word is how the text package draws shaped scripts, and its line breaking stays by word. | A ligature that spans words, or a mirrored settings screen, does not form; a right-to-left form keeps its toggle on the right. | Put right-to-left rows in the order they should read (children last to first), or use Labels for mirrored text. |
| Screen UI on the WebGL2 preview backend (`@margoengine/ui`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the WebGL2 code is a chunk loaded on the first WebGL2 frame, so frames before that load show no UI; the UI is drawn again every frame (WebGL2 clears its canvas) and uploaded only when it changed; one draw per run, since WebGL2 has no base instance. Not done: world panels (`@margoengine/ui/world`) draw on WebGPU only. Unmeasured: cost per frame; WebKit and Firefox (LIM-516). | Loading on first use keeps the WebGL2 code off WebGPU pages; world panels need render-to-texture with mipmaps, kept to the WebGPU path. | A WebGL2 page shows its UI a frame or so after start; a diegetic screen in a WebGL2 scene shows nothing. | Use the screen UI for menus on WebGL2, or the WebGPU backend for world panels. |
| Virtual lists (`VirtualList` in `@margoengine/ui`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `count` and `overscan` whole numbers, 0 or more; `rowHeight` above 0; `row` a function returning a string or a `{ label }` entry. By method: every row is `rowHeight` tall; rows are built in `Ui.update`, so a scroll between an update and a draw can show the overscan's edge for one frame; a focused row that leaves the built range loses the focus. Unmeasured: build cost per scroll (LIM-526). | One row height makes a row's place a multiplication, so the list never measures rows it does not build. | Rows of different heights, or a focus that must outlive a long scroll, need a `List`. | Use a `List` for short lists with tall rows; `scrollToIndex` or `focusIndex` to bring a row back. |
| Markup and style sheets (`@margoengine/ui/markup`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: well-formed markup (every tag closed, attribute values quoted, no repeated attribute or id); known elements, properties and actions; numbers, true or false, and 1, 2 or 4 numbers where an option takes them. Not done: DOCTYPE, CDATA, namespaces and named references beyond the five of XML; pseudo-classes, attribute selectors, `+`, `~`, `@` rules and `!important`; restyling a built screen. Unmeasured: build time of large screens (LIM-528). | A small subset keeps the parser and the cascade a few hundred lines, and the selectors are the ones designers use for widget trees. | A sheet written for the web may use selectors the parser rejects, with the line named. | Give the element a class or an id; set options in code after `buildScreen` for anything dynamic. |
| Label decluttering (`@margoengine/labels/declutter`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `padding` 0 or more, `cell` 1 or more, `positions` pairs of finite numbers, `priority` a number (infinity allowed). By method: boxes are the elements' `offsetWidth` by `offsetHeight` times their distance scale, measured once; the greedy order is priority, then shown last time, then distance, so a lower label can lose a place a later one would have left free. Unmeasured: cost per frame with thousands of labels (LIM-532). | Greedy placement by rank is the standard fast method for point labels and keeps the important ones; a full search for the most labels is far slower. | A label whose text grows keeps its old box until `remeasure()`; a rotated or round element is treated as its bounding box. | Call `label.remeasure()` after changing its text; give labels that must stay a higher `priority`. |
| Depth with CSS 3D objects (`CSS3DObject` in `@margoengine/labels`, 2026-11-08) | By method: an element is cut at the near plane by its layout rectangle (`offsetWidth` by `offsetHeight`, measured once until `remeasure()`), so content painted outside its box is cut with it; an element not laid out yet measures 0 and is shown or hidden by its origin. Not done: no per-pixel depth test against the canvas; the `occlude` hook hides a whole object. While cut, `clip-path` flattens the element's own 3D children (LIM-540). | HTML over the canvas cannot read the depth buffer, and CSS perspective does not clip at the near plane, so the renderer clips by geometry. | A panel half inside a wall shows whole in front of it; a shadow painted outside the box is cut with the box. | Hide panels behind geometry with the `occlude` hook and a raycast; call `remeasure()` after content changes size. |
| Leader lines and callouts (`@margoengine/labels/callouts`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `width`, `dot` and `minLength` finite and 0 or more; `screenOffset` two finite numbers. By method: one straight line per label from its projected point to the nearest point of its box (the measured element size times its distance scale), drawn under every label; a label whose box covers its point, or lies within `minLength` of it, gets none. Not done: lines are not kept from crossing labels or each other, and `Declutter` places boxes without looking at lines. Unmeasured: cost per frame with many callouts (LIM-542). | A leader line is a pointer, so the nearest point keeps it short; routing lines around labels is a layout problem of its own. | Two callouts whose offsets cross draw crossing lines; a line can run under another label. | Choose offsets that fan out from the model (left labels to the left), or let a wrapped `Declutter` move overlapping boxes. |
| Scaled ancestors (`LabelRenderer` in `@margoengine/labels`, 2026-11-08) | By method: the overlay follows CSS scales on the canvas's ancestors, read from drawn widths each update; in another `parent` the canvas's scale is its drawn width over `offsetWidth`, a whole number of pixels, so a canvas of fractional CSS width is matched to about half a pixel across. Not done: rotations and skews on the ancestors, and any transform on the canvas element itself (LIM-546). | `getBoundingClientRect` reports drawn sizes and `offsetWidth` layout sizes, so their ratio is the scale; a rotation needs the full matrix, which the DOM does not give. | Labels drift off their points inside a rotated element or on a canvas with its own transform. | Put the transform on an ancestor of the canvas, not the canvas; keep rotated frames for static pages. |
| Smooth partial occlusion (`fade` in `@margoengine/html`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `radius` 0 to 256 CSS pixels, `samples` a whole number 2 to 8, `duration` 0 to 10 s. By method: the opacity is the clear share of a `samples` x `samples` grid of depth tests over a square around the anchor, so it moves in steps of 1 / samples squared and a gap in the geometry narrower than the grid spacing can be missed; samples off the canvas count as clear. Both backends (WebGL2 by occlusion queries, LIM-552); results arrive a frame or two late (LIM-550). Unmeasured: cost per frame at full capacity (up to 64 depth reads an anchor). | One compute dispatch reads every anchor's grid from the shared depth target, so the fade needs no extra render pass; the grid is what bounds its work. | An anchor at a thin pole or a fence fades by the share of the grid the pole covers, not by the share of the element's box. | Raise `samples` for finer steps, or `radius` to the element's half size to fade by its whole box. |
| Occlusion on WebGL2 (`HTML` in `@margoengine/html`, 2026-11-08) | By method: on the `@margoengine/webgl` preview backend each depth test is an `ANY_SAMPLES_PASSED` occlusion query of a one-pixel point drawn after the scene at the anchor's depth less `depthBias`, through the renderer's own camera matrix; one round is in flight per anchor, read when the browser makes it available (a frame or more later); samples off the view count as clear; `fade` runs here too, so the fade row's "WebGPU only" no longer holds (LIM-552). Not done: geometry other passes draw after the html pass is not seen. Unmeasured: cost per frame with many anchors (one draw call and query per test). | The preview draws straight to the canvas, whose depth WebGL2 cannot sample or read back; a query against it is the only test of that depth. | Many anchors with `fade` on WebGL2 cost samples x samples draws each per round. | Keep `samples` small on WebGL2, or use WebGPU, where one dispatch tests every anchor. |
| DOM drawn into the scene (`HTMLTexture` in `@margoengine/html/texture`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `pixelRatio` 0.25 to 4, a size above 0 (the element's CSS size or `width` and `height`), a matrix of 16 finite numbers, WebGPU (the WebGL2 fallback throws). By method: the element is drawn as a texture, so the quad shows a picture of it, drawn again on a mutation, input, focus, hover or HTML-in-canvas paint event, a frame or more after the change; with `'foreign-object'` the element's fonts and images must be data URLs the SVG image embeds (it loads nothing), and changes no event shows (a loaded font, a style sheet) need `invalidate()`; the pointer is routed by the nearest quad under it, so scene geometry in front of a quad does not stop the pointer reaching the element (LIM-556). Unmeasured: raster cost per redraw, cost with many surfaces. | The browser's own hit test, focus and events reach the element only where it is laid out, so the invisible holder is moved under the pointer; HTML-in-canvas snapshots only elements inside the viewport, so the holder rests at its top left. | A panel half behind a wall still takes clicks through the wall. | Turn `interactive` off on a surface while geometry covers it, or keep interactive quads unblocked. |
| Anchor capacity (`HTML` in `@margoengine/html`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `capacity` a whole number 1 to 1,048,576 (default 1,024), so at most 16 MiB of anchor points and 16,384 workgroups of 64. By method: slots and buffers grow by doubling to the capacity and never shrink; per-frame work runs to the highest slot ever used, so removing the newest anchors does not shorten it; buffers grow at the next depth test, so a new anchor past the old size waits one test (LIM-559). Unmeasured: cost per frame with many anchors (each still has its own DOM element; on WebGL2 one query a test). | Growing to the anchors in use keeps a large `capacity` free until it is used; one dispatch still tests every anchor. | A page that adds 100,000 anchors and removes most keeps their slots and the work to the highest. | Reuse anchors, or make a new `HTML` overlay after removing most of them. |
| Terrain on the WebGL2 compatibility layer (`@margoengine/terrain` with `@margoengine/webgl2-compat`, 2026-11-08) | Measured: native and layer pictures differ by at most 1 of 255 per pixel (rules, painted weights and a hole, the runtime virtual texture); a frame of six clipmap levels is 6 GL draws and 48 GL calls on the layer (50 with the virtual texture); Radeon 680M, ANGLE on Direct3D 11. Unmeasured: frame time on the layer, other GPUs and ANGLE back ends (LIM-503). | The terrain's passes are draws only, which the layer translates as they are, so terrain has no WebGL2 route and no WebGL2 code. | Only the root entry and `rvt` are checked on the layer; `layers-pbr`, `stream`, `sculpt`, `deform`, `displace` and `blend` have not run there (the compute passes of `sculpt`, `deform` and `displace` would run on the layer's own tiers). | Call `install()` from `margoengine/webgl2-compat` before `Engine.create`; keep to the root entry and `rvt` on WebGL2 until the others are checked. |
| Planet terrain (`@margoengine/terrain/planet`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: `radius` above 0, `resolution` an even 4 to 64, `maxLevel` 0 to 24, `screenError` above 0, `budget` 0 or more, `maxPatches` 6 to 16,384, `height` finite. Measured (diagnostic, Node on the busy Beelink): about 1.3 ms of main-thread time to build a patch at resolution 32 with the default ground; a view 100 m above the ground at 1280 x 720 needs 1,682 patches (2.2 s with no budget). Unmeasured: frame cost, other GPUs (LIM-507). | Heights are sampled on the CPU in float64, so any height function works and patches draw relative to the eye with no float32 world coordinates; the price is main-thread building, spread over frames by `budget`. A split builds all four children (culled ones too), so the view never shows a hole while it refines. | By method: no shadows, SSR, lighting effects or layer textures; colours by height and slope only; one view a frame; the drawn surface is the height sampled at the patch's vertices, so `altitude` reads the exact ground, not the drawn one; past `maxPatches` the farther ground stays coarser; the GPU holds about 24 KB a patch (50 MB at the default 2,048). | Raise `budget` for faster refinement, lower `screenError` or `resolution` for fewer patches, and keep a camera above `altitude` 0. |
| Orthographic tile selection (`TilesRenderer`, 2026-11-07) | Proved in Node on generated view-projection matrices (LIM-370), not on a GPU. By method: a camera counts as orthographic when its matrix's clip w row is (0, 0, 0, w) to 1e-9 of w; the view height is 2 w over the length of the clip y row, in world units, divided by the frame's scale. | Core keeps one view-projection matrix for every camera, so the renderer reads it rather than a camera type. | Before the first frame core's matrix is all zeros and selection is perspective; a projection that is neither (a clip w that changes only slightly with depth) is read as perspective. | Call `tiles.update()` after the first frame, or give `Tileset.update` your own `orthographicHeight`. |
| Hosted tileset sign-in and credits (`@margoengine/tiles/ion`, 2026-11-07) | Proved in Node against stand-in servers that answer as the ion endpoint and a keyed service are documented to (LIM-374); no live service was asked (no token on this machine). Credits read from real Khronos GLBs. By method: one renewal per expiry, after a 401 from the asset host; only `asset.copyright` of drawn glTF, b3dm and i3dm tiles is counted. | Tokens are short-lived and the services document a 401 on expiry; the copyright of what is drawn is what the services ask to show. | An ion asset of an external type (Bing, Google 2D maps) is refused with code `unsupported`; nothing draws a logo for you. | Stream external imagery with its own provider; show `credits.html()` where a service needs its logo. |

## Entities

| Limit | Starts at | Why | What you see | Escape hatch |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bulk transform writes (`TransformWriter`, `@margoengine/scene/bulk`, 2026-11-05) | The mesh list is fixed when the writer is made. Measured diagnostic, 10,000 meshes, Node, CPU only, other jobs running: positions 0.12 ms against 0.59 with setters, positions and quaternions 0.84 against 1.60, positions and Euler 0.80 against 1.69. | One pass checks every value first, so a bad row changes nothing; rotations still cost a quaternion or Euler conversion per mesh, the same maths as the setters. | A mesh disposed since the writer was made throws `disposed`, even when another mesh took its slot. | Make a new writer when the list changes; `{ from, count }` writes part of a list. |
| Scene graph helpers (`@margoengine/scene`, 2026-11-05) | Each `getWorldPosition`, `getWorldQuaternion` and `worldMatrix` call walks up the parents, at most 64 levels. | They read the local values, so a result is right before any frame; `mesh.matrix` is the last frame's. | Joint limits apply at the next frame, so a value outside its limit counts as written until then. Under a rotated, unevenly scaled parent `getWorldQuaternion` and `getWorldScale` give the nearest values. | Read `mesh.matrix` in a per-frame loop over many meshes; `worldMatrix` stays exact under shear. |
| Scheduler (`@margoengine/ecs/schedule`) | Enforced: one fixed step size per `Schedule`, at most `maxSteps` (default 8) steps an update; time past that is dropped. Systems are synchronous and single threaded; the fixed phase always runs before the frame phase. | A cap stops a long stall from costing more steps than the next frame can pay for, which would snowball. | `update` returns the steps run and `schedule.dropped` holds the seconds lost. A frame system ordered before a fixed one throws MargoError `'state'`. | Raise `maxSteps`, or run a second `Schedule` for a second rate. |
| Change detection (`@margoengine/ecs/changes`) | Enforced by method: only `component.add`, `remove`, `touch` and `world.destroy` are seen. A write straight into `fields` is invisible until `touch` reports it, and a row counts as changed even when the value written is the same. 24 bytes per world slot per tracker. | `fields` are plain typed arrays, so loops over them stay at full speed; intercepting each write would cost that speed. | The entity is missing from `changed`. | Call `component.touch(entity)` after direct writes. |
| Query filters and tags (`@margoengine/ecs/query`, `world.tag`) | Enforced: `with` needs at least one term, and a `Query` cannot run inside its own `forEach`. Entities that start matching during `forEach` wait for the next call. A tag stores no fields but keeps the 8 bytes per world slot of its entity index. | The smallest `with` store is the list to walk, so there must be one. The match list is one buffer per query. | MargoError `'state'` or `TypeError`. | Use a second `Query` to nest. |
| Engine bridge (`@margoengine/ecs/engine`) | Enforced: one way, component to mesh, and one mesh per entity. Each group is whole or absent. Values pass through `TransformWriter` from `@margoengine/scene/bulk`, an optional peer, one write per changed group for each run of changed rows, so scattered changes cost a write each; `sync` costs one pass over the links even when nothing moved, as the setter loop did, and one pass to rebuild the writer after a link changes. Parents are not copied. | The setters' checks and the Euler view of `mesh.rotation` must hold, so the bridge cannot write the store columns blindly. | `TypeError`, `RangeError` or MargoError `'disposed'` from `link`, `sync` or the constructor. | Read a mesh back by hand when something else moves it. |
| Relationships (`@margoengine/ecs/relations`) | Enforced by method: pairs carry no data of their own and live in Maps and Sets, not typed arrays. `acyclic` pays a walk up from the target on every `add`. | Pairs change rarely next to component rows, and Sets keep both directions direct to read. | A loop on an `acyclic` relation throws `TypeError`. | Keep per-pair numbers in a component on the source. |
| Growable worlds (`@margoengine/ecs/growable`, accepted difference in speed, 2026-11-05) | Enforced: at most `limit` slots, 1,000,000 at most, and a slot retires after about 4,294,967,295 / `limit` reuses. Measured ([ecs-growable.mjs](../examples/package-benchmarks/ecs-growable.mjs), Node 26, three runs on a busy machine, diagnostic): a loop over 100,000 rows takes about twice as long as on a fixed `World`, ratio 2.02 to 2.09. | Ids are 32 bits and spaced by `limit`, so they survive growth. Resizable buffers keep every array reference valid while growing; plain arrays would be replaced, and a cached `fields.x` would silently write to the old copy. A fixed `World` pays nothing. | `create` throws `RangeError` at the limit. | Use a fixed `World` when a bound is known; lower `limit` for more reuses per slot. |
| Roll in `FlyControls` and `TrackballControls` (`enableRoll`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: off by default, so the horizon stays level. With it on, the control owns `camera.up`. Orbit, map, first-person, pointer-lock and walk controls do not roll. Not checked: an engine `CameraProjection` answers `camera.up` with its own `up` for an exactly vertical view, so a roll is not kept at that one orientation. | The level controls read their frame from world +Y; a roll would fight it. | Turning `enableRoll` off leaves the camera rolled, and `camera.up = [0, 1, 0]` throws at an exact vertical view. | Level the horizon with the view off the vertical, or use `ArcballControls`. |
| Gizmo occlusion (`occlude` on `TransformControls`, 2026-11-05) | By method: only the drawing is hidden, through the scene's depth buffer. Picking is on the CPU and has no scene depth, so a hidden handle can still be grabbed. | Reading depth back to the CPU would stall the frame on every pointer move. | The part of each arrow inside the object is hidden as well, and a handle behind a wall still takes the pointer. | Leave `occlude` off (the default), or filter grabs in the app. |
| Third-person camera (`ThirdPersonControls` in `@margoengine/controls/third-person`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: the arm is swept with nine rays a frame, not a true sphere, so an object thinner than the gaps between the rays can slip through the sweep. Skinned models collide in their bind pose, as for the bvh Raycaster. Not checked: the camera has no collider of its own, so it does not stop for something that moves into it from the side beyond the rim rays. | Nine rays against cached trees is cheap on the CPU and needs no physics package. | A pole near the camera may graze the view for a frame. | Raise `probeRadius`, or drive the camera from `@margoengine/physics` shape casts. |
| `DragControls` rotate mode, groups and children (2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: a turn goes about the grabbed object's origin, not the grab point or the group's centre. `recursive` and `transformGroup` follow core mesh children; a model is always dragged whole. | A model's nodes are not meshes the control can list. | A group with a far-off grabbed member swings wide. | Grab a member near the middle, or use `TransformControls`. |
| Orbit target radius and `orthoZoom` (2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: `minTargetRadius` and `maxTargetRadius` act on the target and move the camera by the same step, after `targetBounds`, so the sphere wins when the two disagree. A target exactly on `cursor` leaves along +X. `orthoZoom` needs a `CameraProjection` or a `projection` with a numeric `zoom`. | The shell has no way to slide past its centre, and a plain camera has no zoom. | With `minTargetRadius` above 0, the target cannot pass through `cursor`. | Set `minTargetRadius` to 0, or keep the shell thick. |
| Several objects on `TransformControls` (`attachMany`, `pivot`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: an object's size has only its own axes, so a non-uniform stretch along another frame gives each axis its share instead of a shear, exact when the axes line up or the factors match. A mesh with an attached ancestor follows that ancestor. | Meshes store position, rotation and scale, with no shear. | A turned object scaled unevenly in `world` space keeps its shape family, not the sheared shape a matrix would give. | Scale evenly, or scale in the object's own axes with `individual` and `local`. |
| Interactions (`@margoengine/input/interactions`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, dispatched events on a fake clock; no real gamepad has run it. By method: time is read once per update, so a rule is exact to one tick, and a 500 ms frame can turn a tap into a canceled tap. Rules do not arbitrate: a tap and a double press on one action both run. A gamepad press shorter than one update is unseen (gamepads are polled); keys, mouse and touch buttons keep it for one update. | Rules run on the sampled action, so they work on every device and on replayed input. Waiting for one rule to fail before another fires would add `gap` of delay to every press. | A single tap beside a double press fires on the first press too. | Use the double press's `canceled` as the single press. Run input on a fixed tick. |
| Local players (`@margoengine/input/players`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, dispatched events on a fake clock; no real gamepad has run it. By method: one mouse and one keyboard per browser, because the browser merges every keyboard and every mouse into one, so `'keyboard'` cannot tell two physical keyboards apart. A gamepad is known by its index: a pad that comes back on another index is a new device. Each player's `Input` holds its own listeners, 8 on the target per player. | The browser gives keyboard and mouse events no device id. The Gamepad API's `id` names a model, so two identical pads share it. | Two players on keyboards share one with `schemes`. A pad plugged back after another took its index goes to that index's owner. | Listen for `lost`, pause and use `assign` to pair the pad again. |
| Gyro aiming (`@margoengine/input/motion`, 2026-11-05) | Not measured on a real device: the sign and axis maps come from the Device Orientation specification and are tested with synthetic events. One event integrates 100 ms at most. Drift is not corrected after `calibrate`. Aim is in the screen's frame, so a device held at an angle turns the view about the screen's up, not the world's. Needs a secure page, and on iOS a tap. | No phone is in the test setup. A gap in sensor events must not jump the aim. | `available` stays false on a desktop. Browsers that break the specification's axis order would turn the wrong way. | Offer an invert option in your settings: `scale: -1` on the binding. |
| Input recording (`@margoengine/input/replay`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, dispatched events on a fake clock; no real gamepad has run it. By method: records what bindings gave, one value per tick, so a replay matches only if the game also steps the same way: same ticks, same `dt`, same random seed. Events between ticks are not stored, and raw positions from `Gestures` or `Pen` samples are not part of a clip. A recorder grows until you drop it: about 3 numbers per change and 1 per tick. | Input is recorded at the level the game reads, which is what makes a replay independent of devices. | A game that reads `performance.now` itself drifts from its recording. | Step the game on a fixed tick and take time from `replay.time`. |
| Pen input (`@margoengine/input/pen`, 2026-11-05) | Not measured with real hardware: tested with synthetic pointer events. Values are what the browser reports: a pen or driver without pressure gives 0.5 while touching, and without tilt gives 0 (upright). Action values are the latest reading, once per tick; only `take()` has the full rate. One pen at a time: two pens on one element share its readings. | The Pointer Events specification sets those fallback values, and a page cannot tell them from real readings. | A mouse-like stylus paints at an even half pressure. | Offer a fixed-size brush option. Paint from `take()`, not from action values. |
| `ArcballGizmo`, double-click focus and arcball keys (2026-11-05) | By method: a ring can be grabbed only with `autoHide: false`, by mouse, within 8 pixels of its near half; touch always turns freely. Focus moves the view centre and keeps the turn and distance; with no `pick` the point lies on the plane through the target. Touch has no double-tap gesture of its own: focus needs the browser's `dblclick`. | Picking the scene needs @margoengine/bvh, which `/arcball` does not load. | A double-click on a far object without `pick` centres the plane point, not the object. | Set `pick` with a `Raycaster` and `rayAt`, as the README shows. |
| Moving platforms for `WalkControls` (`carry`, 2026-11-05) | Proved on stand-in input, synthetic events on a fake element. By method: only the floor carries. A moving wall pushes the walker out but gives it no speed, a tilting floor turns the heading about +Y and leaves the pitch, and one more transform pass runs per update so colliders are this frame's. | A wall has no contact the walker keeps between frames, and a walker stays upright by design. | The pass costs a little on scenes with no moving floor. | `carry: false` skips the pass and keeps last frame's matrices. |
| Virtual camera shots (`@margoengine/cutscene/shots`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: shots may not overlap, `blendIn` is at most the shot's length, and the first shot cannot blend, as nothing comes before it. | A shot is a pure function of the time, so it has no memory of earlier frames. | By method: a shot has no memory, so `follow` and `lookAt` sit exactly on their object with no damping, and blending between cameras looking opposite ways turns about the world's up. A live camera is read at every evaluate, so a shot of one is the one thing here that is not a pure function of the time. Not measured: speed. | For damped follow in play, use `@margoengine/cinematic` and bring it in as a `live` camera. |
| Render to video (`@margoengine/cutscene/render`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: the engine must be on a `createHeadlessCanvas` canvas, and a span must lie inside the cutscene. Needs WebCodecs, so a browser or a worker, not Node. Not proven: no player outside the browser has opened a file; the encoder delay of AAC in MP4 is cut by an edit list and Opus states its own pre-skip, so a player that ignores them plays the delay. Not measured: speed. | Rendering needs WebCodecs, and the whole file and the whole mix sit in memory. | No player outside the browser has opened a file, so how another player shows the start of the sound is not checked. | Run it in a browser or worker. In Node, pass your own WebCodecs implementation as the `webCodecs` option, as for `encodeVideo`. |
| Take recorder (`@margoengine/cutscene/record`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: capture times may not go backwards, samples must be finite, and a recorder is spent after `stop()`. | A recorder keeps what the source gave at each `capture()`. | By method: a sample is what the source gave at `capture()`, so what happened between ticks is lost; positions come from the source as given, so a parented `Mesh` records its local transform; `Camera.up` is not recorded, roll is; thinning is checked on the straight line between kept keys, so a track played with an ease or a curved path would differ. | Lip sync (`@margoengine/cutscene/lipsync`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: phonemes must be known ARPAbet or viseme names, inside their clip and sorted; `sampleRate` must be 8,000 or more. | By method: `analyseSpeech` reads level and formants, not words, so it tells closed, hiss and open apart but no other consonants; one clean voice at a time; about half of vowel frames right on two sentences of one speaker, and those two set its thresholds, so other voices and noisy audio are not measured. How each viseme maps to VRM and ARKit shapes is Margo's own choice. Not measured: speed. | It tells closed, hiss and open apart but no other consonants, and about half of vowel frames come out right. | Use phonemes from a forced aligner when the mouth must be exact; the track takes timed ARPAbet or viseme phonemes. |
| VFX and activation tracks (`@margoengine/cutscene/vfx`, 2026-11-06) | By method: a seek replays drawn frames, one for each step, so it costs that many frames of GPU time; the replay is capped at `longest` (default 10 s), and past that it starts that far back, so the particles are alike in kind only. It lands exactly only with frames of `stepSize`; other frame lengths land near. Playing backward is a seek every frame. A pause does not hold particles while you keep drawing. Effects read at the top timeline's time, so a nested timeline with `speed` replays on that time. Not proven: `VFX.reset()` on the WebGL2 route. Not measured: speed of a seek. | A seek replays drawn frames, one for each step, so the GPU must draw that many frames. | Past `longest` the particles are alike in kind only, and a pause does not hold particles while you keep drawing. | Set `stepSize` to your frame time to land on the same particles, raise `longest` to cover the effects' lifetimes, and stop drawing or pass `delta` 0 to hold particles in a pause. |
| Timeline editor (`@margoengine/cutscene/editor`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: every edit must leave a valid document, or it is refused whole. Needs a DOM for the panel; `TimelineModel` does not. | The editor changes a document that is frozen, so every edit makes a new one. | By method: top-level tracks only, a nested timeline moves as one clip; no curve editor, and clip lengths and key values change through `setItem`, not by dragging; undo keeps 200 steps as whole copies of the document, so a very large document costs memory; a change makes a new document, as a `Cutscene` is frozen, so the player is rebuilt by the caller. | Editing other packages' objects (`@margoengine/inspector/packages`, 2026-11-08) | Enforced: each number within its documented range (light intensity, range and decay 0 or more, cone angles 0 to pi / 2, Bloom strength 0 to 4, threshold 0 to 100, exposure -10 to 10, radius 0 to 1, shadow lambda 0 to 1, material factors 0 to 1), a value of the property's kind, no edit of a read-only object. By method: objects are found by shape: a light outside a `clustered*` plugin's `list`, a material that is not a gltf-pro PBR record and a plugin without `options` or settable accessors are not listed unless passed in `objects`; only numbers, switches and 3-vectors are edited (no textures, strings, enums or alpha mode). | The editor imports none of the packages it edits, so it relies on their documented shapes. | A glTF material whose `@margoengine/materials` shading reports a version is shown read only; an option a package reads only when it attaches keeps its old value until it is attached again. | Pass the object in `objects` (a materials `Material`, app settings), or edit it in code. |
| Plugin and pass view (`FrameReport.plugins`, `@margoengine/inspector/frame`, 2026-11-08) | By method: only `onPreUpdate` is timed (setup, pass and pick time are not; a plugin's passes are timed in `graph`), as CPU time with `performance.now`; a frozen plugin is listed but not timed; plugins and scenes added during the frame are not listed. Needs WebGPU, like every capture. LIM-570 | The debugger wraps a plugin's hook only for the captured frame, so a frame not captured pays nothing. | A frozen plugin reads null. | Time the work inside the plugin, or do not freeze it while profiling. |
| Camera brain (`@margoengine/cinematic/brain`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: names in one brain differ, a blend's `duration` is 0 or more, priorities are finite. By method: every enabled camera runs every frame, so the cost grows with the camera count; disable the ones not in use. A blend is linear in position, field of view and roll, with the view turned on the shortest arc, so `'direct'` can pass through a subject (use `'orbit'`). An `ease` is clamped to 0..1, so no overshoot. Blend rules match by name. Not measured: no timed number. | Every enabled camera runs every frame, and a blend is linear in position, field of view and roll. | `'direct'` can pass through a subject, and an `ease` is clamped to 0..1, so no overshoot. | Disable the cameras not in use, and use `'orbit'` instead of `'direct'`. LIM-304 |
| Framing composer (`@margoengine/cinematic/composer`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `softZone` at least `deadZone`, `screen` within 0..1. | The composer turns the camera and never moves it. | By method: it turns the camera and never moves it, so a subject past what a turn can reach (straight overhead) stops 0.001 radians short of the pole. It ignores `pose.roll`. Lookahead is a smoothed velocity times a time, so it trails a change of direction and overshoots a stop. Not measured: no timed number. | Put a stage that moves the camera before the composer, and leave `lookahead` at 0, its default, to avoid trailing and overshoot. |
| Dolly tracks (`@margoengine/cinematic/dolly`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: a track of some length, `damping` above 0, `window` above 0. | By method: the cart goes to the nearest point of the track, so a track that passes close to itself needs a `window`, and a subject that leaves the window drags the cart to the window's edge. Stand-in: the proof tracks are generated curves, not a path from a shipped level. Not measured: no timed number. LIM-308 | The windowed search takes 33 samples and refines the best, so a window holding two close passes finds one. | Give the track a `window` when it passes close to itself. |
| Group framing (`@margoengine/cinematic/group`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `padding` below 0.5, distance and field of view limits in order. By method: the fit is exact for the current members, but the shot eases at `damping`, so a member that bolts is outside the padding until the shot catches up. A member's `weight` moves the centre and does not change the fit; weight 0 drops it at once, with a step the damping smooths. `fits` turns false when the limits stop the shot. Not measured: no timed number. | LIM-309. The fit is exact for the current members, but the shot eases at `damping`. | A member that bolts is outside the padding until the shot catches up, and `fits` turns false when the limits stop the shot. | Raise `damping` (default 4) so the shot catches up faster, widen `padding` (default 0.1), or widen `minDistance`, `maxDistance`, `minFov` and `maxFov` so `fits` stays true. |

| Confiner volumes (`@margoengine/cinematic/confine`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: a box with its low sides below its high sides after margins, an outline of at least 3 points with some area. The nearest clear point can change suddenly in a concave area, so a camera outside it can step across a corner. A gap narrower than two margins has no clear spot, and the camera ends on a wall. A self-crossing outline is not detected. It holds position only: a margin smaller than the camera's near plane lets the view clip a wall. Stand-in: tested on generated outlines, not a shipped level. Not measured: no timed number. | The confiner holds position only, and an area lies on world x and z. | The nearest clear point can change suddenly in a concave area, so a camera outside it can step across a corner. | Set `margin` above the camera's near plane so the view does not clip a wall; for walls between the camera and its subject use the `collide` option of `Cinematic`. LIM-321 | Events waiting when the regions stop, or when a hook throws, are dropped. | From a region's own hook, use `raise` and `forward` in place of `send` and `stop`. |

Stand-in: the proof charts are generated, not from a shipped game. Not measured: no timed number. | LIM-322. (LIM-323) | Delays count the sum of `delta` given to `update`. | By method: time is the sum of `delta`, so a paused game pauses delays; a declined guard is asked again on each update, not once as a delayed event would be; a hook that changes state inside a split `delta` ends that piece; a delay shorter than the rounding of `clock` (about 1e-16 of it) does not count. Stand-in: the proof charts are generated, not from a shipped game. | Jump links (`@margoengine/navmesh/links`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: radius, height, `maxFall`, `spacing`, each jump's height and distance finite and above 0; `climb` and `jumpCost` finite and 0 or more; `directions` 1 to 64. By method: takeoffs are sampled every `spacing` on mesh edges with no ground beyond, and a quarter and a half of the jump's distance back from them, in a fan of 160 degrees around the way out, so a gap only a narrower fan or a finer spacing would find can be missed; links are one way and point to point, thinned to the cheapest per `spacing` cell, landing polygon and kind; the body is tested at steps of a quarter of its width or a sixteenth of the jump (the smaller), so a solid thinner than a step between two tests is caught only by the radius; for two body widths at each end the body's lowest `climb` is ignored, so a steep slope there can read clear of a low solid; obstacle boxes of a `TiledNavMesh` are not seen, so pass solids as triangles; links do not follow level changes: run it again. | Takeoffs are sampled in a fan and the body is tested at fixed steps, so a thin gap or solid can be missed. | A gap only a narrower fan or a finer spacing would find can be missed, and links do not follow level changes. | Lower `spacing` (default 1) and raise `directions` (default 9, up to 64) to search finer; run it again after a level change, and pass solids as triangles. |
| Hierarchical routes (`@margoengine/navmesh/hierarchy`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `clusterTiles` a whole number of 1 or more, `spacing` finite and above 0. | A hierarchy plans over clusters for the one filter it was made with. | By method: routes are near the shortest, not always the shortest, and on generated rooms measured 0.99 of the exact route's length on average and 1.09 at worst (the exact search is a search over polygons, not the true shortest, so it can lose); a hierarchy plans for the one filter it was made with; an agent standing on ground its filter forbids finds no way out across a cluster line, where the exact search walks it out; the two ends search their clusters at each query, so a query's cost grows with cluster size; memory holds a corridor per pair of transitions in a cluster. Diagnostic, measured with other lanes running: 3.1 ms exact against 0.22 ms on a generated 1 km world of 39,698 polygons (`clusterTiles` 2). Quiet run still to come. | Use `world.route` when you need the search over every polygon, make one hierarchy per agent type with `filter`, and use a smaller `spacing` for straighter routes. |
| Debug draw (`@margoengine/navmesh/debug`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `lift` finite. | By method: lines are made on the CPU, the mesh's only when `version` moves and the crowd's on every `update`, so a very big mesh or crowd costs a frame there; off-mesh links draw as an arc of 8 segments that shows the link, not the jump's own arc; the entry imports `@margoengine/lines`, an optional peer; the rest of the package does not. Not yet looked at on a real screen. | A very big mesh or crowd costs a frame in the debug lines, and off-mesh links show the link, not the jump's own arc. | Draw part of it: `showRoute(route)` shows one route, and `crowdLines` takes an array of agents. |
| Voxel navmesh (`@margoengine/navmesh/voxel`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `cs` and `ch` divide a block; a world with nothing walkable throws unless `deferred`. By method: every solid block counts, `solid` chooses others; slabs and stairs count as their half blocks, so a cell of 0.5 sees them and a cell of 1 reads the corner half of a block; cross-shaped plants and liquids are not ground by default; unloaded chunks are air, so the mesh ends at them; heights are flat tops, so `detailSampleDist` has no use here; `update` must be called, as on `./tiles`. A tile reads the columns it sees block by block; speed on real worlds is unmeasured. The entry imports `@margoengine/voxel`, an optional peer. | Every solid block counts, slabs and stairs count as their half blocks, and unloaded chunks are air. | A cell of 1 reads the corner half of a block, and the mesh ends at unloaded chunks. | `solid` chooses other blocks as ground, and a cell of 0.5 sees slabs and stairs. |
| Flying navigation (`@margoengine/navmesh/volume`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `voxelSize` above 0, `radius` and `padding` 0 or more, min below max, 65,536 voxels a side, 4,000,000 leaves. | A voxel counts as solid when any point in it could be nearer than `radius` to a triangle. | By method: a voxel is solid when any point in it could be nearer than `radius` to a triangle, so a gap must be about a voxel's diagonal wider than the body on each side to read open; the inside of a closed solid is a sealed pocket, not solid, so a start in it stays in it; the volume is a cube rounded up to a power of two, so free space runs past `max`, and a route may use it; it is built once and does not change; build time grows with each triangle's bounding box, a long diagonal triangle being the worst case; routes are short, not proven shortest. Speed unmeasured. | Use a smaller `voxelSize` so narrower gaps read open, move an end out of a solid with `closestFree(point)`, and make a new volume when the level changes. |
| Perception (`@margoengine/behavior/perception`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: sight `range` above 0, cone `fov` above 0 and up to a full turn, coffin `shoulder` inside `range`, `suspiciousAt` above 0 and below 1, sound `priority` 0 to 1, target `visibility` above 0. By method: a sight shape tests the target's one `position`, and only line of sight uses its `points`; a shape's edge is hard, 1 cm outside is never seen; time comes only from the `delta` passed in; without `lineOfSight` nothing blocks sight and without `soundDistance` sound goes through walls; `predict` is a straight line and knows no walls; memories are not saved, only what reached the blackboard is; `OccupancyMap.nearest` looks at every node, so `observe` costs a pass over the map; `OccupancyMap.grid` has four neighbours a cell, the paper's has six; `cull` trusts the `visible` it is given, and calling a place visible when it is not rules out the true place. Light as a sense, the paper's room graph and social awareness are not modelled. Not measured on a real level or at a real crowd size. | Sight and sound are tested against shapes and against the functions you pass, not modelled in full. | Without `lineOfSight` nothing blocks sight, and without `soundDistance` sound goes through walls. | Pass `lineOfSight` and `soundDistance`. |
| Environment queries (`@margoengine/behavior/eqs`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: a condition sets `min`, `max` or both, weights are finite, criterion `limits` run low to high, values are finite, `count` is 1 or more, candidates have three finite numbers. | A query is evaluated in one call over the candidates and conditions given. | By method: a query runs in one call and cannot be spread over frames; `count` above 1 evaluates more points fully; `gridAround` and `ringsAround` are flat at the centre's height, and neither knows the navmesh, so a point may be off it unless a condition says so; `distance` is straight, and a walk needs your own criterion; `reachBefore` assumes equal speeds and straight lines; `coverFrom` trusts `facing` and casts no ray; `explainQuery` runs everything. Not measured on a real level or at a real crowd size. | Mark slow criteria `expensive: true` so they run only on the point that can still win, pass fewer candidates, and keep `count` at 1. |
| Goal planner (`@margoengine/behavior/goap`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: symbols are booleans, strings or finite numbers and none is called `__proto__`, action names are unique, costs are above 0, an action has an effect, `maxNodes` is 1 or more. | By method: one value per symbol, with no variables, sums or ranges, so a count must be written as symbols; a search past `maxNodes` (10,000) returns `null` the same as no plan; `available` runs once per plan on the state at planning time; a running action is not rechecked against its preconditions, so `run` must return `'failure'` itself; a goal that failed to plan is not retried until the state changes, so a symbol that changes every tick makes it plan every tick; an action that fails without writing why is planned again; one agent at a time, no squads, no scheduling of actions. Not measured on a real game domain. | A search past `maxNodes` returns `null` the same as no plan, and a symbol that changes every tick makes a goal plan every tick. | Raise `maxNodes` (default 10,000) for a bigger search, write a count as symbols, and have `run` return `'failure'` itself. |
| Smart objects (`@margoengine/behavior/smart-objects`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: object ids are unique, use names are unique on an object, a use with `slots` has one, `attenuation` is 0 or more, needs and advertised amounts are finite. By method: `distance` is straight, not a walk; `offers` looks at every object, with no spatial index; a claim holds its place until released, so an agent that dies must release it; `run` is called wherever the agent is, and walking there is yours to do; `available` is checked when offered and when claimed, not while a claim runs; the object's `state` and an agent's `needs` are not saved. Not measured on a real level or at a real crowd size. | `distance` is straight, `offers` looks at every object, and `run` is called wherever the agent is. | A claim holds its place until released, and `available` is not checked while a claim runs. | Call `claim.release()` (or use `using`) when an agent dies. Pass `radius` to `offers` to cut the list to nearby objects, and walk to `claim.position()` yourself. |
| Flow fields (`@margoengine/behavior/flowfield`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: size and stamp sizes are whole numbers of 1 or more, `cell` above 0, costs whole numbers from 1 to 255, goals three finite numbers, `steer` count, speed and delta 0 or more. | The field is one flat grid built in one call, so cost follows the whole map. | By method: one flat grid, with no sectors, portals or tile cache, so memory and build time grow with the whole map; one field per goal and movement type; every change rebuilds the whole field in one synchronous call; no clearance, so a gap of one cell passes a unit of any size; the ground is flat, and height is ignored; line of sight flags are built for a single goal only and can miss a few cells at the edge of a shadow; first order fast marching reads over the true distance on the diagonal, by 21% one cell from the goal, under 11% past 5 cells and under 3% past 40; units do not avoid each other. Cost is per cell, so `integration` is in cells, not world units. Stand-in proof: generated grids only, and no build or steer time measured. | Build the field over only the area units use (`min` and `size`), and use a larger `cell` where a coarser grid will do. |
| Task network planner (`@margoengine/behavior/htn`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: task names are unique, a compound task has a method, every subtask exists, symbols are booleans, strings or finite numbers and none is called `__proto__`, `maxSteps` is 1 or more. By method: total order only, with no parallel or partly ordered tasks; no costs, so the plan is the first by method order, not the cheapest; tasks take no parameters, so `NavigateTo(x)` needs a task per place or a symbol; a recursion that never ends returns `null` at `maxSteps` (10,000), the same as no plan; function conditions and effects must not touch anything but the state given, or backtracking cannot undo them; a sensor change is found by comparing the state as JSON once per update; a running task's own preconditions are not rechecked; no automatic split into partial plans. Not measured on a real game domain. | The planner is total order only, with no costs and no task parameters. | A recursion that never ends returns `null` at `maxSteps` (10,000), the same as no plan. | Use a task per place or a symbol for something like `NavigateTo(x)`. |
| Context steering (`@margoengine/behavior/context`, 2026-11-07) | Enforced: `resolution` is a whole number of 4 or more, `blur` a whole number of 0 or more, `memory` 0 or more and below 1, `slack` 0 or more, `chase` `range` above 0 and `spread` 0 or more, `avoid` `clearance` above 0 and `radius` 0 or more. | A behaviour sees only what it is given and steers locally. | By method: headings lie on the ground plane; the racing lane mode of the chapter is left out; a behaviour sees only what it is given, with no navmesh or walls, so it steers locally and can sit in a dead end; a gap narrower than a slot can be missed at a low `resolution`; `strength` caps at 1; `memory` is a plain blend, so two pulls that swap places every update with equal averages still swap, by a smaller margin. Not measured on a real level or at a real crowd size. | Raise `resolution` so a narrow gap is not missed. For dead ends, add a behaviour of your own (a `ContextBehavior`) that writes the direction of a `FlowField` or a path into `interest`. |
| View cube drag to orbit (`drag` on `ViewCube`, 2026-11-08) | Proved with real mouse events in Chromium on a Radeon 680M (LIM-579). By method: turntable azimuth and elevation, two radians per cube width; elevation stops at the poles. | The angles are measured from where the press went down, so the pointer always maps to the same view; a wheel zoom during a drag keeps its new distance. | Under 3 px of movement a press is a click; a drag stops wherever it is released, not on a named view. | `cube.drag = false`; click a face, edge or corner for an exact view. |
| View cube home view (`home` and `setHome` on `ViewCube`, 2026-11-08) | Proved with a real mouse click in Chromium on a Radeon 680M (LIM-580). Enforced: a home is a position and a target, three finite numbers each and apart. | The target and distance move with the eased view angles in one animation; any other camera move during it cancels it. | Only position and target are kept: the field of view, the projection and any zoom of an orthographic camera stay as they are. | Set the projection and field of view yourself after `cube.home()`, or call `camera.fitToBoundingBox` to frame a model. |
| View cube roll (`roll` on `ViewCube`, 2026-11-08) | Proved with a real mouse click in Chromium on a Radeon 680M (LIM-584). Enforced: `turns` is a finite number. | The cube writes `camera.up` every frame while rolled, so a control that sets `camera.up` itself takes over and ends the roll. | Rest positions are whole quarter turns; a fractional `roll` lands on the nearest. Any view change from the cube is upright. | Set `camera.up` yourself for a free roll angle. |
| View cube themes (`theme` and `setTheme` on `ViewCube`, 2026-11-08) | Proved by computed WCAG 2 contrast of every built-in pair over a black and a white page, and computed styles in Chromium on a Radeon 680M (LIM-585). Enforced: a theme is a built-in name, `'auto'` or tokens that are strings. | Tokens are plain CSS colour strings and are not parsed; a bad colour falls back as CSS does. | The contrast of your own tokens is not checked; `'auto'` follows the browser's settings only, not the page's. | Start from a `THEMES` set and change single tokens, and check your colours with a WCAG contrast checker. |
| Axis triad (`AxisTriad` in `@margoengine/viewcube/axes`, 2026-11-08) | Proved with real mouse events in Chromium on a Radeon 680M (LIM-588). By method: each ball is placed by the camera's view rotation in 2D, ordered by depth with `z-index`. | Only the six axis views are clickable (no edges or corners); the cube's arrow keys and Home still work. | A ball straight toward the viewer covers its stick and the centre; balls of axes seen end-on overlap. | Use `ViewCube` for edge and corner views; drag or roll to separate overlapping balls. |

## Copies of a model

| Limit | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Textures of `cloneModel` copies | Shared: one GPU texture per device, image and format, freed with its last holder (`p-morph`: 7 textures for 100 copies, not 700). A second model loaded from the same file holds its own images, so it uploads its own textures. |
| Geometry of `cloneModel` copies (2026-11-06) | Shared: vertex and index buffers per device, asset and array, and in the deformation arena one copy of the bind-pose vertices, morph deltas and skin influences per page. Each animated copy still adds 80 B per vertex of deformed output, 64 B per bone and its morph weights, because those are what differ between copies. A second model loaded from the same file packs and uploads its own. |
| GPU morph weights (`@margoengine/animation/morph-gpu`, 2026-11-06) | Not measured for speed yet: the 16 B a frame and one dispatch per page are counted, the frame time is not. `Animation.upload` still loops over each copy to check its weights and bones, so CPU work is smaller per copy, not zero. Time is float32 in the shader: steps of about 0.24 ms after an hour of play. Copies must share one asset. Driven weights ignore `setMorph`, and `primitive.weights` on the CPU does not follow them. Why: The tracks are shared, and reading weights back would stall the frame. What you see: A long-running crowd loses fine timing slowly. CPU code that reads `primitive.weights` sees old values. Escape hatch: Set `player.time` back by whole loops of the clip (exact for copies at `speed` 1; a copy at another speed samples `time * speed`, so it jumps unless the step is whole loops at its speed too). Use `Animation` for a character whose weights you read or set by hand. |
| Animation LOD (`@margoengine/animation/lod`, 2026-11-06) | CPU only, and not measured for speed yet: how many copies update is counted, the frame time is not. Every copy still goes through the GPU deform dispatch each frame, and each still draws. Visibility is a sphere at the model root against the view, with no occlusion. `depth` counts node levels from the scene roots, so its meaning depends on the rig. A tier with a depth does not play `KHR_animation_pointer` channels. Deeper bones hold, so a far character can show a slightly wrong pose until it comes closer. Why: A skipped pose must still draw, and the deformed output is what draws. What you see: A crowd limited by the GPU gains little. A copy far larger than its `radius` can stop updating while part of it shows. Escape hatch: Set `radius` to cover the character. Use `VATCrowd` when the GPU is the limit. |
| Clip editing (`@margoengine/animation/clip-utils`, 2026-11-06) | By method: an eased or cubic segment that a cut passes through becomes straight keys at `fps` (default 60), so it follows the curve only to that step; segments kept whole stay exact. Works on `KeyframeClip`s, not glTF clips. `makeAdditive` leaves property tracks out, and `applyAdditiveClip` poses a `Skeleton` only, without morph weights. Why: An eased curve is defined over its whole segment, so one key cannot carry part of it. What you see: Very fine motion inside a cut curve can flatten between samples. Escape hatch: Pass a higher `fps`. For a glTF clip use an `@margoengine/animator` additive layer. |
| Engine ticks per `cloneModel` copy (2026-11-06) | None when the copy can take its source's static batches, skinned models included, so a crowd no longer costs a tick of every mesh per copy. One when the source moved since baking, has a parent, or had its geometry rewritten: the copy bakes for itself. A copy's world matrices publish with the next tick; a direct `node.matrix` read before it sees the old value. The time saved is not measured yet. |
| Dual quaternion skinning (`@margoengine/animation/dqs`, 2026-11-06) | Per `Animation`, fixed when it is created. `Skin` on raw data, `bakeVAT` and bone-texture crowds stay linear. Stretch blends linearly about the mesh origin, so between bones of different scale the result differs from a linear blend. Each bone costs a polar decomposition on the CPU every frame. Frame time is not measured yet. |
| Bone-texture crowds (`@margoengine/animation/instanced-skin`, 2026-11-06) | One material, opaque, no CPU culling, like `VATCrowd`. Morph targets are frozen at bake time, and bones blend linearly. Two clips a member: more needs another bake. Texture rows cap at 8,192 of 2,048 texels, about 5.5 million bone frames. Each vertex reads up to 96 texels when two clips blend across eight influences (24 for one clip and four). Frame time against `VATCrowd` and `Animation` is not measured yet. |
| Geometry caches (`@margoengine/animation/geometry-cache`, `/point-cache`, 2026-11-06) | PC2 and MDD are read, proved on files Blender's published add-ons wrote; the triangles come from the caller, as neither format holds them. Alembic is not read: the format has no published specification, and Margo is built from specs and papers. 3 to 1,000,000 vertices a chunk, one mesh, one material. The CPU interpolates and writes the whole mesh to the GPU every changed frame, so upload bandwidth is the ceiling: 80 bytes a vertex. Vertices are uncompressed. A seek back on a URL rereads from the start of the file, and a loop rereads it every lap unless it is one chunk: no range requests and no index of chunks. A stream source keeps every chunk it read. No mix across a change of mesh: the last frame holds. Motion vectors, frame time and upload cost are not measured yet. |
| Inertialization (`@margoengine/animator/inertialize`, 2026-11-06) | By method: an offset fades along one line per bone, so speed sideways to the offset is dropped at the switch; the new pose shows from the second frame after the switch. Not a published speed: diagnostic, shared machine, 58 bones, 14.5 to 17.1 us a frame during a fade against 16.5 to 21.5 for a crossfade, and about the same once idle. |
| Sync groups (`@margoengine/animator/sync`, 2026-11-06) | By design: clips are looping cycles, every clip of a marker group has the same markers in the same order, and a state is in one group. A crossfade keeps the old state in phase at the new state's rate. Not measured for speed. |
| Warping (`@margoengine/animator/warping`, 2026-11-06) | By method: travel past `maxTurn` (default 90 degrees) needs a clip for that direction, and a clip cannot play backward, so feet slide on the part of a turn past the limit. Stride scales about the hips on flat ground; it does not follow slopes, for which `addFootPlacement` runs after it. The clip rate changes one frame after the speed does. A pelvis drop past `maxPelvisDrop` leaves the foot short. On real CMU walks a planted foot slides 24 to 50 mm at 0.7 to 3 m/s against 21 mm in the take itself: the take's own slide scales with the stride and the hips' surge is not scaled away. The surge stays on its recorded axis when the hips turn, so a walk 90 degrees off the facing slides 51 mm. A take that curves is warped along the requested straight line, 62 mm on subject 2 at 1.6 m/s. Not measured for speed. |
| Full-body IK (`@margoengine/animator/fbik`, 2026-11-06) | By method: the torso is solved from the animated pose every frame in up to 12 steps, with no memory of the last frame, so a set of targets that needs more steps stops short and says so in `result`. Limbs are three-joint chains; hands, feet and head are the only targets, and a spine joint turns at most `maxBend` from the animated pose. Balance is static (centre of mass over the support hull, about world +Y, from fixed segment shares): it knows nothing of momentum or of the model's real masses. A pinned foot on a limb with no target holds its animated place, so a walking clip keeps its steps. Speed not yet measured. |
| Motion matching (`@margoengine/animator/motion-matching`, 2026-11-06) | By method: the character does what its clips show, so a request with no matching clip plays the nearest row; the weights are defaults made for loops with no start or stop clips, so a real set may want its own. The root is the hips' smoothed ground path about +Y, with a yaw-only character; ground slope is left to foot placement. Scale tracks on the hips' ancestors are read at build but not in the running pose step. Proved on stand-in clips, on Fox.glb's real clips with hand-labelled speeds, and on eight CMU takes (769 rows). A row whose path runs past the end of its take cannot be landed on, so a take has to outlast `trajectory` by a good margin: a 1.1 s run jumps 19 times in 4 s, a 3.5 s walk 4. Foot slide at a jog and a run is not measured. Speed not yet measured on a large table. |
| Runtime mirroring (`@margoengine/animator/mirror`, 2026-11-06) | By method: partners need the same place in the tree on both sides, or `addMirror` refuses them. A rig whose rest pose is not symmetric keeps its own proportions, so the mirrored pose lands that far from an exact reflection. Scale tracks trade between partners as they are. Switching `enabled` is a hard cut. Speed not yet measured. |
| Spline IK (`@margoengine/animator/spline-ik`, 2026-11-06) | By method: bones are chords, so a bend tighter than a bone is cut across, and a chain longer than the curve carries on straight past its end. The curve's frames are rebuilt every frame, and a curve of points is rebuilt too, so a very long chain costs more than one on a fixed `Spline`. `space` needs a uniform scale. Speed not yet measured. |
| Spring bones (`@margoengine/rigging/springs`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: 256 joints a chain, a joint in one chain only, 8 steps a frame (longer frames slow the swing), jiggle up to 15 Hz. By method: a tail can pass through a collider it crosses within one step, as in the specification; after the push the length hold can leave up to 1 mm of overlap; colliders hold their pose for the frame during a long frame's steps; spheres and capsules only, no planes or inside shapes; a chain has no twist, each bone turns by the shortest arc; strips do not pull on their neighbours, so a skirt of chains can part. 144 Hz differs from 60 Hz by up to 2% of the chain's length. Speed not yet measured. |
| VRM avatars (`@margoengine/rigging/vrm`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: VRM 1.0 only; VRM 0.x files are refused with a coded error. By method: MToon draws as its glTF fallback, and a colour bind only MToon has is listed in `unsupported` and not shown; `VRMC_node_constraint` is kept on write and does not run; `firstPerson` is data only; colour binds are checked by value, not in a picture. Not proven: no outside VRM tool has read a file `writeVRM` made (stand-in: read back by Margo only). Not measured: speed. |
| Uneven bone scales in IK (`@margoengine/rigging/ik`, `@margoengine/animator`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: flat and mirrored bones are refused. By method: any positive scale is taken, and an uneven scale stretches the bones under it as they turn, so a chain lands as close as that stretch allows (within 0.14% of its length at 0.9995, 1, 0.9986). A `CCDSolver` keeps one scale per joint, so `addCCDChain` uses the mean of the three, and `setParentMatrix` still takes uniform scales only. |
| Voxel weights (`@margoengine/rigging/voxel-weights`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: resolution 8 to 512, closing 0 to 8. By method: parts closer than about one voxel join, so a finer grid is what keeps fingers apart, and memory grows with the cube of `resolution` (about 20 bytes a voxel). An open mesh is filled only from a bone inside it, through voxels with surface on four of six directions. A part no bone reaches takes straight-line distances, counted in `unreached`. Authored weights are the yardstick by strongest bone, not by value: the mean row gap to the authored weights is 0.30 to 0.54, as bone heat's is 0.32 to 0.43. Not measured: speed in a quiet run (Seed-san's hair mesh took about 3 s at resolution 64 and 33 s at 128 while other lanes ran). |
| Automatic rig (`@margoengine/rigging/autorig`, 2026-11-06) | Enforced: resolution 32 to 384, closing 0 to 8. By method: one humanoid standing in a T or A pose, arms clear of the body, two separate feet at the lowest point of the mesh, so a figure on a plinth, with its feet joined, or in another pose fails with code `failed` or gets a wrong skeleton. Fingers are not placed; the hand is one bone. Joints go by body proportion along the solid's paths, so a joint sits 2 to 4 percent of the height from where people put it (LIM-282), and more on a body far from adult proportions. A skirt or coat that joins the legs on a figure of over 4.5 heads puts the hips by proportion, not by what the mesh shows. Facing comes from the feet; on feet with no clear front +z is assumed and `facingFound` is false. A figure turned more than two degrees against the grid is voxelized again square on, which costs a second pass; a turned figure's joints still move by up to 0.03 of the height, a voxel or two. Bone rotations are all zero: the pose carries offsets only. Stand-in: the refused box and the README's figure of boxes are written by hand. Run only by hand and not committed, as their licences do not allow it: two more unrigged figures, which gave plausible skeletons with nothing to mark them against.
