A face that crosses itself stays a fan, as it has no inside to clip. Measured against Blender 5.3: 862 of 862 real meshes within 4.58e-5 of their span at level 2; 346 of 348 stand-in runs of face-varying values at levels 1 and 2. | Refinement runs on the CPU at load. | `triangleSubdivisionRule = "smooth"` refines by the standard rule, with an issue. A value shared by faces that touch only at a point counts as one value: in mode `none` it stays sharp, and Blender gives one such value sharp and the other split, so those two runs differ (10 corners at level 1, up to 2.5e-2). Blender splits a value where faces turn opposite ways in UV space, and this entry keeps it one value, as the file has it: of 109 real meshes with mirrored or zero-area UV faces 41 match Blender and 68 differ, by up to 3.13e-1 near the folds. On the washer of usd-wg the split alone explains the whole difference; on meshes with zero-area UV faces the rest is not attributed. Time-sampled `points` refine at the first time only. Level 3 of a large stage is slow and large: each level gives four times the faces. | Pass a lower `level` to `subdivision()` (default 2), or a lower `maxTriangles` for the stage. |
| USD lights and cameras (`@margoengine/usdz/lux`, 2026-11-07) | By method: every area light becomes a punctual one, with no size in the shading; a light's or camera's values are read at the stage's first time, though its transform plays with `/anim`. Measured against OpenUSD 26.08 on 55 real stages: 46 of 46 visible lights, 44 of 44 cameras, within 7e-8. | Core has one directional light, so `applyGLTFLights` uses the first sun. Light strength has no outside check: OpenUSD gives no such number. | `DomeLight` is data through `domeLight()`, with an issue, and needs an `Environment`. `GeometryLight`, `PortalLight`, light linking, shadow settings, `diffuse` and `specular` multipliers, `shaping:ies` and `inputs:texture:file` on rect lights are not read. Aperture offsets, `stereoRole`, depth of field and shutter are not read. Below 2000 K colour temperature differs from OpenUSD, up to 0.29 in red at 1000 K. | Read a `DomeLight` with `domeLight()` and light the scene with an `Environment`. |
| USD reset transform stacks (`@margoengine/usdz`, 2026-11-07) | Measured against OpenUSD 26.08 on one stand-in stage it wrote itself: 24 of 24 prims, 15 resetting, at 9 times, within 1e-4 on time codes. No real file: none of the 546 here resets (LIM-324). | A node that resets hangs from the stage's root node, so walking node names does not give its path. Visibility of the prims above it reaches it only through `/anim`, which merges their samples into the node's own. | `node.extras.usdPath` holds the prim's path. | Read `USDAsset.meshes[].path` or `node.extras.usdPath` in place of walking names. |
| MaterialX in USD (`@margoengine/usdz/materialx`, 2026-11-07) | 39 real files, 58 of 58 materials read as OpenUSD 26.08 reads them; 30 of 31 materials of the `.mtlx` files compile on the GPU (LIM-331). By method for looks: no renderer oracle. | A glTF material holds less than these shaders. `uvoffset` and the real world sizes of `tiledimage` are listed and not applied: the Standard Nodes document names them with no formula. `emission_luminance` over 1000 is Margo's convention, from OpenPBR's soft range of 0 to 1000 nits. Subsurface, diffuse roughness, anisotropy on Standard Surface and OpenPBR, `transmission_scatter`, dispersion and `thin_walled` are listed. An image on opacity, specular, sheen or a coat tint with a partial coat is listed. A preview surface's specular workflow is listed. `surface_unlit` has no node material, and opens as an unlit glTF material. Looks, variants and `materialassign` of a `.mtlx` are not read. RealityKit's `no_flip_v` is not read, and RealityKit is proved on a stand-in only. Colour spaces other than sRGB and linear Rec.709 are read unconverted and listed, and USD's `ColorSpaceAPI` and a layer's `colorSpace` are not read. | `materialGraph` keeps more of a network than a PBR material does. | Read `asset.issues`, and `skipped` of `materialGraph`, for what did not land. |
| NURBS (`@margoengine/loaders/nurbs`, 2026-11-06) | By method: checks plain data and tessellates it on the one evaluator of `@margoengine/geometry/nurbs`, which `parseDXF` uses for SPLINE too. Whole curves and surfaces only. `parse3DM` feeds it surfaces saved without a render mesh, untrimmed ones only. Trim curves are not applied, and no solid topology or boolean operation exists. Surface grids use a fixed `segmentsU` and `segmentsV` per knot span, with no chord tolerance as curves have. | Trims and topology belong to a solid modelling kernel, which stays out of this repository. | A trimmed surface draws untrimmed. | Use the render mesh a file saves, or raise the segments. |
| Download size of the LAS and LAZ readers (`@margoengine/points`, 2026-11-06) | Measured with `npm run quality -- --gate=sizes`, min+gzip. A plain LAS file loads `points/las` at 5,761 B, and `loaders/las` adds its thin layer. The LAZ decoder is a lazy part of 8,523 B, loaded by the first compressed file; both together measure 13,735 B. `points/laz` is 8,613 B and `points/copc` 13,390 B, its decoder a lazy part too. The sizes gate holds the base and the lazy part apart, and `packages/loaders/tests/las.test.js` holds the boundary. | Budgets raised on 2026-11-06 for work users get: wave packets, `lasWaveform`, compressors 0 and 1, every extra bytes record, on `points/las` (about 800 B), `points/laz` and `points/copc`. The gate's `leak` row for `loaders/las` is accepted: one reader needs that static import, and the leak counts the joined size, though the decoder stays behind `import()`. | A page that reads LAS through `loaders/las` now carries `points/las` as well. | None needed: core and stage are untouched, and an app that imports neither entry pays nothing. |
| LAS and LAZ (`@margoengine/loaders/las`, `@margoengine/points/las`, 2026-11-06) | By method: one reader, in `points`; `loaders/las` is a thin layer over it. A `.laz` opens with no hook through the decoder of `points/laz`, which loads only when a compressed file arrives. Formats 0 to 10 equal LASzip and lazrs record for record and field by field on real files (`packages/points/tests/laz-real.test.js`). No public file of formats 4, 5 or 9 was found, so LASzip itself compressed those samples from real points, and it wrote the samples where the scanner channel switches. Stand-in proof only, on the test encoder: compressor 0, compressor 1 with version 2 items, and chunk size 0, because no tool here writes them. Refused with code `unsupported`: LAZ item version 1, coders other than arithmetic, compressors above 3, chunk table versions other than 0, waveform samples that are compressed or not 8, 16 or 32 bits. An extra bytes type above 30 is a `header` error. A LAS 1.4 compatibility mode file reads as stored, not remapped. Positions are 32-bit floats relative to `origin`. | Item version 1 is the coding of LASzip 1.0. The LAZ specification 1.4 R1 calls it outdated and leaves it out, the LASzip paper says it does not describe it, and the clean room rule keeps LASzip's source closed. The specification leaves compatibility mode out too. | Older LASzip files stay closed: three real samples of them were seen. `/las` in `loaders` takes whole files, so a file larger than memory does not open there. | Compress the file again with a current LASzip, or pass `decompress` with a decoder of your own. `loadLAS` of `points/las` streams from a URL; `step` thins a cloud; `maxVertices` refuses one past the limit. |
| OpenVDB volumes (`@margoengine/loaders/vdb`, 2026-11-06) | By method: file versions 222 and later; `float`, `double`, `int32`, `int64`, `vec3s`, `vec3d`, `vec3i` and half grids; uncompressed, zlib and Blosc with LZ4. Other grids and frustum transforms are left out. NanoVDB is not read. Proved on OpenVDB's sample sphere, smoke and fire files, version 222, half floats and two grids in one file, and on a file OpenVDB 13 wrote, version 225, with Blosc, float, double and vector grids: active counts, boxes and sampled values equal what OpenVDB reads (LIM-202). `bool` and mask grids and point data go to `skipped`. | No published byte-level specification, so a file version past 225 may need a new look; the real files stay out of the repository. | A left-out grid is named in `skipped`; a file with no grid offsets, another Blosc codec or an older version is refused with code `unsupported`. | Convert the grid to a supported type in the tool that wrote it. |
| MaterialX and Lottie (`@margoengine/loaders/materialx`, `/lottie`, 2026-11-06) | By method: MaterialX converts four surface shaders and the nodes node materials can hold; emission, opacity, procedural nodes, `uvtiling`, includes and custom nodedefs do not convert; shader defaults match the published nodedefs, with `standard_surface` 1.0.0 taken by `version` or `nodedef`, and `realitykit_pbr_surfaceshader` shares the `UsdPreviewSurface` defaults with no published definition checked. Lottie leaves out masks, mattes, text, effects, blend modes, 3D layers, repeaters, merge paths, rounded corners, star roundness, expressions and the clip of a precomposition to its size (LIM-205); group opacity multiplies down to each paint in place of compositing the group. Of 20 real files against lottie-web 5.12.2, 17 differ on under 1.5% of drawn pixels; adrock, bodymovin and navidad (up to 1.3%, 8.9%, 99.9%) use masks, merge paths, mattes or expressions. A layer's `st` and `sr` move only a precomposition's content: the spec text says all of its animated properties, but Bodymovin files and lottie-web read a layer's own keys in the time of its composition, and Margo follows the files. The MaterialX output has not been compared with a reference renderer. | Node materials are a closed set of nodes; a Lottie frame here is paths and images, and those features need a compositor. | MaterialX names each loss in `skipped`, and Lottie each feature in `unsupported`, except expressions. | Bake the feature in the authoring tool, or build the graph with `@margoengine/materials` by hand. |
| Range cache (`@margoengine/cache/ranges`, 2026-11-07) | Proved in a real browser (Chromium and Edge, 2026-11-07): gltf-pro's own streaming code (`GLTFStream`) reads the real SheenChair.glb through `RangeCache.fetch` with the browser's IndexedDB, and its buffers equal the file cold, offline after a reload and when revalidated; the Node tests cover the rest on stand-ins. Not yet run in a service worker, and not timed. By method: blocks are 64 KiB by default; eviction is per file and reads every file's record (one per file, not per block) before each write; two requests in flight at once that share a block both fetch it. | Blocks let a streamed file be stored as it is read, without the whole file. | A cold request is widened to whole blocks, at most one block more on each side. A request needing a block never fetched fails offline. | Use `AssetCache` to store whole files; a smaller `block` widens requests less. |
| Vary keys (`varyKeys` in `@margoengine/cache/vary`, 2026-11-07) | Proved on stand-ins: an in-memory IndexedDB and a stand-in server in Node; not yet run in a browser. By method: each URL and credential set remembers only the last `Vary` it saw; whether a request sends cookies is judged from its `credentials` mode and origin, as the cookies themselves cannot be read. | A page or worker cannot see cookies, and keeping one `Vary` per URL needs one record, not a list. | Without a `partition`, two users signed in with cookies on one browser profile share entries; after a server changes `Vary`, old entries wait for eviction. | Set `partition` to the signed-in user's id; `delete(url)` drops every entry of a URL. |
| Eviction index (`evictionIndex` in `@margoengine/cache/eviction`, 2026-11-07) | Proved on a real browser's IndexedDB (Chromium and Edge, 2026-11-07), counting the records each write reads: 2,000 files, then 30 writes read 120 records with the index and 60,030 without; not timed. By method: the database moves to IndexedDB version 2, and an AssetCache without the index can no longer open it. | An index on last use is how IndexedDB walks records in order without reading them all; the totals live in two records updated in the same transaction. | Opening such a database without the index sends every request to the network. | Open it with `evictionIndex()` every time, or use a new `name`. |
| Asset bundles (`AssetBundles` in `@margoengine/cache/bundles`, 2026-11-07) | Proved on stand-ins: an AssetCache over an in-memory IndexedDB and a stand-in server in Node; not yet run in a browser. By method: every file of a bundle is hashed once per session when loaded, so a load reads each file's bytes once more on the CPU; a file is stored under its URL plus `?v=<hash>`, so the server must serve that query. | Hashes make a file shared by bundles one stored copy and let a release change only what changed. | A first load each session spends CPU time hashing (SHA-256 in the browser's own crypto). | Leave a file out of the manifest to skip its check; it then caches by URL as plain AssetCache files do. |
| Freshness from Expires and Age (`freshness` of `AssetCache` in `@margoengine/cache`, 2026-11-07) | Proved on stand-ins: an in-memory IndexedDB and a stand-in server in Node; not yet run in a browser. By method: no heuristic freshness from `Last-Modified` (RFC 9111 section 4.2.2), so a file with no `max-age` or `Expires` is revalidated each session; `s-maxage` is ignored, as for any private cache. | A heuristic lifetime would serve files the server never said were fresh. `Age` and `Date` are not CORS-safelisted, so cross-origin they count only when exposed. | Cross-origin without exposed `Age` and `Date`, a file is taken as new when it arrives. | Expose `Age` and `Date` (`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`), or send `max-age`; `freshness: 'max-age'` keeps the old rule. |
| Prioritized prefetch (`Prefetcher`, `throttle` in `@margoengine/cache/prefetch`, 2026-11-07) | Proved on stand-ins: stand-in servers, a stand-in `requestIdleCallback` and an in-memory IndexedDB in Node; not yet run in a browser. By method: `throttle` paces reading of response bodies, not the socket, so it holds the rate only once the browser's receive buffers are full; a priority is fixed once a request starts. | A page cannot set the socket's rate; reading slower makes TCP flow control slow the sender. | The first buffers of a download arrive faster than the cap. | Lower `concurrency`, or `pause()` while the app streams something itself. |

### React page

What a minimal React page downloads, and why it is larger than core + stage (23,626 B gzip). The cube page of the
bundler matrix (`Canvas`, `Mesh`, `BoxGeometry`, `PbrMaterial`, `useFrame`, and `OrbitControls` from
`@margoengine/react/controls`), built by Vite 8.3.4, React 19.3 and `@margoengine/react` installed alone with
`@margoengine/controls` (2026-10-27), measured from the build's source maps (minified bytes per package; gzip is each
package's own gzip size, scaled so the parts add up to the chunk's). Core lights every scene by default
(a sun and an ambient term), so this is also the minimal lit page.

| Part | Minified B | Gzip B | Needed for a lit scene? |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `react-dom` (client) | 206,925 | 62,263 | Yes: the app's own UI runtime, not Margo's |
| `react` + `scheduler` | 11,635 | 4,430 | Yes: the app's own |
| `@margoengine/core` (engine, renderer, Wasm kernel, camera, shaders, geometry) | 49,485 | 19,056 | Yes |
| `@margoengine/react` (components, internal, assets, the shadows hook) | 14,241 | 5,488 | Yes: `Canvas`, `Mesh` and the hooks |
| `@margoengine/stage` | 2,771 | 1,405 | Not to draw (core draws and lights the cube); `Canvas` wraps its engine in the stage it hands to `onCreated` and `<Stage>` |
| Vite runtime (preload helper, module preload polyfill), app code and `@margoengine/math` (170 B) | 3,135 | 1,609 | The build's own |
| **Entry, requested before the page runs** | **288,234** | **94,251** | Budget 100,000 B gzip |
| `@margoengine/controls/orbit` + its view helpers + profiles, when `OrbitControls` mounts | 17,999 | 6,951 | Yes, for orbiting |
| **Everything the page fetches** | **306,233** | **101,202** | |

React and React DOM are 66% of the page; Margo's part (core, stage, bindings) is 25,949 B gzip (27,028 B before the
stage feature entries and the React subpaths of 2026-10-27, which took 3,089 B of minified loaders out of the stage). Nothing optional is in
the entry: `PbrMaterial` is core's material and pulls in no other package.

- **Fixed (2026-10-26):** `OrbitControls`, `MapControls` and `CadControls` loaded the `@margoengine/controls` index,
  so mounting them fetched TransformControls, DragControls, the gizmo and the `@margoengine/bvh` raycaster as one
  112,655 B (39,072 B gzip) chunk. They now load `@margoengine/controls/orbit` and `/profiles`, `ArcballControls`
  loads `/arcball` and `TransformControls` loads `/transform`: the mount costs 6,951 B gzip, and a Vite build emits
  107,027 B gzip in all instead of 135,875 B.
- **Emitted, never fetched:** the arcball chunk (3,192 B gzip). Nothing else: since 2026-10-27 neither `Canvas` nor
  the stage reaches an optional package, so there are no stand-in stubs either.
- **A textured model** (`<Gltf src="BoxTextured.glb">` from `@margoengine/react/gltf-pro`, with `@margoengine/gltf-pro`
  installed): the entry grows by
  1,750 B gzip (the `Model` binding), and mounting it fetches gltf-pro (175,257 B, 56,074 B gzip: glTF parsing, the
  PBR material and shader, compression hooks) plus the model. The Draco and Basis Wasm are emitted for files that use
  them; this one does not, and the page does not request them.
- **Through `margoengine/react`:** a page emits no Draco or Basis file. `Model`, `Fbx`, `Animation` and the `materials`
  components reach those decoders, so they are separate entries (`margoengine/react/gltf-pro`, `/fbx`, `/animation`,
  `/materials`); Vite copies a `new URL` file when it reads a module, before tree-shaking.
- **Through a kit or `materials`, `animation`, `fbx`:** no Draco or Basis file either. Only the root of `@margoengine/gltf-pro`
  names the decoders; packages that draw models use `@margoengine/gltf-pro/model`, and `loadGLTF` is imported from
  `margoengine/gltf-pro`, not from `margoengine/game`, `/web` or `/viz`.
- Still on the controls index: `PointerLock` (from `@margoengine/react/controls`), because `@margoengine/controls` has no
  pointer-lock entry; a page that does not use it never emits it.

## External video materials (2026-11-08)

`@margoengine/video/material` adds a caller-owned `HTMLVideoElement` or `VideoFrame` to a `Material.features` list. No JS-side frame upload occurs. The browser/driver may still copy or convert internally; platform zero-copy and throughput are unmeasured. A frame is imported per draw, one sampler per device; there are no mipmaps or repeat wrapping. UV0 (default) and UV1 are supported; `flipY` defaults to true, matching Texture's image orientation (first row at v=1); false puts it at v=0. Sources must be ready/open and origin-clean. WebGL2 returns `MargoError` code `unsupported` with a help link. Core's compact material has no feature slot. Custom WGSL must retain the standard surface/base-colour hook. The existing material's own maps, lighting and other features keep their own costs. External textures consume four sampled-texture slots, one implicit sampler and one uniform slot, in addition to the explicit filtering sampler; a fully mapped material can exceed a device's default limits. Alpha affects colour rendering; auxiliary caster/picking passes retain the material's existing alpha inputs. Generated colour frames and a canvas MediaStream on the RTX A6000/Edge prove imports across tasks, no material-path frame uploads and sRGB byte accuracy within 2 of 255 at 1x and 4x MSAA. Real movie files, other adapters, browser-internal copies, speed and custom shader combinations are unmeasured. Run `node scripts/gpu-lock.mjs run --owner=video-check -- node packages/video/test/material-check.mjs` after a package build.

## Page canvas recording (2026-11-08)

`@margoengine/video/record` records origin-clean page canvases with MediaRecorder, on WebGPU, WebGL2 or 2D. Container support and codecs are the browser's choice: default WebM, optional MP4, otherwise coded unsupported with a help link. The caller supplies optional live audio tracks, cloned so originals remain owned by the caller. No audio mixing is performed. All compressed chunks stay in memory until stop. Real-time capture can drop frames or shorten media on a busy/background page; no exact duration/frame count or A/V synchronization bound is claimed. Stop waits for the final chunk; abort, disposal and errors release owned tracks and discard the result. No Node/OffscreenCanvas API or alternate codec fallback is included. The real RTX A6000/Edge check decodes WebM (VP8/Opus) and MP4 (H.264/Opus) from both backends: generated colour and a tone are stand-ins. Other browsers, players, codecs, microphone permission paths and long recordings are unmeasured. Entry 1,140 B gzip; budget 1,396 B. No speed claim. See LIM-571.

## 360 and stereo video (2026-11-08)

`@margoengine/video/360` uses UV0 of `sphereGeometry`, an unlit back-sided material and a camera at its centre. Image centre -Z, right +X, top +Y. Mono (default) spans the whole source; stereo-left-right holds one complete panorama per half, selected explicitly for a screen material (eye left by default). Stereo sources need at least two pixels across, enforced before binding. Sampling clamps half a texel inside each eye; no mips or repeat wrapping. WebGL2 receives coded unsupported with a help link. One video feature per material; playback and source lifetime remain caller-owned. XR reuses native `XRLayers.video` with an equirectangular sphere, automatic compositor eye choice and radius 0 at infinity; it requires the draft WebXR Layers API and XRMediaBinding. Missing XR support propagates the existing XR error. Generated colour panoramas prove six screen directions and both eyes on RTX A6000/Edge; an integrated stand-in session/binding proves XR delegation and layer lifecycle. No headset, real movie, other browser/GPU or speed evidence. Entry 1,694 B gzip, budget 1,881 B. LIM-573.

## Own 2D certified geometry and solver evidence (2026-11-08)

The optional Rust kernel's exact rectangle SAT path requires a current canonical axis rectangle in pair-local
coordinates, finite vertex squared norms at most 2^120 and B-normal squared norms at most 4. The preferred-face
distance certificate additionally requires a coordinate-magnitude bound M in [1,2^20], positive finite A-face
separation and its finite radius/speculation/result checks. These restrict eligibility for an optimization;
all other inputs retain the full prior collision path. [Proof](architecture/physics2d-manifold-proof.md) and
[entry 101](whitepaper/perf-history.md) record the precise guards and unchanged native/Wasm hashes.

The two-point block solver remains production behavior. Earlier per-point alternatives did not meet all contracts
and establish a browser gain. All original accuracy bars remain unchanged. The subsequent sustained-support fix
establishes the required centered 1:1000 contact stack and deliberately re-pins the mixed scene; see the
[support contract](#own-2d-sustained-contact-support). Owned-2D CCD remains separate T-11 / 127.y work, distinct
from existing 1000:1 joint-chain and 3D CCD evidence.
After browser diagnostics: Edge 3.66/1.69/14.52 ms and Firefox 3.50/1.52/11.20 ms for Margo/Box2D/Rapier; separate
loaded sessions cannot prove before/after speed. The Box2D goal remains open and quiet reruns are required.

### Own 2D input validation

The raw Rust kernel under `packages/physics2d/kernel` exposes checked `Result<_, PhysicsError>` body/shape/chain
creation, world stepping and handle operations, bounds queries, scalar/coherent/four-pair and smooth-chain collision.
Use `?` to propagate a refusal, or read `PhysicsError::code()` and `help()`; the latter points to this section.
The Rapier-backed TypeScript facade is a separate API. Pure math and mass-property utilities are not all checked
entry points; in particular, `mass::mass_of` retains its direct `MassData` return.

| Code | Refusal |
| --- | --- |
| `PHYSICS2D_NOT_FINITE` | A required finite input or current-state value is NaN or infinite |
| `PHYSICS2D_INVALID_CORE` | An invalid active core count or radius would make collision data unsafe to index or use |
| `PHYSICS2D_DEGENERATE` | A requested shape/chain has insufficient usable geometry |
| `PHYSICS2D_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW` | Derived arithmetic cannot represent a required finite value, including positive mass/inertia lost to underflow or an overflowing reciprocal |
| `PHYSICS2D_INVALID_HANDLE` | A body, shape, contact or joint relationship refers outside the current valid objects |
| `PHYSICS2D_INVALID_PARAMETER` | A checked parameter violates its domain, such as negative density, friction or restitution |

Body definitions, collision poses and speculative cutoffs require finite used values. Shape material values are
finite and nonnegative. There is no geometry-independent minimum positive density: shape and aggregate mass,
inertia, moments, centres, extents and required reciprocals must remain representable in their actual arithmetic.
This includes the reciprocal of a positive inverse mass/inertia: a finite first reciprocal can round to a value
whose inverse overflows. Both the rounded centre and reconstructed origin are checked before publication.
Derived contact normal/tangent metrics and their required inverses must also be finite: two individually valid
inverse masses can overflow when added. Such a contact returns `PHYSICS2D_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW` before solving.
Computed contact-point relative velocity, normal approach and anchor base are also checked; finite body velocity
alone does not imply a representable `v + w cross r` at a very large contact arm.
Nonzero finite rotation pairs use scale-safe normalization when their squared length overflows or underflows;
ordinary normalization bits are retained. Exact zero pairs in a body definition retain the documented identity
conversion. A directly mutated zero stored body rotation is `PHYSICS2D_INVALID_PARAMETER`; a nonfinite or zero
rotation derived during integration returns the arithmetic error rather than becoming identity or zero silently.
An infinite density and a unit box at `f32::from_bits(1)` are refused before publishing a shape or NaN centre.
Genuine zero density or zero-area geometry keeps its zero-mass behavior; a positive mass/inertia rounded to zero
cannot take that fallback. A dynamic body with no mass retains the existing 1 kg, no-turn fallback.

Body/shape creation and an entire `create_chain` call are transactional for validation/arithmetic failures:
no partial body, shape list, mass state or chain proxies are published, and refused IDs remain available.
`World::step` preflights current public mutable data and relationships before simulation work: body state and
shape lists, current core counts/used vertices/normals/radii, materials/chain ghosts, contact handles/counts/used
values and joint handles/state. A stale broad-phase shape ID is checked before callback indexing. A linear
visitation check over shape references refuses duplicate, missing and foreign-body references.
Used cached normal impulses must be nonnegative. Friction retains the ordinary product/sqrt result when that
product is finite and uses a scale-safe geometric mean when it overflows. Invalid derived scalar or SIMD clamp
bounds propagate to the step's coded arithmetic-error check, rather than asserting inside Rust's clamp.
These checks use current data. Explicit documented sentinels, such as a joint motor's
positive-infinite maximum force for an unlimited motor, remain supported. A later simulation arithmetic error
is returned to the caller; the whole step is not a rollback transaction.

At `47f81f909`, the reproduced collision panic requires both a NaN pose and NaN cutoff: a missing closest-distance
candidate leaves a zero hit mask, whose trailing-zero index escapes the candidate rows. The literal NaN-pose
review call with a finite cutoff returned an empty manifold here. The fix rejects nonfinite public inputs and
checks missing face/distance candidates internally, including the four-pair path; it does not treat invalid
input as an ordinary empty contact. Native regressions cover both forms, overflow, current-array mutations,
creation rollback and valid retained-reference results. Each existing Wasm `check(i)` also exercises invalid
input refusal before comparing the same three production hashes. No hash was re-pinned and no speed result is
claimed. [Reproduce](whitepaper/reproduce.md#own-2d-input-validation) lists the commands and baseline failures.

Input validation closes the input/publication and absent-candidate error class. The separately derived support
correction below establishes the required centered contact bar. Owned-2D CCD is separate T-11 / 127.y work.

## Frame-accurate video decoding (2026-11-08)

Optional `@margoengine/video/codecs` fully buffers nonfragmented, self-contained, unencrypted AVC avc1 MP4. It reads the first video track, one sample description, stsz/stsc/stco/co64/stts/ctts/stss and a rate-1 media edit optionally after an empty edit, including 64-bit headers and edit versions 0/1. Movie times have microsecond precision; colliding timestamps are refused. Each request decodes from sync through the GOP, flushing reordering, selecting the requested timestamp and closing other frames. Requests are exclusive and abortable; caller closes returned frames. Edits expose only frames whose PTS starts inside the media edit; empty lead-in clamps to the first visible frame. No audio, streaming, playback scheduler, arbitrary edits, fragmented MP4, WebM, other codecs, rotation/display transforms or external media references. Large files/long GOPs can need substantial memory/CPU. WebCodecs/AVC support is browser-specific and checked; renderer independent, including beside WebGL2 (the external material remains WebGPU-only). Unit stand-ins prove reordered sample tables, edit boundary regression and ownership. Generated AVC MP4 is decoded/rendered on RTX A6000/Edge, not a third-party movie or a real B-frame test. Other browsers/GPUs and speed unmeasured. Entry 3,337 B gzip, budget 3,593 B. LIM-575.

## Video alpha and chroma key (2026-11-08)

Optional `@margoengine/video/alpha` reuses the external material feature. Stacked colour is top/left, raw grayscale red-channel alpha bottom/right; equal pixel halves require an even packed dimension of at least 2. Both rectangles clamp at half-texel bounds. Colour must be straight, not premultiplied. RGB keying uses sRGB Euclidean distance, threshold 0.15 and softness 0.1 by default, green key [0,1,0]. Threshold is 0..sqrt(3), softness positive through sqrt(3), with distinct float32 edges; feature parameters are fixed on creation. No despill, lighting adaptation or colour/hair recovery. One external video feature per material, UV0/flipY true defaults; caller owns playback and source lifetime. Alpha multiplies source/material alpha before colour-pass alphaTest/blending; enable transparent for soft edges. Existing direct sRGB output-space blending is preserved. Shadow/picking coverage retains its existing material inputs, without external alpha. WebGL2 refuses with code/help link. Real RTX A6000/Edge checks generated masks at 0/64/128/255 for both layouts, fractional keying and cutout; these are stand-ins, not real movies. Other GPUs/browsers, visual keying quality comparisons and speed unmeasured. Entry 1,898 B gzip, budget 2,154 B. LIM-578.

## Mixer automatic sidechain (batch 111)

Optional mixer/sidechain uses a two-input AudioWorklet, peak threshold, retriggerable hold and linked
one-pole linear gain attack/release. Defaults: amount 12 dB, threshold -40 dBFS, attack 0.01 s,
release 0.2 s, hold 0.05 s, stereo. Bounds: amount 0..96, threshold -120..0, times 0..10 s,
channels integer 1..32. Fixed options, no lookahead or speech recognition. HTTPS/localhost and
blob script CSP permission required, otherwise coded error/help link. Signal aborts waiting;
module registration is shared per context and can still complete. Real OfflineAudioContext
renders with generated signals pass beside WebGPU and WebGL2 on RTX A6000/Edge. Unit stand-ins
check envelopes, hold across quanta, key isolation, disposal, validation and cancellation.
Other browsers, real dialogue and speed/latency unmeasured. LIM-583.
Sidechain entry: 1,565 B gzip, budget 1,815 B; core plus stage remains 23,626 B.

## Mixer snapshots (batch 111)

Optional mixer/snapshots saves fader targets, mute/solo and current numeric effect AudioParams.
Null represents silent faders in JSON. Recall fades with existing mixer exponential smoothing,
about 98% settled in seconds (default 0.25, range 0..3600); at defaults to now and cannot be past.
Pure blends use linear amplitude and numeric parameter interpolation, endpoint-nearest booleans.
All payloads/layouts are validated before scheduling, including feedback safety and float32 fader range.
Effect order/kinds/parameter names must match; subset bus recalls are allowed. No routing, sends,
filter types, delay times, impulses, external worklets or active duck envelopes are captured.
Generated OfflineAudioContext and Node graph stand-ins prove the paths; real WebGPU/WebGL2 contexts
in Edge coexist with the audio. Other browsers, real recordings and timing remain unmeasured. LIM-586.
Snapshots entry: 1,810 B gzip, budget 1,956 B.

## Mixer exact dynamics (batch 111)

Optional mixer/worklet supplies a fixed soft-knee compressor and linked gain safety limiter.
The guaranteed ceiling covers original samples and the ITU four-phase 12-tap FIR estimate, not
all converters/codecs. The absolute-sum bound can over-attenuate; no sample clipping, listening
quality or minimum-attenuation claim. Algorithmic delay is 11 samples. Defaults and bounds are
in the package README; options are fixed, finite PCM is required, secure AudioWorklet/blob CSP
is required and signal cancels waiting. Zero attack/release jumps; otherwise times are one-pole
constants. The meter uses the published 48 kHz/4x model at all rates; no all-rate certification.
Node stand-ins prove curves, envelope, quantum independence and adversarial ceilings; real Edge
AudioWorklet renders pass beside WebGPU and WebGL2. Other browsers, real recordings, playback
latency, perceptual comparisons and speed are unmeasured. See the finite-FIR proof. LIM-589.
Worklet entry: 2,574 B gzip, budget 2,825 B.
Both worklet factories recheck AbortSignal after cached registration, before allocating nodes; microtask-order regressions cover this boundary.

## Mixer early reflections (batch 111)

Optional mixer/reflections computes six first-order rectangular-wall paths per ear, extra delay
relative to the dry path, and pressure gain reflection/path length (one-metre reference).
Dimensions 0.5..100 m; source/listener/ears at least 1 cm inside. Stereo ears are x-aligned;
no HRTF, diffraction, occlusion, higher orders or frequency-dependent wall model. Linear fractional
taps preserve gain/centroid but colour the spectrum. Early gains are not energy-normalized and
close walls may boost/clip. The existing seeded unit-energy noise tail starts at least 30 ms in,
10 ms after the last reflection; its requested duration is 0.05..20 s, RT60 0.01..20 s, gain 0..1.
Tail gain zero omits it. Total IR includes the computed onset plus tail. Integer rates 8..192 kHz,
mono/stereo; static geometry changes need recreation. Default/range table is in the package README.
Pure Node geometry and real Edge convolution beside WebGPU/WebGL2 use generated stand-ins;
real measured rooms, other browsers, listening quality and timing remain unmeasured. LIM-591.
Reflections entry: 3,227 B gzip, budget 3,483 B.

## 2D soft shadows (batch 55)

Optional `@margoengine/lighting2d/soft` adds 2D soft shadows with penumbra edges that soften with distance from the occluder. Penumbra width scales with emitter radius (`sourceRadius`) and distance from occluder edges; 0 gives hard shadows. Up to 64 radial samples per light on GPU and CPU. Occluders support polygon, polyline, segment, box and circle shapes. WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU form. LIM-592.

## Own 2D sustained contact support

The recovery cap applies to the combined elastic residual, `max(beta*s+C*lambda,-P)`, rather than penetration
bias alone. For positive recovery speed P, stationary positive support retains the original spring root
`beta*s+C*lambda=0`: the cap imposes no finite force ceiling on feasible, representable loads. This is not an
unbounded floating-point magnitude or arbitrary-geometry stability guarantee. P=0 retains disabled recovery
and may have nonunique overpenetrated stationary states; the coupled correction declines it.

The coupled equilibrium correction is limited to a certified dynamic contact path with one static terminal.
It retains physical masses and the original compliance. Branches, cycles, multiple anchors, joints,
kinematic/sleeping terminals, nonzero restitution, original separating motion, open rows, unsupported gravity
wrenches or uncertified ranks use general capped dynamics. Current geometry, unilateral/friction feasibility,
finite arithmetic, casts, rotations and residuals are certified before an atomic component commit. Recovery
displacement uses the existing P*h substep budget. Sleep observes actual substep pose rate as well as final
relaxed speed, with unchanged thresholds/time and island propagation.

All 29 native physical bars pass, including the unchanged 1:100 and required 1:1000 centered contact predicates,
100-row pile sag, slope creep, bounce, friction, joints and sleep. The 1:1000 result is 0.518550681 m sag versus
0.518550736 m closed form, zero final displacement from the one-second sample, asleep at 0.8833334 s. The
unchanged regression fails on verified main 47f81f909: 1.186208290 m sag and 92.105 mm creep. The mixed-scene pin changes
intentionally from `9acb68471fbe6ba0` to `92db6847c4e919d4`; pyramid/joint-grid pins are unchanged.
The same two stack predicates run inside Wasm `check(0)`; native scalar/SIMD and shipped Wasm SIMD agree on
the stack metrics. Wasm always selects SIMD even when its stored `contact_lanes` setting is false.

The preserved geometry-dependent point spring has a negative local bending direction in the sagged 1:1000
column under small lateral perturbations, independently differentiated and checked with Margo's own manifolds.
Centered axial acceptance does not establish arbitrary lateral stability. No artificial angular stiffness or
mass threshold was fitted to hide it. See the [support derivation](architecture/physics2d-support-proof.md),
[bending analysis and executable probe](architecture/physics2d-support-bending.md), and
[reproduction commands](whitepaper/reproduce.md#own-2d-sustained-support).

The normal solver's finite-predicate reduction relies on actual private caller dataflow, not arbitrary
independent helper arguments. It preserves the same accepted/exceptional lane masks, raw-row and used-soft
domain checks, cap membership, post-motion finiteness and coded errors. The
[IEEE proof and compiler inventory](architecture/physics2d-finite-certificates.md) state those restrictions;
static predicate counts do not establish dynamic instruction counts or a browser speed gain.

The frame planner is linear in bodies, contacts and joints. Its flat graph/path/gap buffers retain capacity
bounded by historical maximum body/contact counts, rather than per-body or per-component capacity maxima.
Current graph contents, eligibility and weights rebuild each frame. General branching graphs skip the extra
substep path work; accepted components still use local allocations and two banded factorizations each substep.
Retaining scratch memory trades its lifetime for fewer allocation calls; this is not an allocation-free world
or a Box2D speed-parity claim. CCD remains outside this correction.

## 2D light textures and cookies (batch 55)

Optional `@margoengine/lighting2d/cookie` projects 2D textures (cookies / gobos) onto light footprints to shape lights with window patterns and flashlights. Supports scale, offset, rotation, wrapping modes (zero, clamp, repeat, mirror) and blend modes (multiply, alpha, color). WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU reference form. LIM-593.

## Mixer loudness (batch 111)

Optional mixer/loudness analyses finite Float32 PCM, mono/stereo or Web Audio 5.1 order
L/R/C/LFE/SL/SR. LFE is excluded from loudness, surrounds weight 1.41, others 1. All channels
contribute to true peak. Rates: 32/44.1/48/88.2/96/176.4/192 kHz. Published 48 kHz K filters
are bilinearly re-expressed at the other rates; tests preserve 1 kHz calibration within 0.1 LU.
Ungated M/S windows are 400 ms/3 s every 10 ms. Integrated blocks overlap 75%, with absolute
-70 LUFS and relative -10 LU gates. Incomplete windows are discarded; unavailable/silent
measurements are -Infinity. Histories/energy memory scales with duration. No file decoding,
live worklet metering, LRA, UI or full EBU certification. Generated standard tone/offset tests
are stand-ins, not the downloaded corpus or authentic programmes. Real Edge rendered calibration
and normalisation pass beside WebGPU/WebGL2; other browsers and speed are unmeasured.
Normalisation copies PCM, defaults -23 LUFS/-1 dBTP, target above -70 through 0 and peak cap
-96..0 dBTP. It applies constant gain, not compression/clipping; cap and changed absolute-gate
membership may prevent the requested loudness, and the after report exposes the actual outcome.
A programme with no qualifying complete 400 ms block raises coded state with help link.
Peak cap/measurement uses the finite four-phase FIR model, not all DACs/codecs. LIM-596.
Loudness entry: 2,107 B gzip, budget 2,363 B.
Mixer root entry: 6,901 B gzip, budget 6,926 B. Core plus stage remains 23,626 B gzip.
Mixer gain/mute/solo/duck scheduling now validates finite nonnegative audio times/durations and float32 parameter/gain bounds before mutating state or cancelling automation. Invalid releases remain active; undefined release/gate durations retain 0.4/0.01 second defaults. Regression: mixer.test.js.

`@margoengine/lighting2d` supports geometric light masks (`rect`, `circle`, `polygon`) and bitmask layer filtering (`layers` on `Light2DOptions` and `layer` on `Lighting2DOptions`). Lights masked or on mismatched layers are clipped in the shader or culled before draw submission. WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU reference form. LIM-597.

Optional `@margoengine/lighting2d/gi` adds 2D global illumination with radiance cascades for multi-bounce bounce lighting and emissive sprites. Angular directions and ray intervals partition ray marching across cascades; emissive sprites and occluder edges cast light and block rays. WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU reference form. LIM-599.

## 2.5D height and self-shadowing (batch 55)

Optional `@margoengine/lighting2d/height` casts perspective and directional height shadows and self-shadows based on vertical extrusion and elevation for top-down games. Ray-interval clipping along height spans projects shadows on the ground and lower receivers, and self-shadows vertical walls and lower surfaces. WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU reference form. LIM-600.

## Emissive sprites and bloom hookup (batch 55)

Optional `@margoengine/lighting2d/emissive` adds glowing emissive sprites with distance falloff and bloom threshold extraction. Emissive sprites emit light directly into the 2D lighting target and isolate HDR light above a luminance threshold for external bloom passes (`connectBloom`) or integrated 2D bloom glow. WebGPU only; WebGL2 rejects with coded error and help link. Real RTX A6000 check passes with 0 wrong pixels against the CPU reference form. LIM-601.

## Audio occlusion and obstruction (batch 110)

Optional audio/occlusion uses five CPU rays (one with radius 0) and interpolated gain/low-pass filtering. Radius 0..100 m, default 0.25; blocked gain 0..1, default 0.25; cutoff 10..min(20000, Nyquist) Hz, default 800; smoothing 0..60 s, default listener.smoothing. Source and followed-listener meshes are excluded; each segment excludes 1 mm at both ends. Sampled aperture, not diffraction, physical transmission, material/thickness modelling, reflections or reverb-send control. Visible CPU triangles only; alpha masks and arbitrary shader deformation are absent, active skin/morph needs a static proxy. World matrices and sound/listener poses must be current before explicit update. Keep the sound filter chain in place while active.

Real Edge 157 audio renders beside native WebGPU, WebGL2 and WebGL2 compatibility engines show 2 kHz blocked/clear RMS 0.039128 and 100 Hz 0.249970 with zero GPU validation errors. Node tests use renderer/audio stand-ins with real engine transforms and BVH. Generated tones/boxes are stand-ins; real recordings, listening quality, other browsers and performance are unmeasured. LIM-602.

Occlusion entry: 1,516 B gzip, budget 1,772 B, with package dependencies external; audio root: 7,508 B gzip. BVH is a separate optional dependency loaded only on create, never through the audio root. Core plus stage remains 23,626 B gzip.


## Batch 168 lightmap reference

The modified Cornell box has 15 parallelograms and 881 irradiance texels. Against a complete Cycles
128-sample diffuse bake, physical lightmap MAE is 0.069543 (relative 0.211475), max 0.900128.
One coarse radiosity bounce, 16 bounce rays and 16 area samples are not a converged path tracer.
The 8-bit step is 0.016984. This error remains open in known-bugs.md; no speed claim is made.


## Batch 168 transparency reference

Default weighted OIT loses to exact ordered transparency on five overlapping unlit coverage panes:
full-image MAE 1.567519, PSNR 35.388 dB, max byte miss 15. The external depth-peeling picture has
MAE 0.048151. Binary source colours and a common output encoding isolate order approximation; general
nonbinary display-domain material blending remains a separate open defect. Busy-machine frame-plus-completion
2.000 versus 7.100 ms includes different completion mechanisms; no isolated pass-speed claim.


## Batch 168 sky radiance reference

The default Preetham sky does not match Mitsuba 3.9.1 sunsky (Hosek-Wilkie): zenith-Y-normalized RGB
MAE 1.439228 over 297 directions, max 70.681783. Per-case relative MAE is 0.199319 to 0.765628.
Sun elevations 10/45/75 degrees, turbidity 2/3/6, ground albedo 0, sun disk off. This is a sampled
angular-radiance comparison, not hemisphere integration or absolute physical-unit equivalence. Loss open.

## Audio Doppler (batch 110)

Optional audio/doppler: 1,090 B gzip; audio root unchanged at 7,508 B; core plus stage unchanged at 23,626 B. Defaults: speedOfSound 343 m/s, authored playbackRate from the sound. Positive finite sound speed; nonnegative finite rate at most float32 maximum / 39. Estimates world displacement per audio-clock second; first/coincident/backward-clock samples neutral, repeated times retain history. Reset after teleports and motion during suspension. Owns playback rate while attached, restores authored rate on disposal; use controller.playbackRate for authored changes, never Audio.sync concurrently. Buffers only; other source kinds produce unsupported with a help link. One controller per sound. Listener pose/source ordering and teardown are covered.

Classical instantaneous stationary-medium approximation with each radial Mach number clamped to -0.95..0.95, ratio bounded 1/39..39. Not supersonic acoustics, wind, retarded-time positions, travel delay or pitch-only time stretching. Existing listener smoothing applies. Real browser renders beside all three rendering routes measure generated 1 kHz source approach at 1111.111 Hz, recession at 909.091 Hz, listener approach at 1100 Hz and co-motion/rest at 1000 Hz, each within 0.1 Hz with zero GPU validation errors. LIM-603, generated stand-ins only; real recordings, listening quality, other browsers and performance unmeasured.


## Batch 168 contact-shadow reference

Default ContactShadows is a height-faded, fixed-Gaussian local contact cue. Under a radius-0.5 sphere,
against ray-traced uniform-hemisphere visibility, exposed-ground darkness MAE is 0.048174, RMSE 0.074416,
max 0.288788 (15,472 pixels, 4096 rays each; reference quadrature max error 0.001734). Defaults are retained:
size 4, height 1, blur 0.06, opacity 0.6, resolution 256. No physical lighting or area-light parity is claimed;
the measured picture loss remains open.


The independent Cycles OIT reference (1024 samples, seed 168) gives Margo MAE 1.632960 versus Babylon
0.132651. Exact primary-ray transport versus Cycles MAE is 0.094812; maximum miss 125 includes path
noise and silhouette sampling. This validates the ordering loss without treating stochastic reference
pixels as a zero-error raster oracle. No additional speed claim. See whitepaper/results/batch-168-oit-cycles.json.

## Audio event containers (batch 110)

Optional audio/events: 5,491 B gzip with shared audio implementation; audio root 7,638 B and core plus stage 23,626 B. Defaults: mode random (no immediate repeated index), voices 4 per buffer, volume [1,1], pitch [0,0] cents, cooldown 0 seconds, random Math.random, voiceOptions pool defaults, play position origin. Nonempty decoded positive-duration buffers; finite ordered volume endpoints 0..1 and pitch -4800..4800 cents. Cooldown finite and nonnegative; draws finite in [0,1). Pitch changes duration. No loading or timers. Total preallocated voices = buffer count times voices; stealing is per buffer, not a global cap. Snapshot ranges, not caller arrays. Looping disabled; sequence wraps, cooldown rejection consumes no state/draw. Clock suspension freezes cooldown; backwards clock accepts a new interval.

LIM-605: Node stand-in tests and real browser PCM beside all three rendering routes prove order, random selection, volume/pitch and silent cooldown gaps. Generated 1/1.5 kHz tones at +1200 cents give 2/3 kHz within 0.1 Hz, RMS 0.0625 within 1e-6 for 0.5 volume, and zero GPU validation errors. Shared buffers stay usable on disposal. Recordings, listening quality, other browsers, random-generator statistical quality and performance remain unmeasured.

Audio root size growth in this job includes pool input validation before voice stealing, fixing an existing failure that cut valid playback when new arguments were invalid. The new entry remains optional; the root gains only the owning pool bug fix. Current audio/doppler size with rebuilt shared chunks: 1,090 B gzip.

## Own 2D frame-preparation certificates

Private checked-bounds reuse is valid only within the same current-step preflight and with no intervening
core/pose mutation. Public bounds and creation remain checked; derived overflow is still refused. Lazy
planner eligibility skips only pure conjuncts after rejection, retaining complete component traversal.
The [proof](architecture/physics2d-frame-preparation.md) and perf history entry 112 distinguish dynamic
predicate calls from static compiler counts and loaded-machine timing. The profile's adjacent setup,
clock, optimizer/layout and post-timer cache effects prevent an exact production-phase deconvolution.
All original accuracy predicates and three corrected pins remain; no Box2D parity or owned-2D CCD claim.
